# Elecmetric roadmap history (archive)

This is the version-by-version log as it stood at v1.5.13 plus the F3 and F5 to F8 rounds, kept unchanged apart from this note. "Still open" lines below were true on the day they were written and are **not** the current status: for that, read `ROADMAP.md`. Where a line says "v1.5.14, not in this tree", that work (F3) is now merged and ships in 1.5.14.

---

Source: *Deep Web Research: Building a World-Class Electrical Calculator Platform*. Status below is from reading the repo on 2026-11-07. Items marked (verify) were found by keyword only and need a hands-on check.

## Already in place (v1.4.x)
- One engine, no DOM, shared by browser and Node; calculators defined as data with formula, assumptions, notes, next-steps and embedded tests
- 113 calculators across 33 of the 36 divisions; unit engine with per-field unit switching
- Calculation basis layer (General/IEC-style, NEC-style, user-defined) with no copyrighted tables embedded
- Feeder chain (load to current, cable, voltage drop, breaker, fault), calculation sheet, CSV, print, PWA/offline, verification page, JS API
- Tests: self-tests, independent textbook vectors (now all 113 of 113 calculators, 1,601 vectors), property tests, registry integrity, CI that regenerates vectors

## Gaps against the research doc, in priority order
1. **Verification coverage**: every calculator now has independent vectors (Issue 6). Remaining gaps are partial coverage of generator-kva (standard sizes, rated current) and rack-cooling (airflow), and the standard-size lists, which are conventions to confirm against your own standard or manufacturer data.
2. **Standards bases**: add IS (India), BS, AS/NZS as selectable bases. Method constants only, with licensed tables loaded as user-supplied data. Largest differentiator in the doc (section 7, 20).
3. **Indexable pages** (first cut shipped in v1.5.7: `node tools/build_pages.js` writes 46 intent pages plus a hub into `pages/`; `BASE_URL=https://your.site/elecmetric` adds canonical links and sitemap.xml. Still open: one page per calculator beyond those 46, FAQ blocks, per-page structured data): the app is one `index.html`. The doc's SEO plan (section 8, 16, 17) needs one crawlable URL per calculator with formula, worked example, assumptions, FAQ and related links, generated from the registry at build time so the engine stays the single source.
4. **Breadth to Phase 3 (250)**: still thin divisions are 01 Fundamentals, 22 Control, 33 Building, 35 AGL (1 calculator), 36 Converters (2), 20 Harmonics, 26 Renewable, 30 Safety. Add in layers, each with vectors in the same commit.
5. **Real HTTP API and embeds** (section 24): today `Engine.api` is a JS function. A thin serverless wrapper plus an embeddable widget.
6. **Excel/PDF export** (section 25): CSV and print exist (verify true .xlsx and PDF).
7. **Natural-language "What should I calculate?"** (section 12): a `route()` exists (verify depth). Keep the rule that AI picks the calculator and the engine computes.
8. **Multilingual, reviewer/version trust panel, one-click error report**: not yet.

## v1.5.8 changes (audit follow-up)
- Electricity bill calculator with three slabs, fixed charge and tax (`electricity-bill-slabs`, with independent vectors); the Electricity Bill landing page now uses it. Closes audit #6.
- Typical power-factor values shown beside every PF input, labelled indicative only. Closes F9 (PF table).
- Still open from the audit: main AC `voltage-drop` still takes R and X per km (the material and AWG route is in `dc-voltage-drop`, resistive only); wire gauge has no SWG; no standalone AWG chart page.

## v1.5.9 changes
- `ac-voltage-drop`: single- and three-phase voltage drop with resistance computed from material, size (area, AWG or diameter) and temperature, plus power factor and data-sheet reactance. Independent vectors included. The main `voltage-drop` calculator (R and X typed in) is unchanged.
- Still open: SWG, a standalone AWG chart page, skin and proximity effect for large conductors.

## v1.5.10 changes
- `swg-wire`: SWG 7/0 to 50 to diameter (mm and inches) and area, using the standard table. "swg" searches route to it; "wire gauge" still leads with AWG. Vectors cover all 57 table entries.
- The AWG chart already exists as an in-app view and on the wire-gauge landing page, so that item is closed.
- Still open: skin and proximity effect for large conductors, SWG area-to-gauge lookup.

## v1.5.10 fix
- `sw.js` had been saved as a 0-byte file, so the offline claim was untrue. Restored as a versioned app-shell cache (index, engine, manifest, icon, all `pages/`), with tests that fail if it is empty, stale or missing a page. Bump `CACHE_VERSION` in `sw.js` together with `VERSION` in `engine.js`.

## v1.5.10 second audit (SEO and structure)
- `index.html`: title, description, manifest link, theme colour and font stylesheet were after `</head>` inside the body; moved into the head, `lang="en"` added. Header engine version now comes from `E.VERSION` (it said v1.3).
- `tools/build_pages.js`: `robots.txt` always written to the site root; `sitemap.xml` is written to the root (not `pages/`) when `BASE_URL` is set, and now lists `index.html` too. Canonical links on the pages also need `BASE_URL`, so build with it before deploying.
- `ac-voltage-drop`: lagging or leading power factor (Vd = k I (R cos φ ± X sin φ)). Leading can give a negative drop (voltage rise); a warning says so. Independent vectors include both.
- Verification page no longer says every calculator is independently checked; it separates author-written built-in tests from the independent vectors.
- Still open: interactive form on each of the 46 pages, full AC Ohm's law / phasor solver, conductor material and kcmil/resistance outputs on the wire gauge page, independent vectors for the 10 listed calculators (AGL CCR first), AGL division expansion, canonical link on `index.html`.

## v1.5.11 changes (third audit, F1)
- F1 closed: each of the 46 landing pages now has a "Try your own numbers" form (inputs, unit selectors, calculation-type selectors) that recalculates as you type with the same engine.js the app uses (`../engine.js`). The worked example stays on the page as the no-JavaScript fallback; the form is hidden until JavaScript runs.
- Form code lives once in `tools/build_pages.js` (`llCompute` pure, `llInit` DOM wiring) and is copied into every page. Engineering-mode inputs (line-to-neutral, etc.) keep their defaults; the app link covers them. The voltage-drop page offers area only, no AWG or custom resistivity.
- Tests: 393 landing-page checks, including the form left at defaults reproducing the worked example on all 46 pages, textbook changes (Ohm's law, √3 power, divider, AWG diameter, power factor angle), unit switching, solved-for blank fields and invalid entries.
- Still open from the audit: F2 AC Ohm's law, F3 energy cost, F4 head/sitemap/structured data/FAQ and the deploy-build test, F5 search routing, F6 unit prefixes, F7 copper resistivity hint, F8 bill labelling.

## v1.5.12 changes (third audit, F4)
- Every landing page has a visible FAQ (3 to 6 questions, 207 in all) written for readers from the page's own formula, notes and assumptions; no figure in an answer is typed by hand, and the worked-example figures come from the engine. No FAQPage markup, because Google shows FAQ rich results for few sites.
- Head: twitter card tags on every page; with `BASE_URL` set, also `canonical`, `og:url`, and JSON-LD (BreadcrumbList plus WebApplication on each page, BreadcrumbList on the hub). `index.html` gets og and twitter tags, a no-JavaScript fallback that links to the conversion pages, and a canonical, og:url and WebApplication JSON-LD between `<!--seo:start-->` and `<!--seo:end-->` when built with `BASE_URL`.
- Deploy build: `BASE_URL=https://your.site/elecmetric node tools/build_pages.js` writes `sitemap.xml` and the `Sitemap:` line in `robots.txt`, and fills the index.html markers. Run it in the deploy step, not in the committed tree. `build({ base })` now takes the address as an argument.
- The test "committed pages equal what the generator produces" builds with `base: ''` and ignores only the deploy-time canonical, og:url and JSON-LD on disk, so it passes with or without `BASE_URL`, before or after a deploy build, and still fails on any other change. CI has a deploy-build smoke step.
- Fixed: worked-example text said "tariff per kwh"; capitalisation of units is now kept.
- Still open from the audit: F2 AC Ohm's law, F3 energy cost, F5 search routing, F6 unit prefixes, F7 copper resistivity hint, F8 bill labelling. Sitemap and canonical need the real site address, which is not in the repo.

## v1.5.13 changes (third audit, F2)
- F2 closed: new calculator `ac-ohms-law` (AC Ohm's Law (Phasors), division 02). Enter any two of voltage, current and impedance, each with a phase angle in degrees; the third phasor and the power triangle come back. V = I·Z (magnitudes multiply, angles add), S = V·I* (angle ∠V − ∠I), P = |S| cos θ, Q = |S| sin θ (positive Q is inductive), pf = cos θ. Angles wrap to (−180°, 180°]. A blank angle on an entered value counts as 0°; an angle on the value being solved is rejected; three values or one value are rejected; an impedance angle beyond ±90° warns (negative resistance).
- Search: "ac ohms law", "ohms law ac", "phasor calculator", "ohms law with phase angle" and "complex impedance ohms law" now land on it first; "ohms law calculator" still goes to the any-two solver. Six new router cases (49 in all).
- Links: the DC Ohm's law and any-two solver list it under Next; the Ohm's law and watts-volts-amps-ohms landing pages carry a chip to it and their FAQ answer on AC now points to it. The 46-page set is unchanged.
- Tests: 12 self-tests, 36 new independent vectors (`gen_vectors.py`, Python `cmath`, written without reading engine.js; the earlier 1,158 vectors are unchanged), 6 router cases.
- Still open from the audit: F3 energy cost, F5 remaining phrasings (power-unit converter, "amps to kw 3 phase", mm² voltage drop), F6 unit prefixes, F7 copper resistivity hint, F8 bill labelling.

## F5 changes (search routing; built on v1.5.13, version not bumped)
- F5 closed: new calculator `power-converter` (Power Unit Converter, division 36). One power in W, kW, MW or HP, read back in W, kW, MW and HP. No landing page: the 46-page set is unchanged.
- Router (index.html, ROUTER-CORE): "1 phase", "3 phase", "single/three phase", "400v", "50hz", "0.8 pf" and bare numbers are ignored when reading "A to B", so "amps to kw 3 phase" goes to Amps to kW and "100 kw to amps 3 phase 400v" to kW to Amps. New unit names: MW, HP, PS, horsepower, megawatt. "kw to watts", "watts to kw", "kw to w", "mw to kw" go to the power converter; "hp to kw", "kw to hp", "ps to kw" go to HP to kW first.
- Voltage drop with a conductor size word (mm2, mm², cross section, AWG, kcmil, copper, aluminium) now goes to the size-based calculators (DC first, AC first when the query mentions AC, power factor or reactance). Plain "voltage drop calculator" still goes to the R and X version.
- Tests: router cases 49 to 73; 4 self-tests; 16 new independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (the earlier 1,194 are unchanged).
- Still open from the audit: F3 energy cost (done separately in v1.5.14, not in this tree), F6 unit prefixes, F7 copper resistivity hint, F8 bill labelling. Remaining search gap: no mW or µW converter until F6 adds those units.

## F6 changes (unit prefixes; built on the F5 tree, version not bumped)
- F6 closed: power gains µW and mW, voltage gains µV and MV, current gains µA and MA, matching the µ, m, k, M prefixes RapidTables accepts on Ohm's law. Every calculator and landing page that offers these dimensions shows them. Pages regenerated with `node tools/build_pages.js`.
- Not changed: apparent power (VA) and reactive power (VAR) stay at VA, kVA, MVA and VAR, kVAR, MVAR. The router still reads "mw" as megawatt, since the search box ignores case.
- Tests: 28 new independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (prefix to SI on Ohm's law, the any-two solver and the power converter; the earlier 1,210 are unchanged).
- Still open from the audit: F3 energy cost (v1.5.14, not in this tree), F7 copper resistivity hint, F8 bill labelling.

## F7 changes (copper resistivity; built on the F6 tree, version not bumped)
- F7 closed: the Custom resistivity field in `dc-voltage-drop` and `ac-voltage-drop` now starts at the Copper preset, 1.7241e-8 Ω·m, instead of 1.68e-8. Its hint quotes the preset values (copper 1.7241e-8, aluminium 2.8264e-8) and says the pure-metal handbook values, about 1.68e-8 and 2.65e-8, are lower. The notes give the size of the gap: on the audit example (50 A, 50 m, 10 mm², two-wire) the drop is 8.6205 V with the preset and 8.4 V with 1.68e-8, 2.6% lower; aluminium would be about 6.7% lower.
- New output "Resistivity used (at conductor temperature)" in both calculators, and the result sentence ends with the value at 20 °C or the stated temperature (five significant digits). The result row itself shows four, because the shared number formatter prints small numbers that way.
- Tests: 3 new self-tests (resistivity at 20 °C and 70 °C, and an untouched Custom field matching the Copper preset). Pages regenerated.
- Still open from the audit: F3 energy cost (v1.5.14, not in this tree), F8 bill labelling.

## F8 changes ("electricity bill" labelled apart; built on the F7 tree, version not bumped)
- F8 closed: the two meanings are now named differently everywhere. `energy-cost` is "Appliance Running Cost (Energy and Cost)", the cost of one load at one flat rate. `electricity-bill-slabs` is "Electricity Bill from Tariff Slabs (fixed charge, tax)", the whole bill from the units on the meter. Purpose, keywords and notes of both point at each other, and `energy-cost` now lists the slab bill under Next.
- Landing pages: titles are "Energy Cost (one appliance)" and "Electricity Bill (tariff slabs)"; descriptions say which one is for what; the Electricity Bill page has a link to Energy Cost, the Energy Cost and Energy Consumption pages link to Electricity Bill, and each has an FAQ entry ("Is this my whole electricity bill?" / "Is this the cost of one appliance?").
- Search: queries about an appliance (appliance, running cost, "bill for a heater", "cost of running a fan", "monthly electricity bill for ac") go to the appliance calculator first; "electricity bill", "slab" and "fixed charge" go to the slab bill first. 9 new router cases (82 in all).
- Merge note: the `energy-cost` title, purpose, keywords, notes and Next line were edited here. F3 (v1.5.14) rewrote that definition, so expect to merge those five lines by hand; the F3 per day, month and year outputs are the appliance mode this labelling refers to.
- Audit status: F1 to F8 are all closed (F1, F4 in earlier rounds, F3 in v1.5.14, F2, F5 to F8 here).

## F3 changes (energy cost; built on the F8 tree, version not bumped)
- F3 closed: `energy-cost` (Appliance Running Cost, definition 1.1.0) now takes load, a **run-at %** (capacity, 1 to 100), **hours of use per day, week or month** (limits 24, 168, 744 checked together with the period), the days to total, tariff and a **currency symbol** (none, ₹, $, €, £; default none so landing pages stay neutral). Results: cost and energy per **day, month and year** (month = 365/12 days, year = 365 days) plus the total for the days entered (the old `E` and `cost` keys, unchanged at the defaults: 480 kWh and 3,840).
- Power gains **BTU/h** (1055.05585 J / 3600 s) and **TR** (tons of refrigeration, 12,000 BTU/h = 3,516.85 W), so every calculator and page that takes power offers them. The notes say these are cooling ratings and the electrical input power is what to use for running cost.
- **Appliance picker**: 20 typical running wattages (`Engine.APPLIANCES`, labelled indicative) fill the load in its current unit and can be edited afterwards; typing in the load clears the picker. Generic: any input with `presets` gets one. Works in the app and on the Energy Cost and Energy Consumption landing pages.
- Engine: `show()` prints an output's optional `prefix`; `compute()` may return `outMeta` to set it per output. Test cases may assert `prefix`.
- Landing page FAQ for Energy Cost has two new answers (per day/month/year and run-at; BTU/h, tons and typical wattages), 7 questions in all.
- Tests: 11 self-tests (up from 2), 16 independent vectors (`gen_vectors.py`, its own random generator so the earlier 1,238 are unchanged; they caught a rounded BTU/h constant, now exact), 6 landing-form checks. Browser check in Chromium at 375 px: picker, unit switch to TR, week/month hours, currency prefix, error at 200 h a week; no overflow.
- Not done: a minutes-per-day or days-per-week input (enter hours per week instead), a months-per-year input, saving tips.
- Audit status: F1 to F8 are all closed in this tree.

## Rule for every new calculator
Definition + formula + assumptions + self-tests + independent vector from a textbook formula written without reading engine.js, merged together.

## v1.5.14 (re-audit of v1.5.13-F3, Issues 1 to 8)
- Feeder Designer: breaker breaking-capacity input and check, status that separates checks performed from checks pending; test suite no longer re-run on each input (Issues 1, 2).
- Production build: canonical, og:url, JSON-LD, sitemap, robots, deploy guard and a Pages workflow (Issue 3).
- 13 calculator landing pages plus an engineering hub in `calculators/` (Issue 4).
- Feeder Designer standards: NEC continuous-load choice, BS 7671 lighting limit, AS/NZS 3008 ampacity basis kept apart from the AS/NZS 3000 voltage-drop reference (Issue 5).
- 347 new independent vectors for the last ten calculators; every calculator now has vectors; size-table overflow tested (Issue 6).
- Documentation and version: roadmap split into a current-status file and this archive; engine and `sw.js` bumped to 1.5.14; `DEPLOY.md` upload list now includes `calculators/`; a test keeps the status section in step with the code (Issue 7).
- This release also contains F3 and F5 to F8, which earlier notes call "v1.5.14, not in this tree" or "version not bumped".
- 12 category hubs at `calculators/<category>/` (Issue 8): the audit's ten categories plus Electronics and Instrumentation and Lighting and Facilities so that no calculator is left out. Prose in `tools/category_hubs.js`; calculator lists, purposes and verification counts come from the registry and the vector file. Linked from the engineering hub and from each calculator page, listed in the sitemap and the service-worker cache.

## v1.5.15 (search and sharing pass)
- Structured data: `FAQPage` JSON-LD on every conversion and calculator page, built from the same question list as the visible FAQ; `WebApplication` now declares it is free (`offers` 0, `isAccessibleForFree`) and belongs to a `WebSite` node added on the home page.
- Sharing: `og:site_name` everywhere, `summary_large_image` cards, and a 1200x630 `og-image.png` (drawn by `tools/make_og_image.py`) referenced by `og:image` and `twitter:image` in the production build.
- Crawling: the home page is drawn by JavaScript, so it now carries plain links (all engineering calculators, all conversions, the 12 category hubs, 8 popular conversions) between `<!--browse:start-->` markers, generated by `build_pages.js`.
- Sitemap: `<lastmod>` only when `SITE_LASTMOD` is given (the Pages workflow passes the last commit date); a `noindex` `404.html` is built and published.
- Guard (`tools/check_deploy.js`): also requires `og:image`/`twitter:image`, FAQPage matching the visible FAQ, valid `lastmod`, `404.html`, and `og-image.png` on disk.
- Version: engine and `sw.js` bumped to 1.5.15 so offline caches pick up the new home page.
- Not done, needs the owner: a real production domain (`BASE_URL`), Search Console and Bing verification, author and reviewer information on pages, real-browser checks.

## v1.5.16 (audit follow-up: install, icons, contrast, dialog)
- Home page now links icon.svg and apple-touch-icon.png. Every landing and calculator page links the manifest and registers sw.js, so offline and install work from any entry page.
- Manifest gained PNG icons (192, 512, and a separate 512 maskable with a safe zone); all four icon files are in the service-worker shell.
- Light-theme gold darkened from #9A6B00 to #8A5F00 (at least 4.9:1 on every light surface).
- Calculation sheet: second h1 replaced by an h2, aria-modal, focus moved in on open, Tab trapped, focus restored on close.

## v1.5.16 (naming and trust follow-up)
- Product renamed Elecmetric, site address https://elecmetric.com in the deploy examples; storage keys are now `elecmetric-*` and the service worker removes old `loadline-*` caches.
- The four calculator pages that said independent vectors were missing now take their wording from the generated verification line.
- generator-kva and rack-cooling vectors extended (standard size, rated current, airflow); the engine agreed first time.
- Author and review status in page footers and the app footer.
- Google Fonts link removed (system fallback fonts); draft `privacy.html` and `terms.html` added, linked from every footer and from the deploy list and guard.
- "Write how to improve this page" feedback box on every page and the app: Submit opens the visitor's email app addressed to the contact email (no server); privacy notice updated.

## v1.6.0 (Conversion Centre, phase 1)
- New `converters/` section generated by `tools/build_converters.js`: a hub with a universal unit converter, 8 category converters (power, energy, voltage, current, resistance, frequency, capacitance, inductance) and 8 direction pages (W/kW, kW/HP, V/kV, J/kWh, each both ways).
- Real, apparent and reactive power convert only within themselves; amps to kW, kVA to kW and kW to kWh are sent to their calculators because they need voltage, power factor or time.
- Factors come from `UNITS` in `engine.js`. A new test layer checks every unit against independently written SI factors, the page behaviour, the generated pages, the sitemap and the deploy guard.
- The sitemap grew from 74 to 91 URLs; `sw.js` caches the new pages.

## v1.7.0 (Conversion Centre, Level 2 and 3)
- 13 new category converters: length, area, volume, mass, force and torque, pressure, temperature, flow rate, magnetic flux density, illuminance and luminance and luminous intensity, density, rotational speed, thermal conductivity. 22 new direction pages (mm and inches, feet and metres, bar and psi, kPa and psi, Celsius and Fahrenheit, kg and lb, N·m and lbf·ft, rpm and rad/s, m³/h and CFM, µT and mG, lux and foot-candles, each both ways).
- Number Base Converter (binary, octal, decimal, hexadecimal, whole numbers, BigInt) and Scientific Notation Converter (scientific, engineering, E notation, plain decimal).
- `CONV_UNITS`, `unitsOf` and `convertUnits` added to `engine.js`. They extend the quantities for the converters only, so the unit lists in the calculators did not change. Shared units are copied from `UNITS`. Temperature is converted through kelvin because it has offsets.
- The hub's universal converter now has 26 quantities and groups the converters as electrical, general engineering and advanced.
- Independent SI and customary factors for every new unit are written in the test, and the page script is checked for temperature.

## v1.8.0 (Conversion Centre, thermal quantities)
- Two new category converters: Temperature Difference (ΔK, Δ°C, Δ°F, Δ°R, an interval with no 32-degree offset) and Specific Heat (J/(kg·K), kJ/(kg·K), J/(g·K), cal/(g·°C), kcal/(kg·°C), BTU/(lb·°F)).
- `tempdiff` and `specheat` added to `CONV_UNITS` in `engine.js`; calculators' unit lists unchanged. The hub's universal converter now has 28 quantities.
- Independent factors for every new unit written in the test (1 BTU/(lb·°F) = 4,186.8 J/(kg·K) by the International Table definitions).
- `engine.js` and `sw.js` bumped to 1.8.0; sitemap grows to 130 URLs.

## v1.8.1 (Conversion Centre, wire gauge page)
- New `converters/wire-gauge-converter.html`: AWG chart (0000 to 40) and SWG chart (7/0 to 50) with mm, inch and mm² values and the nearest AWG for each SWG size. It reads the existing `awgChart` and the SWG table added in v1.5.10; `swgChart()` was added to `engine.js` to expose it.
- Correction: earlier notes said SWG had no engine data. It has had a table and the `swg-wire` calculator since v1.5.10.
- `engine.js` and `sw.js` bumped to 1.8.1; sitemap grows to 131 URLs.
- Committed tree rebuilt with `BASE_URL=https://elecmetric.com` (canonicals, og tags, JSON-LD, sitemap with lastmod, robots.txt `Sitemap:` line, 404). Tests for `converters/` and the category hubs now ignore the deploy-only tags, as the `pages/` test already did.

## v1.8.2 (exact HP, BTU and CFM)
- The calculators' unit table now uses the exact definitions: mechanical horsepower 550 ft·lbf/s = 745.69987158 W (was 745.699872), International Table BTU 1055.05585262 J (was 1055.05585) and CFM 0.3048³/60 m³/s (was 4.7194745e-4). Relative changes were 5.6e-10, 2.5e-9 and 1.4e-8. The rack-cooling BTU/h factor uses the same BTU.
- `HP_W`, `BTU_J` and `CFM_M3S` are named constants at the top of `engine.js`, so the value is written once.
- `tests/gen_vectors.py` uses the exact values and `reference_vectors.json` was regenerated: 30 of 1,601 vectors changed (the hp-kw-converter and BTU/h and TR energy-cost vectors), all in the ninth significant figure or beyond. Displayed results are unchanged at the precision the pages show.
- `engine.js` and `sw.js` bumped to 1.8.2.

## v1.8.3 (brand release: logo and logo colours on every page)
- New `logo.png` (cropped, 760 px wide) is shown on a white plate in the header of the app, every generated page, the 404 and the privacy and terms pages. It is the same file everywhere; generated pages reference it with the page's own root prefix.
- Palette moved from midnight navy, gold and cyan to the logo's navy `#043B79`, orange `#FF7A00` and blue `#0062DE`. Text accents use a darker orange (`#B34A00` light, `#FF9A3D` dark) so they pass contrast on white and on navy; the bright orange is used for lines, borders and primary buttons only. Dark theme follows the same hues. Variable names (`--gold`, `--cyan`) were kept so existing rules still work; `--orange` is new.
- The header carries the logo's orange underline with its pulse mark. Static-page primary buttons are orange with navy text.
- `icon.svg`, `icon-192.png`, `icon-512.png`, `icon-maskable-512.png` and `apple-touch-icon.png` redrawn from the logo's gear and bolt mark; `og-image.png` redrawn by `tools/make_og_image.py` with the logo. `theme-color` and the manifest colours follow the palette.
- `sw.js` caches `logo.png`. `engine.js` and `sw.js` bumped to 1.8.3. No calculation changed.

## v1.8.4 (AGL calculators, hub and documentation review)
- Three new Division 35 calculators: **AGL Maximum Fittings on a CCR** (the reverse of the CCR sizing calculator: whole fittings that fit after cable loss), **AGL Isolating Transformer Current and Loading** (secondary lamp current from the rated current ratio, lamp voltage, primary voltage drop, loading) and **AGL CCR Input Power and Annual Energy** (input kW, kVA and kVAR, annual kWh and running cost). Each has a formula, assumptions, a limits note, self-tests and independent reference vectors. No standard's tables are embedded: efficiency, power factor, lamp power and transformer rating are inputs to be taken from data sheets.
- `tests/gen_vectors.py` gained three blocks at the end (75 vectors), so the first 1,601 vectors are unchanged. `reference_vectors.json` now has 1,676 vectors across 116 calculators.
- The Airport and AGL hub introduction describes the new calculators. The Electrical Fundamentals hub no longer says it holds "converters" and points to the Conversion Centre; every category hub now carries a Conversion Centre link (the calculator-page link test allows `../../converters/index.html`).
- `DEPLOY.md` and `tests/README.md` corrected to the real counts (57 Conversion Centre pages, 23 category converters, 131 sitemap URLs). They still described v1.6.0.
- Hub "limits" statements were checked against `engine.js` (harmonic calculator uses the 3rd, 5th and 7th only; CCR sizes stop at 30 kW) and match.
- `engine.js` and `sw.js` bumped to 1.8.4. No existing calculation changed.

## v1.8.5 (generator-kva boundary vectors)
- `tests/gen_vectors.py` gained a block of 59 boundary vectors for generator-kva, appended at the end so the first 1,676 vectors are unchanged: each of the 23 standard sizes hit exactly (pf 1, no margin) and 10 W over it, eleven exact hits through pf 0.8 and 25 % margin, a load far below the smallest size, and loads above the largest size (2,500.01 and 3,000 kW) where only the required rating is returned. All pass against the engine; no calculation changed.
- Rack-cooling was checked and has no size list, so it has no boundary to test.
- `reference_vectors.json` now has 1,735 vectors across 116 calculators. `engine.js` and `sw.js` bumped to 1.8.5.

## v1.8.6 (transformer and CCR boundary vectors)
- 38 more boundary vectors appended to `tests/gen_vectors.py` (first 1,735 unchanged): transformer-sizing at 10 W over each of the 18 standard sizes, four exact hits through pf 0.8, 25 % growth and 80 % loading, exactly 3,150 kVA, and a load above the list; agl-ccr-loading exactly on each of the seven CCR sizes and 10 W over each, including the case above 30 kW where no size is returned. Breaker-size already had exact and just-over cases for every rating, and generator-kva was done in v1.8.5, so every standard-size list now has boundary vectors. All pass; no calculation changed.
- `reference_vectors.json` now has 1,773 vectors across 116 calculators. `engine.js` and `sw.js` bumped to 1.8.6.

## v1.8.7 (Conversion Centre: electrical hub page, weight naming, efficiency on Amps to kW)
- New `converters/electrical-unit-converter.html`: one converter for voltage, current, resistance, power, apparent and reactive power, energy, frequency, capacitance and inductance, with the limits explained (no factor between volts, amps and ohms; kVA to kW needs a power factor). The Conversion Centre now has 58 pages (24 category converters) and the sitemap 132 URLs.
- The mass converter page is now titled "Mass and Weight Converter" so it matches searches for "weight converter". The URL `mass-converter.html` is unchanged and no duplicate page was added.
- Amps to kW now takes an optional efficiency (default 1, hidden in the calculator's basic mode) and returns output power `Pout = η × P` beside the electrical input power. With η = 1 the result is the same as before. The Amps to kW and kW to Amps pages now show the voltage type (line-to-line or line-to-neutral) and efficiency in the form (`showEng` in `tools/build_pages.js`); the Amps to kW worked example uses η = 0.92.
- Five vectors appended to `tests/gen_vectors.py` (first 1,773 unchanged): amps-to-kw with efficiency for three-phase L-L and L-N, single-phase and DC. `reference_vectors.json` now has 1,778 vectors across 116 calculators. `engine.js` and `sw.js` bumped to 1.8.7.
- Found in a headless Chromium pass (desktop 1280 px and iPhone 13, 11 pages each, no console errors, failed requests or horizontal overflow; offline load from the service-worker cache works): the new electrical page listed "1 A = NaN × 10 mA" under Common conversions because pairs were looked up in the first quantity only. Fixed in `tools/build_converters.js`; the Conversion Centre test now also fails if any converter page shows NaN, undefined, Infinity or [object].
- Accessibility scan with axe-core 4.10.2 (WCAG 2.0/2.1 A and AA plus best-practice rules) on all 135 HTML pages at 1280 px: no A or AA violations, colour contrast passed. Two best-practice findings fixed: the feedback box is now a labelled region (`aria-labelledby`), and each scrollable table has a unique label that includes its quantity and column headings. Re-scan: no violations on any page. The scan covers the pages as first loaded; calculator results inside the app view, keyboard order and a screen-reader pass are still to be checked by hand.

## v1.8.8 (Conversion Centre: battery capacity converter)
- New category page `converters/battery-capacity-converter.html` for the Level 1 "Batteries" division: charge (mAh, Ah, kAh) and energy (Wh, kWh) in one converter, with a note that Ah to Wh needs the nominal voltage, a worked capacity-to-energy example computed from the engine, and links to the mAh to Wh and Wh to mAh calculators. Two direction pages added: `ah-to-mah` and `mah-to-ah`. The Conversion Centre now has 61 pages (25 category converters, 32 direction pages) and the sitemap 135 URLs.
- The engine's `charge` dimension gains **kAh** (1,000 Ah). Coulomb was deliberately not added to it: `C` already belongs to the separate `coulomb` dimension used by Joules, Volts, Charge and eV, and a second `C` broke that calculator in the first attempt (caught by its self-tests and vectors).
- The independent unit-factor test now covers the `charge` dimension (mAh 1e-3, Ah 1, kAh 1e3 Ah). `engine.js` and `sw.js` bumped to 1.8.8; the three new pages are in the service-worker list.
- The stale test-README note about rounded horsepower and BTU constants was removed: v1.8.2 already made them exact.

## v1.8.9 (Feeder Designer: mixed continuous and non-continuous load)
- New optional input **Continuous part of a mixed load** (kW) in the Feeder Designer. On the NEC-style basis the breaker rating and the cable check use the equivalent current Ieq = Ib × ((P − Pc) + 1.25 × Pc) / P, that is non-continuous load plus 125% of the continuous part. Left blank, the existing Continuous / Non-continuous choice applies exactly as before; when entered it overrides that choice. A value above the total load, or negative, is rejected. With Pc = 0 the result equals non-continuous, with Pc = P it equals continuous (tested).
- Other bases (General / IS / BS 7671 / AS/NZS) have no 125% factor and ignore the value. The calculation sheet states the continuous and non-continuous kW and the rule applied. Current is taken as proportional to power at the same voltage and power factor.
- Not covered: several separate loads with different power factors; enter a single total and continuous kW. `engine.js` and `sw.js` bumped to 1.8.9.

## v1.8.10 (Airport and AGL hub wording review)
- Reviewed the Airport and AGL category hub text against what the four AGL calculators actually compute. One overstatement corrected: the isolating transformer calculator was described as giving results "at each brightness step", but it works at the single circuit current entered (and also gives the primary voltage drop), with the lamp power taken from the fitting data sheet at that step. The 6.6 A full-brightness statement, the CCR sizing description, the cross-listed cable and insulation calculators and the limits paragraph were left unchanged. A domain review by you is still needed; see the roadmap. `engine.js` and `sw.js` bumped to 1.8.10.

## v1.8.11 (calculation trust panel layout)
- The trust panel under each calculator had seven cells in an auto-fit grid, so the last row showed a boxed empty cell beside the Engine entry (seen on the 555 timer page). It is now four columns on wide screens with the last cell spanning the free space, two columns up to 900 px and one up to 520 px, so no empty cell appears. Checked in a headless browser at 1400, 700 and 380 px. `engine.js` and `sw.js` bumped to 1.8.11.

## v1.8.12 (audit fixes)
- Unit names that match inherited object properties (for example `constructor`) were accepted by the API and by share links and returned empty results. Units are now checked as own properties only, in the engine and in the app.
- Conduit fill: a cable as large as or larger than the conduit now gets a specific "does not fit" warning, with a test.
- `sitemap.xml` regenerated: it was missing the three battery capacity converter pages (135 URLs).
- `DEPLOY.md`: removed the last section, which wrongly said the committed pages carry canonicals and JSON-LD (they do not; the production build adds them), and corrected the sitemap count.

## v1.9.0 (two AGL calculators)
- New `agl-brightness-steps`: circuit current, lamp load, cable loss, CCR output voltage and load at one step of a 5-step (2.8, 3.4, 4.1, 5.2, 6.6 A) or 3-step (4.8, 5.5, 6.6 A) 6.6 A circuit. Lamp power scales as (I / 6.6)^k with the exponent entered by the user (default 2).
- New `agl-lamp-life-curve`: power-law fit through two lamp life readings from a data sheet, life at another current and lamp replacements per year. No manufacturer curve is built in.
- 55 independent vectors added (30 and 25), written from the textbook relations without reading `engine.js`; the earlier vectors are unchanged. The Airport and AGL hub now has six calculators.

## v1.9.1 (technical review: default resistance fixes)
- Feeder Designer default cable resistance changed from 0.0601 to 0.0766 Ω/km: 0.0601 is the IEC 60228 20 °C value for 300 mm² copper, but the default cable is XLPE at 90 °C. The default example no longer shows a false PASS (2.78% became 3.25% against the 3% limit). Hint now says to use the operating-temperature value.
- Voltage Drop default resistance changed from 0.193 (95 mm² Cu at 20 °C) to 0.246 Ω/km (90 °C); hint updated.
- AC Voltage Drop: conductor temperature now defaults to 70 °C (was 20 °C, shared with the DC calculator, which is unchanged), and a warning appears when the temperature is below 50 °C, giving the rise in the resistive part at 90 °C.
- Calculator page test now uses the 70 °C textbook value; two regression tests added. Pages regenerated.

## v1.9.2 (technical review: deploy packaging)
- Pages workflow and DEPLOY.md now upload `icon-192.png`, `icon-512.png`, `icon-maskable-512.png`, `apple-touch-icon.png` and `logo.png`. They were listed in the service worker shell, the manifest and the page heads but not copied, so `cache.addAll` would have failed and the offline cache never installed on the live site.
- New `tools/check_assets.js <dir>` checks the upload set itself (service worker shell, manifest icons, root files named in page heads); the workflow runs it after copying, and the tests prove it catches a missing icon and that the workflow copies every root file the shell needs.
- `engine.js` is now network-first in the service worker, so a new page cannot run on the previous release's formulas for one visit.
- Transformer Fault Current warns when impedance is below 1 % (a typed per-unit value or a unit slip), with two regression tests.

## v1.9.3 (audit fixes)
- Router and API: calculator ids that match inherited object properties (`constructor`, `toString`, `hasOwnProperty`, `__proto__`) no longer resolve. `BY_ID` and the router's special-page map are null-prototype objects. Before, `#constructor` crashed the calculator view and was saved in the recents list; `E.api('constructor', {})` threw instead of returning `{ok:false}`. Regression tests added.
- Service worker: the cache-first branch returns `Response.error()` instead of `undefined` when a file is neither cached nor reachable; navigations with a query string are no longer written to the cache, so it cannot grow by one entry per URL variant.
- `STANDARDS.md` and the roadmap release note corrected: mixed continuous load is modelled since v1.8.9, and the browser and axe passes already run are stated.

## v1.9.4 (legal page asset links)
- `privacy.html` and `terms.html` linked `../icon.svg`, `../apple-touch-icon.png` and `../manifest.webmanifest`. They sit at the site root, so on a subpath deployment (for example `github.io/elecmetric/`) those three links returned 404. Cause: `page()` in `tools/build_pages.js` read `o.prefix || '../'`, and the legal pages pass an empty prefix, which is falsy. It now tests `!== undefined`. A regression test checks that the three head links on both pages are root-relative and the files exist.

## v1.9.5 (audit follow-ups)
- Content-Security-Policy added as a meta tag to `index.html` and, through `page()` in `tools/build_pages.js`, to every generated page (GitHub Pages cannot send headers). Everything must come from the same origin: no remote scripts, styles, fonts, images or requests, no plugins, `base-uri` fixed, forms cannot submit anywhere. Inline script and style are still allowed because every page carries its own, so this limits what injected markup can load or send, not whether it can run. A test checks every html file. Not yet exercised in a real browser: load a few pages with the console open before release.
- Out-of-range warning: when a result overflows or divides by zero (shown as a dash), the calculator now says so, unless a more specific warning already explains it. Test added.
- The committed `404.html` still said "116 calculators"; it is refreshed from the production build (118).
- Checked, no change needed: the brightness-step exponent hint already tells the user to use the lamp data sheet.

## v1.9.6 (SEO audit items 1 to 3)
- Internal links: every conversion page now ends its Related block with "Also see" links to up to four sibling conversions that share a unit or calculator (walked in a circle so inbound links are spread evenly), the wire gauge page links to the AWG and SWG converter, and `ohms-law`, `watts-volts-amps-ohms` and `power-factor` link to `power-calculator` by hand. The home page gains a "More tools" row (wire gauge, number base and scientific notation converters, AGL CCR loading, energy consumption). Before this, 14 pages had one or two inbound links; a test now fails if any indexable page has fewer than three.
- `lastmod`: the workflow already passed `SITE_LASTMOD`. It now stops if that is empty, and `check_deploy.js` with `REQUIRE_LASTMOD=1` fails unless every sitemap URL has a valid, non-future `lastmod`.
- Unbuilt tree: `check_assets.js <folder> --production` fails if `index.html` has no canonical (the committed tree has none by design). The workflow and the manual steps in `DEPLOY.md` use it.
- Version bumped to 1.9.6 (engine and service worker).

## v1.10.0 (lighting calculators, from the RapidTables comparison)
- Six calculators added, closing the lighting gap found in the comparison with rapidtables.com: `lumens-lux-area` (any two of lumens, lux, area, with a fraction-reaching-surface factor and footcandles), `candela-lux-distance` (inverse-square law with angle of incidence), `candela-lumens-beam` (solid angle of a cone), `lumens-watts` (luminous efficacy), `lux-to-watts` (lamp lumens, watts and W/m² for a target lux) and `agl-light-intensity-distance` (Allard's law with the meteorological optical range defined at 5% transmissivity).
- The AGL calculator reports illuminance at the eye only. It embeds no visual threshold or RVR method; the page says so and points to the method the user follows.
- 145 independent vectors added in a new block at the end of `tests/gen_vectors.py` (the earlier 1,833 are byte-identical). Every solver case enters exactly the stated variables.
- Divisions are unchanged (33). Lighting calculators sit in division 28 (Lighting and Facilities hub) and the AGL one in division 35 (Airport and AGL hub).
- Version and service-worker cache bumped to 1.10.0. Pages regenerated with `node tools/build_pages.js`.

## v1.11.0 (unit gaps, from the RapidTables comparison)
- Converter units added: GHz, THz and Hz to rad/s (angular frequency, 1 Hz = 2π rad/s) to frequency; GV to voltage; GW and BHP to power (BHP is brake horsepower, the same 550 ft·lbf/s as mechanical HP); calorie (International Table, 4.1868 J) and GeV to energy; and coulomb units (C, mC, kC) to the battery charge quantity, so Ah and coulomb now convert directly (1 Ah = 3600 C).
- The units are added to `CONV_UNITS` only. Calculator unit lists are unchanged.
- The independent factors in `tests/run_tests.js` were extended from definitions, each new unit has a definition note, and spot checks cover the new units. Two assertions that compared converter unit lists with `E.UNITS` now compare with `E.unitsOf` or `E.CONV_UNITS`, because the converter lists are now longer than the calculator lists on purpose. The converter quantity count in the hub test goes from 18 to 23.
- Not done in this step: dBm and dBW. They are logarithmic, so they need a separate power level converter rather than a factor table.
- Version and service-worker cache bumped to 1.11.0. Pages regenerated with `node tools/build_pages.js`.
- Angle and ratio converters added (RapidTables comparison, item 4, table-based part): an Angle Converter (mrad, rad, deg, arcmin, arcsec, grad, turn) with degrees to radians and radians to degrees pages, and a Percent and PPM Converter (ratio, %, ‰, ppm, ppb) with ppm to percent and percent to ppm pages. Factors are written from definitions in the test. The Conversion Centre is now 67 pages and the sitemap 141 URLs.
- DMS to Decimal Degrees Converter added (`converters/dms-converter.html`, kind `number`): DMS to decimal and decimal to DMS in one page, with a sign for south and west, validation (minutes and seconds below 60, whole degrees beside smaller parts), the 60-second carry on rounding, a common values table, the distance each unit covers, and a note on survey and airfield coordinates. Pure functions `dmsToDec` and `decToDms` run on the page and in the build and are tested against D + M/60 + S/3600. The Conversion Centre is now 68 pages and the sitemap 142 URLs.
- dBm to Watts Converter added (`converters/dbm-converter.html`, kind `number`): W, mW, kW, dBm and dBW in one page, with all five results shown, common levels, the formulas, and a note that voltage decibels use 20 log10. dBm and dBW are logarithmic, so they have a pure function `powerLevel` rather than an entry in the factor tables. Tested against 10 log10(P / 1 mW) and round trips. The Conversion Centre is now 69 pages and the sitemap 143 URLs.
- Still open from item 4: decimal to fraction to percent.
- Reference pages and worked examples added to `converters/` (kinds `reference` and `example`, generated by `tools/build_reference.js`, logic in `tools/reference_logic.js`): Resistor Color Code Calculator (3, 4, 5 and 6 bands, both directions, tolerance range, colour table, E12 and E24), Ohm's Law Formulas (twelve forms, worked examples), Kirchhoff's Laws (three worked circuits), Coulomb's Law Calculator, and Electrical Symbols (19 inline SVG drawings plus quantity, letter and unit table).
- 17 numeric pages: 100 to 10,000 watts to amps (10) and 5 to 100 amps to watts (7), each with a small calculator (DC, single-phase, three-phase, power factor), a table across common supplies and a note on typical loads. Every figure comes from the formula.
- Tests: new section 19 with expected values written independently (all 1,080 digit and multiplier combinations, 264 E24 round trips, Coulomb from 1/(4 pi eps0), every number on the 17 pages). Checked in headless Chromium: forms, console and CSP clean, no sideways scroll at 390 px.
- Sitemap 165 URLs. Not done: decimal to fraction to percent (skipped, little fit for the site).
- 12 more numeric pages: 1 to 50 kW to amps (7) and 10, 16, 32, 63 and 100 amps to kW (5), same layout and tests as the watts pages. Sitemap 177 URLs; Conversion Centre 103 pages.
- 8 kVA to amps pages (25, 63, 100, 160, 250, 400, 630 and 1,000 kVA, the common transformer ratings): single and three-phase, 400 V default, no power factor (kVA is apparent power). Sitemap 185 URLs; Conversion Centre 111 pages.

## v1.13.1 (dB to ratio converter)
- New `converters/db-converter.html` (kind `number`): dB, power ratio and voltage or current ratio in one page, from a pure function `dbRatio` (dB = 10 log10 of the power ratio = 20 log10 of the voltage ratio). Includes a table of common values, the formulas, when to use 10 and when 20, an FAQ, and two-way links with the dBm to watts converter. It is linked from the Conversion Centre hub, the dBm page and the home page tools list.

## v1.35.0 (six more formula articles)
- 6 new reference articles in `converters/` from `tools/reference_articles.js`: battery C-rate and cycle life (current for a C-rate, ideal run time, energy throughput over the life), EV charging time (charge point power, energy added, time with the charger efficiency), conveyor belt power (material per metre, friction force, lift power, motor input), parallel plate capacitors (capacitance from area, gap and relative permittivity, charge, energy, field), solenoid and coil inductance (inductance, flux density, stored energy) and power factor correction payback (capacitor kVAR, fall in kVA demand, saving, payback in months). Same layout and rules as before: every number computed in the generator.
- Tests: figures re-written in `tests/run_tests.js` in a different arrangement (for example the belt power as mass flow times g times (mu L + h) plus the belt friction term, the coil inductance as N times flux over current, the capacitance with eps0 from 1 / (mu0 c^2), the correction kVAR from sine and cosine). Counts: 138 reference pages, 265 pages in `converters/`, 357 sitemap URLs.
- Illustrative values: all inputs. The C-rate page gives no cycle life limits, the EV page uses an example efficiency of 0.9 and ignores the charge taper, the conveyor page is a first estimate and points to ISO 5048 and CEMA, the capacitor permittivities are typical values, the solenoid page is for a long coil below core saturation, and the payback page uses an example tariff and bank cost.
- Still open: medium and long transmission-line models, IE efficiency class tables (not reproduced), cable tray rules by country, 9548.8 torque constant on older motor and gear pages.
- Version and service-worker cache bumped to 1.35.0. Pages, `sitemap.xml`, the `sw.js` list and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md` regenerated.

## v1.36.0 (six more formula articles)
- 6 new reference articles in `converters/` from `tools/reference_articles.js`: transformer through-fault current (rated currents, fault current with an infinite or finite source, I2t over the rated duration), the transformer EMF equation (voltage per turn, turns for a voltage, flux density by frequency), delta-star resistor conversion (both directions, equal resistors), current shunts (resistance, power, current from the voltage, resistance for a drop), solar energy yield and degradation (yearly energy, specific yield, output by year, energy over 25 years) and Faraday electrolysis (mass deposited, coating thickness, time for a thickness). Same layout and rules as before: every number computed in the generator.
- Tests: figures re-written in `tests/run_tests.js` in a different arrangement (for example the fault current from S / (sqrt3 V Z), the voltage per turn as 2 pi f flux / sqrt2, the delta back from the star, the 25 year energy as a geometric sum, the plating time from the charge needed). Counts: 144 reference pages, 271 pages in `converters/`, 363 sitemap URLs.
- Caught while building: the generator limits descriptions to 160 characters, and two of the new ones were 164 and 163, so they were shortened.
- Illustrative values: all inputs. The through-fault page is a terminal fault and ignores cable impedance. The EMF page uses 4.443 (sqrt2 pi) and rounds the turns. The shunt page leaves the power rating and the temperature coefficient to the maker. The solar page uses example degradation of 2% then 0.5% a year, and the plating page uses an example current efficiency of 95%.
- Still open: medium and long transmission-line models, IE efficiency class tables (not reproduced), cable tray rules by country, 9548.8 torque constant on older motor and gear pages.
- Version and service-worker cache bumped to 1.36.0. Pages, `sitemap.xml`, the `sw.js` list and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md` regenerated.

## v1.39.0
- Release number aligned with the upload name (the v1_39_0 zip carried 1.38.0 internally). Engine version and service-worker cache are 1.39.0
- Six new calculator pages: `transformer-efficiency`, `cable-sc-withstand`, `earth-grid-resistance`, `agl-isolating-transformer`, `agl-max-fittings`, `agl-ccr-input-energy` (19 in all; AGL has four). Cross-linked from related pages, the engineering hub and the category hubs. `sw.js` cache list, committed `sitemap.xml` (385 URLs), `DEPLOY.md`, `ROADMAP.md` and `tests/README.md` updated

- Feeder Designer: three new steps. Fault at the far end of the feeder (source impedance plus cable impedance, added arithmetically, shown three-phase and phase-to-phase), breaker let-through energy vs cable withstand (I²t ≤ k² S², optional input), and instantaneous pick-up vs the far-end fault (optional input). Both checks show "not verified" until the input is entered and fail the feeder only on a real failure. Trip curve and selectivity are still stated as not checked. The next step below covers the earth fault loop
- Feeder Designer: earth fault loop step (optional inputs Ze and PE resistance, reusing the pick-up as the operating current). Zs = Ze + |(R + Rpe) L + j X L| (arithmetic sum, phase reactance only, bolted fault), If = (V/√3) / Zs, pass when If is at or above the pick-up. Not verified until all three inputs are entered. Permitted disconnection times and Zs limits are not embedded: they come from the wiring rules

## v1.40.0
- Benchmark practice 1 (individual calculator URLs): every calculator now has a crawlable page. 79 new pages in `calculators/<slug>/`, generated from the registry by `tools/calc_pages_auto.js` (purpose, inputs, outputs, formula, assumptions, notes, related calculators; every number computed by the engine at build time). With the 19 hand-written pages that is 98 calculator pages

## v1.41.0
- Benchmark practice 2 (better formula explanations): all 98 calculator pages now carry an Inputs and results table (unit, allowed range, note, taken from the registry), a How it is calculated section and a Standards and further reading section (`tools/calc_refs.js`, one entry per category hub). The reference lists are background reading only and are pending domain review; a reference is not a reviewer approval

## v1.42.0
- Benchmark practice 3 (related-calculator navigation): the hand-picked "Related" chips on the 98 calculator pages are replaced by a "Related calculators" section built from one link graph (`tools/calc_related.js`). Each link has a one-line purpose from the registry and is grouped as Next steps (the registry `next` list, then the hand-chosen links), Usually done before this (calculators whose `next` list names this one) and Also related (same division, same category, neighbouring categories named in the hub, then shared keywords). Every next-step and before-step link is two-way, and topic links are made two-way too, so a calculator that is filed under "same topic" by another links back to it. Before: 2 to 5 peer links per page, 293 of 409 one-way. A related calculator is linked to its own page, or to its conversion page for the 28 calculators that have no page of their own, and to the app only as a last resort.
- The conversion pages that have a calculator behind them (not the pure unit pages or the AWG page) gain a "Next steps in a design" row, for example kW to amps now links to voltage drop and breaker rating. The in-app link to each public page is still not added.
- Tests: new section 23 (every next-step link is two-way, at least 6 links per page, none to itself or repeated, every registry next step is linked, every link resolves, every page is linked from at least two others, the transformer sizing page links to the five other transformer calculators). The FAQ, link-resolution and "kwh" tests were adjusted for the new link shapes (the "kwh" check now ignores URLs).

## v1.43.0
- Benchmark practice 4 (simple calculator presentation): on all 98 calculator pages the calculator and its live result now come straight after the title and one short line, before any explanation. The longer introduction moved to an "About this calculator" section below, and a row of jump links (About, Formula, Worked example, Inputs and results, FAQ, Related) follows the calculator. Formula, worked example, assumptions, FAQ and related links stay open. Four reference sections (Inputs and results, How it is calculated, the typical-values table, Standards and further reading) are folded into closed `<details>` blocks; their text stays in the HTML, they open when printed or linked to by `#inputs`, `#method`, `#typical` or `#standards`. The static default answer card is kept for visitors without JavaScript and hidden once the live form shows. On a 1280x800 desktop the calculator now starts about 90 to 110 px higher (for example 380 to 271 px); on a 375x667 phone, about 130 to 155 px higher.
- Not changed: the conversion pages, the category hubs and the app view. The engineering-mode inputs still follow the existing form rules.
- Tests: new section 24 (calculator before explanation, one lede, four folded sections, anchors present); the calculator-page link check now accepts in-page anchors whose target exists.

## v1.46.0
- New division 37 Engineering Mathematics, built one calculator at a time from the CEO competitor analysis (Calculator.net benchmark). First calculator: `complex-number` (Complex Number Calculator): add, subtract, multiply or divide two complex numbers, or convert one between rectangular (a + jb) and polar (magnitude and angle) form. Results in both forms, angle in degrees and radians, with the working. Division by zero is rejected, a zero result warns that its angle is undefined, and atan2 gives the correct quadrant. 127 calculators across 34 divisions
- 14 built-in self-tests and 70 independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (Python `cmath`, written from the textbook definitions; 2,078 in all, the earlier 2,008 unchanged). The vectors include the axis and quadrant edge cases
- New category hub `engineering-mathematics` (13 hubs) with its standards note in `tools/calc_refs.js`; new formula guide `complex-number-formulas` (146 formula articles, 151 reference pages, 286 pages in `converters/`), mapped to the calculator in `tools/calc_articles.js`
- The app router sends "complex number", "rectangular to polar", "polar to rectangular" and "polar form" queries to `complex-number` (router test 103 of 103). "Phasor" and AC Ohm's law queries still go to `ac-ohms-law`
- Version and service-worker cache 1.46.0. `sw.js` caches the three new pages and the committed `sitemap.xml` lists 479 URLs. `REVIEW_PACK.md` and `API.md` regenerated. Tests: counts, a worked-example check for the new guide (values written again independently and tied to the calculator through `E.api`)
- Next in the plan: Polar to Rectangular and Phasor calculators, Trigonometry, Percentage Error and the Scientific Calculator, one at a time

## v1.47.0
- Second calculator in division 37 Engineering Mathematics: `phasor-sum` (Phasor Sum and Difference, polar inputs). Adds or subtracts up to three phasors given as magnitude and angle (voltages or currents at different phase angles), with a plus or minus choice on phasors 2 and 3 and a magnitude of 0 to leave a phasor out. Results: resultant magnitude, angle in degrees and radians, rectangular parts and the plain sum of the magnitudes, with the working. A balanced three-phase set or a phasor subtracted from itself reports zero with a warning that the angle is undefined. Rectangular inputs and multiplication or division stay in `complex-number`; the voltage, current and impedance relation stays in `ac-ohms-law`, so nothing is duplicated. 128 calculators across 34 divisions
- 9 built-in self-tests (including 230 ∠0° − 230 ∠−120° = 398.37 ∠30°, the three-phase line voltage) and 36 independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (Python `cmath.rect`, written from the textbook definition; 2,114 in all, the earlier 2,078 unchanged)
- New formula guide `phasor-addition-formulas` (147 formula articles, 152 reference pages, 287 pages in `converters/`), mapped to the calculator in `tools/calc_articles.js`. The engineering mathematics hub lists the new tool
- The app router sends "phasor addition", "add phasors", "phasor sum", "sum of phasors" and "resultant phasor" queries to `phasor-sum` (router test 108 of 108). A bare "phasor" or "phasor calculator" query still goes to `ac-ohms-law`
- Version and service-worker cache 1.47.0. `sw.js` caches the new calculator page and guide; the committed `sitemap.xml` lists 481 URLs. `REVIEW_PACK.md` and `API.md` regenerated
- Next in the plan: Percentage Error, Trigonometry and the Scientific Calculator, one at a time

## v1.48.0
- Third calculator in division 37 Engineering Mathematics: `percentage-error` (Percentage Error). Compares a measured or calculated value with a true (reference) value and returns the error, absolute error, relative error and the signed and absolute percentage error, with the working. The error is taken relative to |true|, so a negative result means the reading is low. A zero true value is rejected, and an error larger than the true value itself warns. 129 calculators across 34 divisions
- 8 built-in self-tests and 32 independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (written from the definition; 2,146 in all, the earlier 2,114 unchanged)
- New formula guide `percentage-error-formulas` (148 formula articles, 153 reference pages, 288 pages in `converters/`) with a voltmeter tolerance example and a measured-constant example, mapped to the calculator in `tools/calc_articles.js`. The engineering mathematics hub lists the new tool
- Version and service-worker cache 1.48.0. `sw.js` caches the new page and guide; the committed `sitemap.xml` lists 483 URLs. `REVIEW_PACK.md` and `API.md` regenerated. Router test 113 of 113
- Next in the plan: Trigonometry and the Scientific Calculator, one at a time

## v1.50.0
- Scientific Calculator, a standalone tool at `calculators/scientific-calculator/` (not a registry calculator, so it has its own engine, page generator and tests). **Standard mode:** safe tokenizer and recursive-descent parser (no `eval`, no `Function`), correct operator precedence (`-2^2` is -4, `2^3^2` is 2^9), implicit multiplication (`3(4+5)`, `2sin(30)`), `%`, `!`, `°`, sin/cos/tan with sec/csc/cot, inverse and hyperbolic functions, log, ln, log2 and any-base logarithms, powers, square, cube and nth roots, factorial (0 to 170), nCr, nPr, mod, abs and rounding, pi, e, EXP, Ans, RND, memory (MC, MR, MS, M+, M-), DEG, RAD and GRAD with exact values at multiples of 30 and 45 degrees, output as automatic, scientific, engineering (with SI prefix) or fraction, 5 to 15 significant digits, history, keyboard input, copy result and a shareable link (the expression in the URL hash; nothing is stored). Domain errors and division by zero are explained in words. **Engineering mode:** complex numbers with `j`, rectangular and polar results, polar input (`230∠30` or `230<30`), re, im, abs, arg, conj, `par()` for impedances in parallel, SI prefixes after a number (`4.7k`, `100u`), a unit label, a table of real part, imaginary part, magnitude and phase, the working, and a phasor diagram. In Standard mode a result outside the real numbers is a domain error; Engineering mode returns the principal complex value.
- The engine (`tools/scicalc_engine.js`) is one file with no dependencies, required by the tests in Node and inlined unchanged into the page, so the page runs the code the tests run. `tools/scicalc_client.js` is the page script, `tools/scicalc_page.js` the generator. Every example number in the page text is calculated by the engine at build time.
- 657 independent vectors and 14 polar conversions in `tests/gen_scicalc_vectors.py` (Python `math` and `cmath`, written without reading the JavaScript; they cover all three angle modes, inverse and hyperbolic functions, logarithms, powers and roots, factorials, precedence, 70 error cases and complex arithmetic). On a branch cut (for example asin(2)) the sign of the imaginary part is a convention, so those vectors check the real part and the size of the imaginary part. The document's reference cases (sin 30° 0.5, cos 60° 0.5, tan 45° 1, log 1000 3, ln e 1, √144 12, 5! 120, 2³ 8, 1/0 error) are tested, and Z = 10 + j15 Ω gives 18.028 ∠ 56.31°.
- The page is linked from the home page, the calculators hub and every category hub. Placed at `calculators/scientific-calculator/` to match the site structure, not `/math/` as the product note suggested. Not embedded from calculator.net: an original engine and branding.
- Version and service-worker cache 1.50.0. `sw.js` caches the new page; the committed `sitemap.xml` lists 486 URLs. `REVIEW_PACK.md` and `API.md` regenerated
- Next in the plan: Phasor, Complex Number and Trigonometry tools can link into Engineering mode; graphing, equation solving, matrices and numerical calculus remain separate future tools

## v1.49.0
- Fourth calculator in division 37 Engineering Mathematics: `trig-functions` (Trigonometry: sin, cos, tan). Gives sin, cos, tan, csc, sec and cot of an angle in degrees or radians, with the angle in both units and the equivalent angle from 0° to 360°. An angle within 1e-9° of a multiple of 90° is treated as exact, so cos 90° is 0 and tan 90° is not defined (shown as a dash with the reason). Angles beyond ±1,000,000,000° are rejected. 130 calculators across 34 divisions
- 10 built-in self-tests and 50 independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (Python `math.sin`, `math.cos` and `math.tan` on the angle in radians; 2,196 in all, the earlier 2,146 unchanged). The vectors include the standard angles from −180° to 720° and radian inputs
- New formula guide `trigonometry-formulas` (149 formula articles, 154 reference pages, 289 pages in `converters/`) with a power factor example (0.8 gives 36.87°, Q = 75 kVAR for 100 kW) and a 150° example, mapped to the calculator in `tools/calc_articles.js`. The engineering mathematics hub lists the new tool
- The app router sends "trigonometry calculator", "trig calculator", "sin cos tan", "sine calculator", "cosine calculator" and "tangent calculator" queries to `trig-functions` (router test 119 of 119)
- Version and service-worker cache 1.49.0. `sw.js` caches the new page and guide; the committed `sitemap.xml` lists 485 URLs. `REVIEW_PACK.md` and `API.md` regenerated
- Next in the plan: the Scientific Calculator

## v1.51.0
- Voltage drop, AC voltage drop from conductor size and maximum cable length take a new input, conductors per phase in parallel (1 to 9, default 1, shown in the engineering view). Resistance and reactance per phase are divided by the number in parallel, and the maximum length is multiplied by it. The assumption is stated on each page: identical conductors, the same length, sharing the current equally, mutual effects ignored. A warning says so when more than one is chosen. Results for one conductor are unchanged.

## v1.52.0
- Statistics batch in division 37 Engineering Mathematics: `mean-median-mode` (Mean, Median and Mode) and `standard-deviation` (Standard Deviation and Variance). Each takes up to 12 readings in one consistent unit (the first is required, blank boxes are ignored). 132 calculators across 34 divisions
- Mean, Median and Mode returns the mean, median, mode, count, sum, smallest, largest and range. With no repeated value there is no mode (a dash and a warning); with several tied modes the smallest is shown and all are listed in the warning. One reading warns that the spread says nothing
- Standard Deviation and Variance returns both the sample (n − 1) and population (n) standard deviation and variance, the mean, the standard error of the mean and the coefficient of variation. The sample values need two readings and are a dash for one; the coefficient of variation is a dash at a zero mean, with a warning in each case. No distribution or confidence interval is assumed
- Absolute and relative error from the same CEO plan are already covered by `percentage-error` (it returns error, absolute error, relative error and percentage error), so no separate page was built
- 8 and 8 built-in self-tests and 52 independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (Python `statistics` module; 2,276 in all, the earlier 2,224 unchanged)
- New formula guide `statistics-formulas` (150 formula articles, 155 reference pages, 290 pages in `converters/`) with a six-reading voltage example (mean 230.2167 V, sample s 0.4491 V), mapped to both calculators in `tools/calc_articles.js`. The engineering mathematics hub lists both tools; the router sends "mean median mode", "average calculator", "median calculator", "mode calculator", "standard deviation", "variance calculator" and "sample variance" queries to them (router test 127 of 127)
- Version and service-worker cache 1.52.0. `sw.js` caches the new pages; the committed `sitemap.xml` lists 489 URLs. `REVIEW_PACK.md` and `API.md` regenerated
- Limits: 12 readings is a fixed number of input boxes, not a list, because the engine has no list or text input; a larger data set needs a list input type
- Next in the plan: Division 01 (Fraction, Ratio, Exponents, Roots, Logarithm, Rounding, Quadratic) or the phasor diagram on the complex number page

## v1.53.0
- Division 01 batch in division 37 Engineering Mathematics: `quadratic-solver` (Quadratic Equation Solver), `logarithm` (Logarithm Calculator) and `powers-roots` (Exponent and Root Calculator). 135 calculators across 34 divisions
- Quadratic Equation Solver: roots of ax² + bx + c = 0 as real or complex numbers (real and imaginary parts shown separately), the discriminant and the vertex, with the working. The small real root comes from x₁ × x₂ = c / a, so b = 10⁸ with a = c = 1 still gives −1e-8. x₁ is the root with +√D, so for a negative a the larger root is x₂. a = 0 is rejected (the equation is linear). A repeated root and a negative discriminant each warn
- Logarithm Calculator: log to any base b (greater than 0, not 1) of x (greater than 0), plus the common, natural and binary logarithms. Base 10 and base 2 use the exact functions, so log₁₀ 1000 is 3
- Exponent and Root Calculator: x to the power n, the k-th root (k a whole number 1 to 1000), square, cube and reciprocal. A negative base needs a whole-number exponent, an even root of a negative number is rejected, an odd root of a negative number is returned as a negative, zero to a negative power is rejected, and 0⁰ is taken as 1 with a warning. Real numbers only
- 8, 10 and 11 built-in self-tests and 80 independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (Python `math` and `cmath`; 2,356 in all, the earlier 2,276 unchanged)
- New formula guide `quadratic-log-power-formulas` (151 formula articles, 156 reference pages, 291 pages in `converters/`) with real-root, complex-root and decibel examples, mapped to all three calculators in `tools/calc_articles.js`. The engineering mathematics hub lists them; the router sends "quadratic equation", "quadratic formula", "log calculator", "natural log", "exponent calculator", "square root calculator", "cube root calculator" and "nth root" queries to them (router test 137 of 137)

## v1.54.0
- Second Division 01 batch in division 37 Engineering Mathematics: `percentage-calculator` (Percentage Calculator), `ratio-calculator` (Ratio Calculator) and `rounding-sig-figs` (Rounding and Significant Figures). 138 calculators across 34 divisions
- Percentage Calculator: from two numbers A and B, A% of B, A as a percentage of B, the percentage change from A to B (against |A|, so 80 to 100 is +25% and 100 to 80 is −20%), and B increased and decreased by A%. A percentage of zero and a change from zero are shown as a dash with a warning; A above 100% warns on the decrease
- Ratio Calculator: simplifies A : B with the greatest common divisor when both are whole numbers (decimals are not simplified and warn), gives A / B and each share of A + B, and solves A : B = C : X when C is entered. A and B must be greater than zero
- Rounding and Significant Figures: rounds to decimal places (−6 to 15) and to significant figures (1 to 15), plus rounded up (toward +∞), rounded down (toward −∞) and truncated to the same places. Halves round away from zero and the number is rounded as written, so 2.5 gives 3 and 1.005 to two places gives 1.01. Decimal places and significant figures must be whole numbers; the number is limited to ±1e15
- 8, 8 and 9 built-in self-tests and 84 independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (plain Python, `math.gcd` and the `decimal` module; 2,440 in all, the earlier 2,356 unchanged)
- New formula guide `percentage-ratio-rounding-formulas` (152 formula articles, 157 reference pages, 292 pages in `converters/`) with percentage change, turns ratio and rounding examples, mapped to the three calculators in `tools/calc_articles.js`. The engineering mathematics hub lists them; the router sends "percentage calculator", "percent change", "percentage increase", "ratio calculator", "simplify ratio", "proportion calculator", "rounding calculator", "significant figures calculator", "sig fig calculator" and "round to decimal places" queries to them (router test 147 of 147)

## v1.56.0
- Phasor diagram on the Complex Numbers page (`calculators/complex-number/`). Under the results the page now draws z₁, z₂ and the result as arrows on the complex plane (real axis across, imaginary axis up), with the result's angle θ shown as an arc and in a caption. Addition also draws the two dashed sides of the parallelogram; "Convert z₁ only" draws z₁ alone. It redraws as the inputs change and disappears when an input is invalid. No calculation changes
- Built as `llPhasorSvg` in `tools/build_pages.js` (plain SVG, no library, no remote files), fed by `llCompute`, which now returns a `diagram` object for this one calculator (`cfg.diagram = 'phasor'`). Only the complex number page carries the drawing code. The drawing scales to the largest phasor, uses the page colour variables (so it follows light and dark mode), has a text alternative (`aria-label`) giving z₁, z₂ and the result, and names each arrow in text, so colour is not the only cue. A value that is not finite draws nothing
- 9 new test checks (data returned by the form, arrow counts and labels for add, multiply and convert, the angle caption, a non-finite value, zero, huge and tiny phasors with no NaN or Infinity, coordinates inside the drawing, and that no other page carries the code); 16,229 checks in all
- Checked in headless Chromium (the committed page opened from disk, the form driven through multiply, add, convert and subtract with negative values, a blank input, and a 375 px phone width in light and dark mode): no script errors, no sideways scroll, drawing correct in each case. This is not the real phone, keyboard and screen-reader pass listed under Open work 2

## v1.57.0
- First Division 02 batch in division 37 Engineering Mathematics: `simultaneous-equations` (Simultaneous Equations Solver) and `linear-interpolation` (Linear Interpolation Calculator). 141 calculators across 34 divisions
- Simultaneous Equations Solver: two equations in two unknowns or three in three, each written a x + b y + c z = k (enter 0 for a missing term; the third column and the third equation are blank and ignored for two unknowns). Returns x, y and z (z is a dash for two unknowns), the determinant of the coefficients and the largest check error from substituting the answer back. The solution is found by elimination with row swapping and the determinant is shown for a check by Cramer's rule. A determinant that is zero (within 1e-12 of the size of the coefficients) is rejected as having no single solution; a determinant very small against the coefficients warns that the equations are nearly dependent. Three unknowns with any of the third-equation or z entries blank are rejected. Coefficients are limited to ±1e12
- Linear Interpolation Calculator: from two points (x₁, y₁) and (x₂, y₂) gives y at a chosen x, the slope, the fraction of the way from x₁ to x₂ and the value at x = 0, and, when a y is entered, the x that gives it. A chosen x or y outside the two points warns that the result is extrapolated. x₁ equal to x₂ is rejected, and a y to look up on a level line (y₁ equal to y₂) is rejected. The default is a 4 to 20 mA scaling over 0 to 100
- Outputs that do not apply (z for two unknowns, x for a blank y) are returned as null and shown as a dash, so they do not trigger the generic out-of-range warning
- 7 and 8 built-in reference self-tests, with the standard input and output checks, and 72 independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (43 and 29; exact `fractions.Fraction` arithmetic; 2,551 in all, the earlier 2,479 unchanged)
- No new guide: the calculators are mapped in `tools/calc_articles.js` to `kirchhoffs-laws`, `thevenin-norton-formulas` and `quadratic-log-power-formulas` (equations) and to `loop-4-20ma-formulas`, `measurement-uncertainty-formulas` and `pt100-rtd-formulas` (interpolation); 152 formula articles and 292 pages in `converters/` unchanged. The engineering mathematics hub lists both tools and its text names them; the router sends "simultaneous equations", "system of equations", "3x3 system", "linear interpolation", "interpolation calculator" and "extrapolation" queries to them (router test 160 of 160 first, 161 of 161 in the top three). "solve for x and y" is not tested, because the Watts, Volts, Amps and Ohms solver outranks the equations solver for that phrase
- Version and service-worker cache 1.57.0. `sw.js` caches the two new pages; the committed `sitemap.xml` lists 500 URLs. `REVIEW_PACK.md` and `API.md` regenerated
