## v1.58.0
- Second Division 02 batch in division 37 Engineering Mathematics: `matrix-calculator` (Matrix Calculator, 2 × 2 and 3 × 3) and `vector-calculator` (Vector Calculator, 3D). 143 calculators across 34 divisions
- Matrix Calculator: a 2 × 2 or 3 × 3 matrix entered row by row (the third row and column are blank and ignored for 2 × 2). Returns the determinant, the trace, the nine inverse entries (a dash outside the 2 × 2 block for a 2 × 2 matrix) and the largest error of A × A⁻¹ against the identity. The inverse is found by Gauss-Jordan elimination with row swapping. A determinant of zero (within 1e-12 of the size of the entries) is rejected as having no inverse; a determinant very small against the entries warns that the matrix is nearly singular. A 3 × 3 matrix with any blank entry is rejected. Entries are limited to ±1e12. Matrix multiplication, rank and larger sizes are not included
- Vector Calculator: two vectors A and B in three dimensions (leave z at 0 for a flat vector). Returns |A|, |B|, A · B, the angle between them in degrees (from atan2 of |A × B| and A · B, which stays accurate near 0° and 180°), the three components and the length of A × B, and the component of A along B. A zero vector is allowed but warns, and the angle and the component are then shown as a dash. Components are limited to ±1e12
- Outputs that do not apply are returned as null and shown as a dash, as in v1.57.0
- 7 and 5 built-in reference self-tests, with the standard input and output checks, and 68 independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (36 and 32; exact `fractions.Fraction` arithmetic; 2,619 in all, the earlier 2,551 unchanged). One built-in self-test had a wrong hand-calculated determinant (7 instead of 6 for [2 0 1; 1 3 2; 1 1 2]); the self-test caught it and the expected value was corrected, the engine was right. All 68 independent vectors matched the engine on the first run
- No new guide: the calculators are mapped in `tools/calc_articles.js` to `kirchhoffs-laws`, `thevenin-norton-formulas` and `complex-number-formulas` (matrix) and to `trigonometry-formulas`, `complex-number-formulas` and `ac-circuit-formulas` (vector); 152 formula articles and 292 pages in `converters/` unchanged. The engineering mathematics hub lists both tools and its text names them; the router test sends "matrix calculator", "inverse matrix", "matrix determinant", "3x3 matrix", "vector calculator", "dot product", "cross product", "angle between two vectors" and "vector magnitude" to them (router test 169 of 169 first, 169 of 169 in the top three)

Blank pairs are ignored; a pair with only one value entered is rejected, as are fewer than two points and x values that are all equal (the slope is not defined). Warnings: only two points (the line is exact, r² is 1, the standard error is a dash), level y values (r is not defined), and a chosen x outside the entered points (extrapolation). Values are limited to ±1e12

## v1.60.0
- First Division 04 (Geometry) batch in division 37 Engineering Mathematics: `pythagorean-theorem` (Pythagorean Theorem right-triangle calculator), `triangle-calculator` (Triangle Calculator from three sides) and `circle-calculator` (Circle Calculator). 147 calculators across 34 divisions
- Pythagorean Theorem: any two of a, b (legs) and c (hypotenuse); the blank one is found. Also gives both acute angles (atan2), the area and the perimeter. Entering all three, fewer than two, a side that is zero or negative, or a hypotenuse not longer than the leg entered with it is rejected. Sides are limited to 1e12
- Triangle Calculator: the three sides of any triangle give the three angles (law of cosines, summing to 180°), the area (Heron's formula in a sorted form that stays accurate for thin triangles), the perimeter and the inscribed (area / s) and circumscribed (abc / 4 area) radii. A zero side, or a longest side that is not shorter than the other two added together (including the flat case), is rejected. A right triangle shows a note pointing to the Pythagorean calculator. Sides are accepted in any order
- Circle Calculator: choose radius, diameter, circumference or area as the known value; gives all four. A value that is zero or negative is rejected. The notes say the area found is that of a solid round conductor of the same diameter, not the copper area of a stranded one
- 8, 7 and 6 built-in reference self-tests, with the standard input and output checks, and 105 independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (38, 35 and 32; 60-digit `decimal` and exact `fractions.Fraction` arithmetic; 2,758 in all, the earlier 2,653 unchanged). ## v1.61.0
- Division 04 (Geometry) continued in division 37 Engineering Mathematics: `area-calculator` (Area Calculator for eight flat shapes) and `volume-surface-area` (Volume and Surface Area Calculator for five solids). 149 calculators across 34 divisions
- Area Calculator: choose a rectangle, triangle (base and height), trapezoid, parallelogram, ellipse, circular sector, ring (annulus) or regular polygon and enter up to three dimensions (the third is used only by the trapezoid). Gives the area, and the perimeter where the dimensions fix it (rectangle, ellipse, sector, ring, regular polygon); the triangle, trapezoid and parallelogram show a dash for the perimeter because their slanted sides are not entered. The ellipse perimeter is found with the arithmetic-geometric mean method. A missing or zero dimension, a ring whose inner radius is not smaller than the outer, a sector angle above 360°, and a polygon whose number of sides is not a whole number from 3 to 1000 are rejected. A dimension the shape does not use is ignored with a note
- Volume and Surface Area Calculator: choose a box, cylinder, sphere, cone or hollow cylinder (pipe). Gives the volume, the total surface area and the curved or side surface area (without the ends). The pipe volume is the wall material and its total surface counts the outer and inner curved surfaces and both ring ends. The notes say one cubic metre is 1000 litres. A missing or zero dimension and a pipe whose inner radius is not smaller than the outer are rejected; an unused dimension is ignored with a note
- 21 and 14 built-in checks including the reference self-tests, and 84 independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (48 and 36; 50-digit `mpmath` with the elliptic integral for the ellipse perimeter; 2,842 in all, the earlier 2,758 unchanged)

## v1.62.0
- Division 04 (Geometry) continued in division 37 Engineering Mathematics: `slope-calculator` (Slope Calculator) and `distance-calculator` (Distance Calculator). 151 calculators across 34 divisions
- Slope Calculator: two points (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) give the slope (rise over run), the grade in percent, the angle from the horizontal (atan2 on the run's sign, so it lies between −90° and 90° and does not depend on the order of the points), the run per unit rise (the N in "1 in N"), the rise, the run, the length along the line and the y-intercept. A vertical line shows the angle (90°), the rise, the run and the length, with a dash for the slope, grade, 1-in-N and intercept, and a note; a level line has slope zero and no 1-in-N figure, with a note. Identical points are rejected. Coordinates are accepted from −1e12 to 1e12 and may be negative. The default is a 1 in 20 fall (0, 0 to 20, 1)
- Distance Calculator: two points in 2D or 3D give the straight-line distance, the horizontal distance, the differences along x, y and z, the midpoint and the angle of the line above the horizontal (negative when point 2 is lower; a dash when the points are the same). A blank z is taken as 0, so leaving both z blank gives the 2D distance, and a note says so if only one z is entered. Identical points give a distance of zero with a note. The notes say the result is the straight-line distance, not a cable route length
- 7 and 6 built-in reference self-tests, with the standard input and output checks, and 68 independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (34 and 34; exact `fractions.Fraction` differences and 50-digit `mpmath`, with `atan` where the engine uses `atan2`; 2,910 in all, the earlier 2,842 unchanged). All 68 matched the engine on the first run. Expected zeros are left out of the vectors because the comparison is relative

## v1.63.0
- Probability, Binary and SI prefix tools from the Engineering Mathematics plan. New calculator `probability-calculator` in division 37, and two Conversion Centre number tools, `binary-calculator` and `si-prefix-converter`. 152 calculators across 34 divisions
- Probability Calculator: two events A and B, independent, mutually exclusive or overlapping (P(A and B) entered), giving P(A and B), P(A or B), P(neither), P(A only), P(B only), the complements, both conditional probabilities and, for n independent trials, P(every time) and P(at least once) (computed with expm1 and log1p so a small P(A) keeps its precision). Inconsistent inputs are rejected (exclusive events adding above 1, overlap outside its bounds, a fractional n)
- 11 built-in cases and 50 independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` from exact `fractions.Fraction` arithmetic (2,960 in all, the earlier 2,910 unchanged). All 50 matched the engine on the first run
- Binary Calculator: add, subtract, multiply, divide (whole quotient and remainder, truncated toward zero), AND, OR, XOR and shifts on whole numbers in binary, octal, decimal or hexadecimal, up to 200 digits per entry, exact BigInt arithmetic. Bitwise operations and shifts need non-negative numbers. Tested against native BigInt literals
- SI Prefix Converter: tera to femto, by moving the decimal point on the digit string (no floating-point residue), with E notation and a best-fit prefix. Tested against value x 10^(from - to) over all 100 prefix pairs and exact text anchors
- Mapped in `tools/calc_articles.js` to `statistics-formulas`, `measurement-uncertainty-formulas` and `percentage-ratio-rounding-formulas` (loose fit; a probability guide would suit better). Hub text and description updated (description kept within 160 characters); the word "undefined" is avoided in page text because the page check rejects it

## v1.64.0
- Permutations, combinations and the binomial distribution, the extension named at the end of v1.63.0. Two new calculators in division 37, `permutations-combinations` and `binomial-distribution`, and one new formula guide, `probability-formulas`. 154 calculators across 34 divisions
- Permutations and Combinations Calculator: n items (1 to 100) and r chosen (0 to 100) give n!, permutations nPr and combinations nCr without repetition, and permutations nʳ and combinations C(n + r − 1, r) with repetition. Counts use exact BigInt arithmetic and are shown to 10 significant figures, with every digit in the steps up to 40 digits. An r above n gives 0 for nPr and nCr with a note, and the repetition figures stay valid. Fractional n or r is rejected
- Binomial Distribution Calculator: n independent trials (1 to 1000), success probability p and k give P(X = k), P(X < k), P(X ≤ k), P(X > k), P(X ≥ k), the mean n p, the variance and the standard deviation. Terms come from sums of logarithms, and each tail is added term by term rather than found as 1 minus the other side, so a small tail keeps its precision. p of 0 or 1 gives a certain outcome with a note. A fractional n or k, and k above n, are rejected
- 12 and 14 built-in cases and 66 independent vectors in `gen_vectors.py` (32 and 34; exact integers from `math.perm` and `math.comb`, and exact `fractions.Fraction` sums for the binomial tails; 3,026 in all, the earlier 2,960 unchanged). All 66 matched the engine on the first run
- New guide `probability-formulas` (Probability, Combinations and Binomial Formulas) with the formulas, three worked examples (two redundant units, counting selections, 20 lamps) and four questions; the three probability tools are mapped to it in `tools/calc_articles.js` ahead of the statistics guide, which answers the loose-fit note from v1.63.0. 153 formula articles and 295 pages in `converters/`. An article test checks the worked figures against the three calculators

## v1.65.0
- Phasor diagram on the Phasor Sum page, the first item named at the end of v1.64.0. The page's "Try your own numbers" result now draws the phasors on the complex plane under the figures: each phasor as an arrow from the origin (P₁, P₂, P₃, in blue, orange and red), a subtracted phasor drawn already negated and labelled with a minus (−P₂), the dashed head-to-tail path through the partial sums, and the resultant R as a heavy arrow with its angle arc and a line reading "Resultant R = … ∠ …°". A phasor with magnitude 0 is left out, as in the calculation. A balanced three-phase set shows the closed triangle and the line "The phasors close into a loop: R = 0", with no resultant arrow
- New drawing function `llPhasorSumSvg` in `tools/build_pages.js`, next to `llPhasorSvg` (complex numbers). Only the Phasor Sum page carries it, and only the complex number page carries `llPhasorSvg`; a test checks that no other calculator page carries either. `llCompute` builds the drawing data (`ph`: number, sign, magnitude, angle and the signed x and y of each phasor; `res`: the engine's real and imaginary result), and `llInit` picks the drawing function by page. The drawing is an SVG using the site's CSS variables (light and dark), with a text alternative that gives every phasor and the resultant, and an equal scale on both axes
- A rounding residue in the resultant (1e-13 of the largest value or smaller) is drawn as exactly zero, so a balanced set never shows a stray arrow or "1.13687e-13". A value that is not finite draws nothing. Zero, huge and tiny phasors give no NaN or Infinity, and every coordinate stays inside the 320 by 260 drawing
- Tests: 11 new checks in `tests/run_tests.js` (form data for the default case, a left-out phasor, a subtracted phasor and the balanced set; the drawing for two phasors, three, a subtraction and one; the zero residue; a value that is not finite; zero, huge and tiny phasors; the coordinate bounds). The earlier check that only the complex number page carries diagram code now covers both pages. No calculator, vector or page count changes: 154 calculators, 3,026 vectors, 516 sitemap URLs
- Division 05 (Number Systems and Engineering Mathematics) was checked against the pages that already exist and needed no new page: Binary Calculator (`converters/binary-calculator.html`), decimal to binary, binary to decimal and hexadecimal (`number-base-converter.html`), engineering notation (the Engineering line of `scientific-notation-converter.html`), scientific and engineering unit prefixes (`si-prefix-converter.html`) and degrees to radians and back (`degrees-to-radians.html`, `radians-to-degrees.html`, `angle-converter.html`). A separate Engineering Notation page would repeat the scientific notation page; it is an optional search-wording choice, not a gap in the tools

- Not built: a separate Basic Calculator (the Scientific Calculator covers it). Not changed: the real-device and accessibility testing, the launch checklist and the reviewer records from the roadmap are still open. The drawing was checked by rendering four cases in headless Chromium and by the tests, not on a real phone

## v1.66.0
- Phasor diagram on the AC Ohm's Law page, the second item named at the end of v1.65.0. The "Try your own numbers" result now draws the voltage V and the current I as arrows from the origin with the angle between them as an arc, and says in one line whether the current lags (inductive), leads (capacitive) or is in phase. The voltage is drawn at full length and the current at 70 percent of it, because volts and amps share no scale, and the drawing says "not to scale". It also works when the voltage or the impedance is the value being solved for. New drawing function `llPhasorVISvg` in `tools/build_pages.js`, carried only by the AC Ohm's Law page; a test now checks that each of the three phasor drawing functions (complex numbers, phasor sum, AC Ohm's law) is on its own page and no other. The "Re" axis label sits below the axis so it never collides with the V label. Tests: 7 new checks (form data for inductive, capacitive, resistive and solved-for-voltage cases; the drawing for lagging, leading and in-phase; a value that is not finite; huge, tiny and wrapped angles; coordinate bounds)
- Basic Calculator, the item the plan rates Critical and that v1.62.0 to v1.65.0 had treated as covered by the Scientific Calculator. New standalone page `calculators/basic-calculator/` (`tools/basiccalc_page.js`, page script `tools/basiccalc_client.js`) on the same expression engine and parser as the Scientific Calculator (Standard mode), so it is covered by the same 756 independent vectors and the same hostile-input tests. A 30-key pad (digits, + − × ÷, brackets, %, ±, square, square root, reciprocal, memory MC, MR, M+, M−, and Ans), a live result, a history of confirmed results that stays in the page, copy result, keyboard entry (Enter confirms, Escape clears, an operator after a confirmed result continues from the answer, a digit starts afresh). A history entry that used Ans or memory is reused as its value, not as the changing name. The page has a worked key table, the order of operations, a percent section, accuracy checks and 8 FAQs, with every example number calculated by the engine at build time. Percent keeps the engine's meaning (divide by 100, so 200+10% is 200.1) and the page says so and gives 200*(1+10%) for 220
- Tests: 11 new checks in `tests/run_tests.js` (26 hand-checked expressions, errors for division by zero, a missing operand and an unclosed bracket, the answer, memory, reciprocal, square and sign keys, the committed page equals the generator output, title and description lengths, the engine and script are inlined unchanged, no external script or address, no dynamic code, storage, network or HTML injection in the script, 30 keys each with an aria-label or plain text, every button action has a handler, labelled field and live region, the percent examples, and the links in and out). The page was also driven in headless Chromium: typing, the keypad, memory, history reuse, errors and keyboard continuation, with no script errors. That pass found two faults that the unit tests cannot see, both fixed before release: the example expression the page opens with was extended by the first keys pressed (5 × 6 became 5*612.5*8+...), so it is now treated like a confirmed result (a digit or typed character starts afresh, an operator continues from its result), and the backspace key did nothing before the field had been focused, so key input now uses the end of the text until the field has a caret
- Registered like the Scientific Calculator: the engineering hub's General tools, the home page's More tools block, a link from the Scientific Calculator, the sitemap (517 URLs: `calculators/` 142), the offline cache list in `sw.js`, the committed `sitemap.xml`, and the counts in ROADMAP.md, DEPLOY.md and the tests. Calculators 154, vectors 3,026 and the registry are unchanged (it is a standalone tool, not a registry calculator)
- Accessibility: axe-core 4.10.2 (WCAG 2.0, 2.1 and 2.2 A and AA plus best-practice) over all 520 HTML pages at 1280 px in light mode found one issue, the 24 px target size (WCAG 2.5.8) of plain text links in lists on 14 Conversion Centre pages (the converters hub and 13 category converters), 19 px tall. Fixed with one shared rule, `main li>a:only-child{display:inline-block;padding:3px 0}`; the rescan of all 520 pages is clean. A second pass on the Basic Calculator, Phasor Sum, AC Ohm's Law, Complex Number, Scientific Calculator, Probability, the calculators hub, the SI prefix converter and the home page, at 390 px and 1100 px in light and dark, was clean, and a check with deliberately broken markup showed the scanner catches missing labels and names. Colour contrast on the Basic Calculator measures 8.9:1 to 15.6:1. Axe cannot decide the contrast of the page header and gradient headings, which stay on the manual list. This is automated scanning only: it finds part of the problems, and the real-device, keyboard and screen-reader checks are still open

## v1.67.0
- Phasor diagram on the Series RLC Impedance page, the item named at the end of v1.66.0. The "Try your own numbers" result now draws the impedance triangle on the complex plane: R along the real axis, the net reactance X = XL − XC along the imaginary axis (up when inductive, down when capacitive), and Z as the arrow from the origin to R + jX with the phase angle as an arc. One scale is used on both axes, so a mostly resistive circuit looks flat. A line under the drawing reads "|Z| = … Ω ∠ …° (inductive, capacitive or resistive)", and the drawing has a text alternative. A reactance below 1e-9 of R is drawn as zero.
- The drawing is `llPhasorRLCSvg` in `tools/build_pages.js`, inlined into the one page that uses it, like the other three phasor drawings. The engine and the registry are unchanged.
- Tests: 9 new checks in `tests/run_tests.js` (only this page carries the code, the values for the defaults, 200 mH and 10 mH and resonance, the drawing for inductive, capacitive and resistive circuits, values that are not finite, huge and tiny values, coordinates inside the drawing, and a negligible reactance).
- Contrast of the page header and headings, the item axe-core cannot judge. Measured from rendered pixels in headless Chromium (light and dark; 1280, 1024, 768 and 390 px; 12 pages; 1,468 header and heading text elements, each against the worst background pixel behind it, with the WCAG thresholds 4.5:1 and 3:1 for large text). Found and fixed: on the app view the header status strip labels (`.tb b`, 10 px, `#FFB066`, 3.4 to 4.3:1) and the PASS text (`.tb .ok`, `#7EE0A4`, 4.3:1) fell short on the lighter right of the header gradient, and the hover colour of the theme button (`.th:hover`) had the same colour. They are now `#FFDDB8` (about 4.8:1) and `#B4F3CC` (about 4.9:1); the strip re-measured with no failures at any width in either theme. Everything else measured at or above the threshold. The check is a pixel sample, not a real-screen review; the first pass also raised false alarms (an underline, a sticky pill and long-page screenshots) that were each re-checked one element at a time.

## v1.67.1
Pre-publication engineering check (independent re-derivation of about 50 calculators against textbook formulas, extreme-input fuzzing of all 154, and a read of the standards figures quoted in the guides). One engineering fault found and fixed, two wording fixes.
- **Short-circuit withstand k for large PVC cables (fixed, was non-conservative).** IEC 60364-4-43 Table 43A, IEC 60364-5-54 Table A.54.4 and BS 7671 Table 43.1 give PVC conductors above 300 mm² a lower k (copper 103, aluminium 68, final temperature 140 °C instead of 160 °C). `cable-sc-withstand` used 115 and 76 at every size, so a PVC cable needing more than 300 mm² came out too small. It now finds the area with the up-to-300 mm² k and, when that is above 300 mm², recomputes with the lower k. The Feeder Designer let-through check now takes k from the chosen cable area for the same reason. XLPE is unchanged. Example: 40 kA for 1 s, copper PVC, now needs 388 mm² (400 mm² standard) with k = 103, where 348 mm² (400 mm² standard, but the margin was wrong) was shown before.
- **Independent vectors.** The generator draws the same 30 cable-sc-withstand cases as before, so every other vector is unchanged (3,026 in total); the expected values for the 10 cases above 300 mm² were rewritten from the table. Four hand-written self-tests added.
- **Guides.** The formula guides now say PVC k falls to 103 and 68 above 300 mm².
- **Brightness step guidance.** The lamp power exponent hint now says a tungsten filament is nearer 2.5 to 2.7 and that the default of 2 over-states the low-step load (the safe side). Default unchanged.
- **Wording.** Mean, Median and Mode with one reading said "The 1 reading have"; now "has".
- Version and service-worker cache 1.67.1. `ROADMAP.md`, `REVIEW_PACK.md`, `API.md` and the generated pages regenerated.

## v1.68.0
Scientific Calculator worksheet, the optional feature recommended in the v1.67.1 technical review.
- **Worksheet card.** Add to worksheet (next to Copy result) keeps the confirmed or current result with its expression, the mode, angle unit, significant digits, the unit label in Engineering mode, the values of any variables the expression uses, and the polar or rectangular form of a complex result. Each entry has an optional note (300 characters) and a Remove button; the worksheet has a title and a Prepared by field and holds at most 50 entries. An error result is refused, and so is an unconfirmed random number, because the value shown would not be the value kept.
- **Output.** Print or save as PDF builds a plain black-on-white table (number, expression, result, settings, note) with the date and a limits line, hides the rest of the page while printing and removes the sheet afterwards; the PDF is made by the browser's own Save as PDF, so no library and no network request is added. Copy as text gives the same content as plain text.
- **Privacy.** The worksheet is held in memory only, like the history, and is gone when the page closes. Notes and titles are written with `textContent`, never as HTML. The existing test that forbids storage, network calls and dynamic code in the engine and page script still passes.
- Tests: 5 new checks in step 26, now 79 (page carries the worksheet card and its buttons, the print rules, the FAQ and privacy wording, text-only writing of notes, the entry cap and print clean-up; the existing check that the script has no storage still applies). Behaviour was also exercised in a simulated browser (jsdom, outside the repo): entries, variables, complex results, notes, escaping, print and clean-up, refusal of errors and random numbers, the 50-entry cap, clear.

## v1.74.0
- First calculator built from the Technical Library audit (`Elecmetric_Calculator_Audit_vs_Technical_Articles.xlsx`, Build list, P1, article TL-020 "Superposition Theorem: Method, Conditions and Examples"). New calculator `superposition-theorem` (Superposition Theorem Calculator) in division 04 DC Circuits: two voltage sources each behind a series resistor, one current source and a load resistor. Each source is switched on alone (other voltage source shorted, current source opened), the load voltage and current are the sum, the load power is taken from the total voltage with the non-additive sum of powers shown for comparison, the source currents are given (a negative one means that source absorbs power), and the result is checked against a direct node-voltage solution.
- 44 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` written from the node-voltage method in exact fractions (each source's contribution solved with the others set to zero, a different route from the engine's resistor-divider formulas; the generator also asserts that the three contributions add to the total exactly). 25 built-in self-tests. Library card TL-020 now links to the calculator (`TOOL_CALC` in `tools/build_library.js`).

## v1.75.0
- Second calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-025 "Magnetic Circuit Calculator"). New calculator `magnetic-circuit` in division 04 DC Circuits: series magnetic circuit of a constant-permeability core and an optional air gap with a fringing factor. Gives core, gap and total reluctance, MMF split between core and gap, flux, flux density and field strength in each part, inductance L = N²/R and stored energy; warns above 1.6 T in the core (saturation). Gap flux density, field and MMF are shown as no value when there is no gap.
- 35 independent vectors (`tests/gen_vectors.py`; MMF drops found by the series share F × R_part / R_total, a different route from the engine's H × l) and 24 built-in self-tests. Library card TL-025 links to the calculator. Guides linked: solenoid inductance, transformer EMF, inductors, magnetic flux density converter. Stage 3 screen: Not applicable (scope screen only). Not added to `tools/team_review_approval.json` (see v1.74.0).
- Bumped engine and service-worker cache to 1.75.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.76.0
- Third calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-033 "DC Motor and Generator: Construction and Operating Principles"). New calculator `dc-machine-emf-torque` in division 12 Motors (kept there so the division count does not change): EMF and torque of a DC machine from poles, total armature conductors, lap or wave winding (A = P or A = 2) and flux per pole. Motor mode takes the terminal voltage and armature current and gives the back EMF Ea = V − Ia Ra − Vb, the speed N = 60 A Ea / (Φ Z P) and the torque Te = (P Z / (2π A)) Φ Ia; generator mode takes the speed and gives Ea = Φ Z N P / (60 A) and the terminal voltage V = Ea − Ia Ra − Vb. Also gives the developed power Ea Ia, the armature copper loss, the brush loss, the terminal power and the armature-circuit efficiency (field, core, friction and windage losses are not included, and the page says so). A warning appears when the armature and brush drop exceeds 15% of the reference voltage; a motor voltage or generator EMF that does not exceed the armature drops is rejected; an odd number of poles or conductors is rejected; a motor without a voltage or a generator without a speed is rejected.
- 45 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` written in exact fractions from the textbook relations (own random stream, so the earlier 3,193 are unchanged). The route differs from the engine: torque is found from the power balance Te = Ea Ia / ω rather than from the torque constant, the motor speed is the exact ratio Ea / (EMF per rpm), the efficiency comes from the loss balance, and the generator and motor power balances (Ea Ia = V Ia + Ia² Ra + Vb Ia) are asserted in the generator. 8 built-in self-tests. Library card TL-033 now links to the calculator (`TOOL_CALC` in `tools/build_library.js`). Guides linked: motors, motor efficiency, motor temperature rise, transformer EMF. Stage 3 screen: Not applicable (textbook relations, scope screen only, not a clause review).
- Counts: 160 calculators, 3,238 vectors, 132 calculator pages (113 generated), 525 sitemap URLs. The calculator is NOT yet in `tools/team_review_approval.json` (see v1.74.0); that one coverage check still fails by design until the site owner extends the designation.
- Bumped engine and service-worker cache to 1.76.0. No existing calculation formulas or numerical results changed.

## v1.77.0
- Fourth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-041 "Transformer Open-Circuit and Short-Circuit Tests"). New calculator `transformer-test-data` in division 13 Transformers. From the open-circuit readings (LV side) it gives the no-load power factor, the core-loss and magnetising currents and the core-loss resistance Rc and magnetising reactance Xm referred to the HV side; from the short-circuit readings (HV side) it gives the series R, X and Z per phase, the same on rating in per cent, and the copper loss at rated current (scaled from the test current by the square of the current ratio). At a chosen load, power factor and lagging or leading it gives the exact phasor voltage regulation and the efficiency, and the load and value of maximum efficiency. Single-phase or three-phase (equivalent star, line readings, total power).
- Warnings: open-circuit test voltage more than 5% from rated; short-circuit test current more than 20% from rated; impedance above 25% on rating. Rejected: open-circuit or short-circuit power not less than the apparent power of the test. Core loss is the measured value (not corrected for test voltage) and winding resistance is not corrected to a reference temperature; both are stated on the page.
- 39 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 3,238 unchanged), by a different route from the engine: the magnetising branch from the admittance triangle (Y₀, G, B), the series branch from the short-circuit power triangle (Q = √(S² − P²), X = Q / I²), regulation from a complex phasor sum, efficiency from the loss sum. 5 built-in self-tests. Library card TL-041 links to the calculator. Stage 3 screen: Not applicable (scope screen only). Not added to `tools/team_review_approval.json` (see v1.74.0).
- Counts: 161 calculators, 3,277 vectors, 133 calculator pages, 526 sitemap URLs. Bumped engine and service-worker cache to 1.77.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.78.0
- Fifth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-050 "Load Flow Analysis: Gauss-Seidel vs Newton-Raphson"). New calculator `load-flow-3bus` in division 19 Power Systems (an existing division, so the division count does not change): a three-bus network of a slack bus, a load (PQ) bus 2 and a bus 3 that is either a load (PQ) or a generator holding its voltage (PV), joined by three series R + jX lines in per unit on a chosen MVA base. Gives the voltage and angle at buses 2 and 3, the slack-bus real and reactive output, the bus 3 reactive output for a PV bus, the real and reactive flow at the sending end of each line, the total line loss and the final power mismatch. A method switch runs either Newton-Raphson (polar, analytic Jacobian, mismatch limit 1e-10 pu) or Gauss-Seidel (plain, no acceleration factor, voltage change limit 1e-11 pu), and the iteration count is shown so the two can be compared (default case: 4 against 17).
- Warnings: a bus voltage outside 0.95 to 1.05 pu; the slack bus absorbing real power. Rejected: a line with zero resistance and zero reactance, a PV bus without a voltage setpoint, a PQ bus without Q, and any case where the iteration does not converge (a load beyond the network capability, voltage collapse). No line charging, off-nominal tap, bus shunt or reactive limit is modelled; the page says so.
- 42 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 3,277 unchanged) by a route with no iterative solver on the reference side: the bus voltages are chosen first, the injections follow from the bus admittance matrix (S = V conj(Y V)) and are fed to the engine as the loads, so the engine must iterate back to the chosen voltages; line flows come from branch currents (Vi - Vj) / z. 9 built-in reference tests were checked against a separate rectangular-coordinate `fsolve` solution.
- Library card TL-050 links to the calculator. Guides linked: per-unit, transmission line, three-phase power, complex numbers. Stage 3 screen: Not applicable (textbook network analysis, no governing standard for the calculation); this is a scope screen, not a clause review. The calculator is NOT yet in `tools/team_review_approval.json` (see v1.74.0); that one coverage check still fails by design until the site owner extends the designation.
- Counts: 162 calculators, 3,319 vectors, 134 calculator pages, 527 sitemap URLs. Bumped engine and service-worker cache to 1.78.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.79.0
- Sixth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-051 "Symmetrical Components and Fault Analysis"). New calculator `symmetrical-components` in division 19 Power Systems (an existing division, so the division count does not change), with three modes. Phase to sequence: three phase phasors (magnitude and angle, volts or amps) are resolved into the zero-, positive- and negative-sequence components of phase a, with the negative- and zero-sequence unbalance factors |V2| / |V1| and |V0| / |V1| and a round-trip check that rebuilds the phases. Sequence to phase: the phase phasors are rebuilt from given sequence components. Fault currents: from the line-to-line voltage and the complex sequence impedances Z1, Z2 and Z0 (each as R and X in ohms), the bolted three-phase, single line-to-ground (phase a), line-to-line (b-c) and double line-to-ground (b-c-g) fault currents, with the phase b and c currents and the earth return current 3 I0 of the double line-to-ground fault. The operator is a = 1∠120° and phase order is a-b-c.
- Warnings: negative-sequence unbalance above 2% (a common screening level, not a requirement of one standard); a negative-sequence component larger than the positive (reversed phase order); zero sequence above 2% of the positive; a zero positive-sequence component; and a single line-to-ground fault larger than the three-phase fault (Z0 smaller than Z1). Rejected: all three magnitudes zero, a missing input for the selected mode, and a zero positive- or negative-sequence impedance. Not modelled: fault impedance, prefault load, motor contribution, IEC 60909 voltage factors, mutual coupling between sequences; the page says so.
- 44 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 3,319 unchanged) with no sequence-network closed form on the reference side: for the phase and sequence modes the sequence components are chosen first and the phases follow from the transformation matrix, and for the fault mode the boundary conditions are written in the phase domain (Zabc = A diag(Z0, Z1, Z2) A^-1) and the three currents and three voltages are found by one linear solve. 15 built-in self-tests were cross-checked against the same phase-domain solve.
- Library card TL-051 links to the calculator. Guides linked: short-circuit, three-phase power, complex numbers, per-unit. Stage 3 screen: Not applicable (textbook power-system analysis; IEC 60909 and ANSI/IEEE C37 short-circuit procedures are not applied); this is a scope screen, not a clause review. The calculator is NOT yet in `tools/team_review_approval.json` (see v1.74.0); that one coverage check still fails by design until the site owner extends the designation.
- Counts: 163 calculators, 3,363 vectors, 135 calculator pages, 528 sitemap URLs. Bumped engine and service-worker cache to 1.79.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.80.0
- Seventh calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-092 "Rectifiers, Inverters and DC-DC Converters"). New calculator `power-electronics-converter` (title "Power Converter Calculator") in division 24 Semiconductor Components (an existing division, so the division count does not change). The id `power-converter` was already taken by the Power Unit Converter, so the new calculator uses a different id and page URL. Three modes. Rectifier: ideal-diode, resistive-load single-phase half-wave and bridge and three-phase half-wave and bridge circuits, giving the peak, average and RMS output voltage, DC current and power, rectification efficiency, form factor, ripple factor, ripple frequency, peak inverse voltage and the average and RMS current per diode, with an optional smoothing capacitor (output voltage, load current and peak-to-peak ripple from the straight-line discharge model, solved in closed form so the load current is consistent with the lower output voltage). Inverter: single-phase square wave, single-phase sine PWM, three-phase six-step, three-phase sine PWM and three-phase PWM with third-harmonic injection or space-vector, giving the fundamental line-to-line and phase RMS voltage, the total RMS and the distortion (square-wave and six-step only, where they are exact), the DC bus utilisation and the volts per hertz. DC-DC converter: buck, boost and buck-boost in continuous conduction, giving the ideal duty cycle, average and peak inductor current, ripple, the inductance and output capacitance needed, the switch and diode off-state voltage, the input current and the loss from a stated efficiency.
- Warnings: capacitor ripple above 20% of the peak voltage; PWM modulation index close to its linear limit; duty cycle above 85% or below 10%. Rejected: a sine-PWM index above 1.0, a third-harmonic or space-vector index above 2/√3, a buck converter that does not step down, a boost converter that does not step up, an inductor ripple above 200% (discontinuous conduction), and a missing input for the mode selected. Not modelled: diode forward drop, source impedance, ESR, dead time, overmodulation, carrier harmonics, discontinuous conduction, controlled (thyristor) rectifiers; the page says so.
- 89 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 3,363 unchanged) with no closed form on the reference side: the rectifier output waveform is built from the phase voltages (maximum, or maximum minus minimum for the bridge) and its average, RMS and one diode's current are integrated numerically with mpmath, the capacitor case is solved by bisection on the voltage balance; the inverter fundamental and total RMS come from numerical Fourier integration of the switched pole and line waveforms (PWM fundamentals from complex pole phasors); the DC-DC results come from inductor volt-second and capacitor charge balance in exact fractions (the buck inductance from the on-interval slope and the buck capacitor from the area of the charge triangle). The engine agreed with all 89 on the first run. 45 built-in self-tests (23 cases written for this calculator plus the generic ones).
- Library card TL-092 links to the calculator. Guides linked: bridge rectifiers, rectifier ripple, buck and boost converters, soft starters and VFDs. Stage 3 screen: Not applicable (textbook power-electronics relations; harmonic limits such as IEC 61000-3-2 and converter product standards are not applied); this is a scope screen, not a clause review. The calculator is NOT yet in `tools/team_review_approval.json` (see v1.74.0); that one coverage check still fails by design until the site owner extends the designation.
- Counts: 164 calculators, 3,452 vectors, 136 calculator pages, 529 sitemap URLs. Bumped engine and service-worker cache to 1.80.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.80.1
- **Fix: three-phase bridge rectifier in `power-electronics-converter` was 1.73 times too high.** The hint, assumptions and formula text all define the supply voltage for the bridge as the RMS line-to-line voltage, but the code used Vm = √3 × √2 × Vs, which treats it as a phase voltage. 400 V line-to-line gave Vdc = 935.6 V; the correct value is 3√2 × 400 / π = 540.19 V (1.3505 × VLL). Vm, Vdc, Vrms, DC current and power, PIV, diode currents and the capacitor-filter results for that circuit were all affected, and so was the one-line summary. The single-phase and three-phase half-wave circuits, the inverter and the DC-DC modes were not affected. Vm is now √2 × Vs for every circuit, and the three-phase summary says line-to-line (bridge) or per phase (half-wave).
- The independent generator had the same error (`pc_rect` in `tests/gen_vectors.py`, although its own comment said Vm is the line-to-line peak), so the 8 three-phase-bridge vectors in `tests/reference_vectors.json` agreed with the wrong engine. They were regenerated after the fix (the only 8 of 3,452 that changed). The built-in test was corrected to 540.19 V, with expected values from an independent mpmath integration of the max-minus-min bridge waveform (Vdc 540.1897897, Vrms 540.6652707, ripple 4.196661%, Ida 18.00633 A, Idr 31.21532 A).
- Test "team-level reviewer designation" no longer claims the designation covers every calculator. It now checks that the designation lists only real calculators and that its count matches its list. The designation (12 November 2026) still names the 157 calculators it named then; the seven added since are not covered until the user directs it.
- STANDARDS.md: review to-do updated to 164 calculators; note added that the 157 is the v1.73.0 count.
- Counts: 164 calculators, 3,452 vectors, 136 calculator pages, 529 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.80.1.

- Replaced the cancelled FAA AC 150/5345-47B with 47C (22 July 2011) in the review records of `agl-isolating-transformer` and `agl-lamp-failure-load`, and in the applicability review.

## v1.81.0
- Eighth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-143 "Industrial Plant Electrical Distribution: From Utility to Loads", tool "Plant Load Flow Planner"). New calculator `plant-load-flow` in division 29 Industrial Electrical (an existing division, so the division count does not change). It follows real and reactive power from the loads back to the utility. Inputs: plant bus and utility voltage, main transformer rating, impedance, full-load copper loss, no-load loss and no-load current; up to four load groups (connected kW, demand factor, power factor; a group at 0 kW is unused); a coincidence factor between the groups; the capacitor bank on the plant bus; a target power factor at the utility. Outputs: each group's demand in kW and kVA; the plant demand in kW and kvar and its power factor before capacitors; the net kvar, kVA, power factor and line current at the plant bus; the transformer loading, spare capacity, real and reactive loss; the real, reactive and apparent power, power factor and line current at the utility; and the extra capacitor still needed for the target power factor. The groups add as complex power, so their power factors combine correctly; the coincidence factor scales both kW and kvar.
- Transformer loss model: iron loss and magnetising reactive power constant (Q0 = √((i0 St)² - P0²)), copper loss and leakage reactive power scaling with the square of the loading (Qz = √((uk St)² - Pcu²)). The extra-capacitor figure is worked at the present loading, so it errs slightly high (a larger bank trims the transformer reactive loss a little). Warnings: transformer above 100% of its rating, or above 80% (little growth room; the page says about 70 to 80% is a common planning level and the specification governs); a capacitor bank larger than the reactive demand (leading power factor); utility power factor below 0.9. Rejected: every group at zero load, a copper loss at or above the impedance voltage (Pcu ≥ uk × St), a no-load loss above the no-load apparent power. Not modelled: cable and busbar losses, voltage drop, harmonics, motor starting, unbalance, capacitor steps; the page says so.
- 42 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 3,456 unchanged) by a different formulation from the engine's: group power from the power angle, complex addition, and the transformer as a per-unit equivalent circuit (series r + jx carrying the load current, shunt g + jb at 1 pu), instead of the engine's P0 + x²Pcu and Q0 + x²Qz expressions. Both sides rest on the same modelling assumptions (square-law copper loss, constant iron loss); the vectors check the arithmetic and the algebra, not the assumptions. 5 built-in self-tests (default case, an overloaded transformer, a leading power factor, and two rejections), with expected values from the same independent route.
- Library card TL-143 links to the calculator. Guides linked: demand and load factor, power factor, transformer efficiency, transformer formulas. Stage 3 screen: Not applicable (textbook plant load estimation; demand and coincidence factors, utility power-factor limits and transformer loading limits come from the project specification, supply agreement or code and are not applied); this is a scope screen, not a clause review. The calculator is NOT in `tools/team_review_approval.json`, which still lists the 157 designated on 12 November 2026.
- Counts: 165 calculators, 3,498 vectors, 137 calculator pages, 530 sitemap URLs. Bumped engine and service-worker cache to 1.81.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.81.1
- Ninth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-016 "Thevenin and Norton Equivalent Circuits", tool "Thevenin-Norton Equivalent Calculator"). New calculator `thevenin-norton` in division 04 DC Circuits (an existing division, so the division count does not change). Two ways in: a divider circuit (source Vs, series R1, shunt R2, series R3 to the load terminals) or a measured open-circuit voltage and short-circuit current. Gives the Thevenin voltage and resistance, the Norton current, the load current, voltage and power for a load resistor RL, the maximum power the terminals can deliver (Vth² / 4 Rth, at RL = Rth) and the share of the equivalent source power that reaches the load. Resistive circuits only; the page says an AC network with reactive parts needs the phasor calculators and that the equivalent says nothing about the inside of the original network.
- Warnings: load equal to Rth (maximum-power point, half the power lost in the source); a zero load (short circuit). Rejected: measured mode without Voc or Isc, Isc of zero, Voc of zero, Voc and Isc of opposite sign; divider mode with R1 and R2 both zero, or a zero equivalent resistance (R3 zero and R1 or R2 zero).
- 40 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 3,498 unchanged). Divider case: the whole circuit with its load is solved by nodal analysis in exact fractions; Voc from the same circuit with the load open, Isc with the load shorted, Rth = Voc / Isc, and the maximum power by a numerical search over RL on the real circuit (not the closed form). Measured case: the Norton source Isc in parallel with G = Isc / Voc driving RL. The engine agreed with all 40 on the first run. 9 built-in self-tests.
- Library card TL-016 links to the calculator. Guides linked: Thevenin and Norton, Kirchhoff's laws, Ohm's law, resistors. Stage 3 screen: Not applicable (textbook circuit theorem); this is a scope screen, not a clause review. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json` (it still lists the 157 designated on 12 November 2026).
- Counts: 166 calculators, 3,538 vectors, 138 calculator pages, 531 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.81.1. No existing results changed.

## v1.82.0
- Tenth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-042 "Three-Phase Transformer Connections and Vector Groups", tool "Transformer Vector Group Reference"). New calculator `transformer-vector-group` in division 13 Transformers (an existing division, so the division count does not change). Pick one of nine groups (Dyn11, Dyn1, Dyn5, Yd1, Yd11, Yy0, Yy6, Dd0, Dd6), enter the high-voltage and low-voltage line voltages and the kVA rating. Gives the line voltage ratio, the turns ratio (the winding voltage ratio, which differs from the line ratio when one side is delta and the other star), the winding voltage and the line and winding current on each side, the clock number and the phase lag of the low-voltage side (clock number x 30 degrees). An optional second group gives the phase difference between the two groups and the voltage across an open paralleling link (2 sin(difference / 2) as a percentage of the low-voltage line voltage).
- Warnings: two groups that differ in phase displacement (do not parallel, with the link voltage); a star-star group with no delta winding (no path for third-harmonic and zero-sequence current); a high-voltage value below the low-voltage value (step-up check). Rejected: a zero or negative voltage or rating. Zigzag groups (Yzn, Dzn) are not included, because their turns ratio needs a separate definition.
- 61 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 3,538 unchanged): all nine groups at random voltages and ratings, 27 comparisons between groups and 7 named pairs. Written from phasors and per-phase power rather than the engine's formulas: winding voltage from the line-voltage phasors (star Van = (Vab - Vca) / 3), winding current from per-phase power (S / 3) / Vw, line current from S = sqrt(3) V I, and the phase difference and link voltage from the two low-side phasors |VL1 - VL2|. The engine agreed with all of them once the vector inputs were given in the calculator's own default units (kV and kVA). 7 built-in self-tests.
- Library card TL-042 links to the calculator. Guides linked: transformer vector groups, parallel transformers, transformers, three-phase power. Stage 3: Scope screened against IEC 60076-1:2011 Ed 3.0 (the document that defines the connection symbols and clock-number notation); this is a scope screen from public catalogue information, not a clause review. The earlier calculators in this series were screened Not applicable; this one is not, because a standard does define its notation. The registry now has 51 scope screened plus 116 Not applicable = 167. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json` (it still lists the 157 designated on 12 November 2026). The hard-coded Stage 3 test now expects 51.
- Counts: 167 calculators, 3,599 vectors, 139 calculator pages, 532 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.82.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.83.0
- Eleventh calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-058 "Overcurrent, Differential and Distance Protection Relay Setting Calculator", tool "Relay Setting Calculator"). New calculator `relay-setting` in division 16 Protection (an existing division, so the division count does not change). Covers the overcurrent relay (50/51) only: differential and distance settings are not included, and the page says so. Enter the curve (IEC 60255-151 standard, very, extremely or long-time inverse), maximum load current, CT primary and secondary, a pick-up factor, the maximum fault at the relay, the minimum fault at the end of the zone, the operating time wanted at the maximum fault, the fault at the next device downstream and a high-set factor. Gives the pick-up (primary, relay secondary and plug setting as a percentage of the CT rating), the plug setting multiplier, the exact TMS, the nearest 0.025 TMS and the time it really gives, the sensitivity (Ifmin / Is) and operating time at the minimum fault, and the high-set pick-up (primary and secondary). The pick-up factor, required time and high-set factor are the user's design inputs, not chosen by the calculator.
- Warnings: load current above the CT primary; maximum fault above 20 times the CT primary (saturation check); plug setting outside 50% to 200%; minimum fault not above the pick-up (relay does not operate, no time shown) or below 1.5 times it; exact TMS outside 0.025 to 1.5; high-set not below the maximum fault. Rejected: a maximum fault at or below the pick-up (no TMS exists), a pick-up or high-set factor below 1.
- 46 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier vectors unchanged): 40 random and 6 named, all four curves. Worked at 40 digits (mpmath) from the IEC operating equation; the TMS is found by root-finding on t(TMS) = target rather than by rearranging the formula, and the 0.025 setting uses exact decimal half-up rounding. One named case reproduces the guide (pick-up 100 A, fault 500 A, TMS 0.2: 0.8559 s). The engine agreed with all 46 on the first run. 8 built-in self-tests.
- Library card TL-058 links to the calculator. Guides linked: overcurrent relay curves, CT burden, circuit breakers, short-circuit level. Stage 3: Scope screened against IEC 60255-151:2009 Ed 1.0 (public catalogue information, not a clause review). The registry now has 52 scope screened plus 116 Not applicable = 168; the hard-coded Stage 3 test now expects 52. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 168 calculators, 3,645 vectors, 140 calculator pages, 533 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.83.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.84.0
- Twelfth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-060 "Overcurrent Relay Coordination and Grading", tool "Relay Coordination Calculator"). New calculator `relay-coordination` in division 16 Protection (an existing division, so the division count does not change). It grades one upstream IEC inverse-time overcurrent relay against one downstream relay at one grading fault current and checks one lower fault current. Enter the downstream relay (curve, pick-up, TMS), the upstream curve and pick-up, the fault current through both relays, a lower fault current and the grading margin. Gives the downstream operating time, the time the upstream relay must have (downstream time plus margin), the exact upstream TMS, the upstream TMS to set (the next 0.025 above it, so the margin is never below the one asked for), the upstream time and margin you really get, and the downstream and upstream times and the margin at the lower fault. The grading margin, the downstream settings and the pick-ups are the user's design inputs, not chosen by the calculator.
- Warnings: upstream pick-up below the downstream pick-up; TMS (downstream or exact upstream) outside 0.025 to 1.5; lower fault above the grading fault; lower fault not above the downstream pick-up (nothing to grade); lower fault not above the upstream pick-up (no back-up there); margin at the lower fault below the one asked for, or the upstream relay not slower than the downstream one. Rejected: a grading fault at or below either pick-up (no TMS exists).
- Scope: the overcurrent inverse-time element only, with no instantaneous, earth-fault, directional or differential stage, no transformer or infeed between the relays, no CT saturation or burden check, and no time-current chart. The page says so.
- 46 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier vectors unchanged): 40 random and 6 named, all four curves on both relays. Worked at 40 digits (mpmath) from the IEC operating equation; the upstream TMS is found by root-finding on t_up(TMS) = t_down + margin rather than by rearranging the formula, and the 0.025 setting uses exact decimal ceiling. The engine agreed with all 46 on the first run. 8 built-in self-tests.
- Library card TL-060 links to the calculator. Guides linked: overcurrent relay curves, CT burden, circuit breakers, short-circuit level. Stage 3: Scope screened against IEC 60255-151:2009 Ed 1.0 (public catalogue information, not a clause review), the same standard as `relay-setting`. The registry now has 53 scope screened plus 116 Not applicable = 169; the hard-coded Stage 3 test now expects 53. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 169 calculators, 3,691 vectors, 141 calculator pages, 534 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.84.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.85.0
- Thirteenth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-080 "CT Burden Calculator"). New calculator `ct-burden` in division 16 Protection (an existing division, so the division count does not change). Enter the CT (rated secondary current 1 A or 5 A, rated burden in VA, rated accuracy limit factor, winding resistance), the lead cable (one-way length, cross-section, resistivity, temperature, out-and-back or balanced three-phase star connection), the relay and meter burdens, contact resistance, and the maximum fault current against the CT primary rating. Gives the lead loop resistance, the relay and meter burdens as resistance, the total burden in ohms and VA, the share of the rated burden used and the margin, the CT internal burden, the actual accuracy limit factor at that burden (ALF′ = ALF × (Sn + Sct) / (S + Sct)) against the one the fault needs (Ifmax / Ip), and the longest lead cable at the rated burden.
- Warnings: total burden above the rated burden; total burden below 25% of it (measuring CT range); actual accuracy limit factor below the one the fault needs (saturation risk); fixed burdens alone using all of the rated burden (no lead cable possible). A zero CT primary or rated burden is rejected by the input limits.
- Scope: a screening check only. Measuring classes, knee-point voltage, transient classes (TPS, TPX, TPY, TPZ), DC offset, remanence and thermal and dynamic withstand are not checked, and the page says so. The burden is treated as a resistance at power factor 1.
- 48 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier vectors unchanged): 40 random (both copper and aluminium, 1 A and 5 A, both connections, four temperatures) and 8 named, including the two worked examples of the CT burden guide (19.2 VA on 5 A, 0.768 VA on 1 A). Written in exact rational arithmetic; the longest lead cable is proved by putting it back into the burden equation, which must return exactly the rated burden. The engine agreed with all 48 on the first run. 9 built-in self-tests.
- Library card TL-080 links to the calculator. Guides linked: CT burden, conductor resistance, overcurrent relay curves, short-circuit level. Stage 3: Scope screened against IEC 61869-2:2012 Ed 1.0 (public catalogue information, not a clause review). The registry now has 54 scope screened plus 116 Not applicable = 170; the hard-coded Stage 3 test now expects 54. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
