## v1.86.0
- Fourteenth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-137 "Busbar Sizing Calculator"; the audit status was "Guide only"). New calculator `busbar-sizing` in division 29 Industrial Electrical (an existing division, so the division count does not change). Enter the material (copper or aluminium), the load current, a design current density, the width and thickness of one bar, the fault current, its duration, the material constant k and a working temperature. Gives the cross-section the load needs (I / J), the area of one bar, the bars for the load (rounded up), the cross-section the fault needs (Isc x sqrt(t) / k) and the bars for it, the bars to install (the larger of the two), the installed cross-section, the actual current density, the short-circuit withstand of the installed bars (n x Ab x k / sqrt(t)) and its ratio to the area the fault needs, the mass per metre, and the DC resistance and loss per metre per phase at the working temperature. The current density, k and the working temperature are the user's inputs, not chosen by the calculator; 1.2 A/mm2 and k 115 are only the example values of the formula guide.
- Warnings: the fault, not the load, decides the number of bars; two or more bars in parallel (no derating is applied for the skin and proximity effects, so the arrangement must be checked with the maker or the standard); an actual current density above 3 A/mm2; more than 20 bars needed. Rejected by the input limits: a zero or negative current, density, width, thickness, fault current, duration or k; a fault duration above 5 s; a current density above 10 A/mm2 and a k above 300.
- Scope: a screening calculation. The temperature rise, the derating of bars in parallel, the electromagnetic force on the bars and supports (peak current, support spacing) and the verification of the assembly by test or by comparison with a tested design are not checked, and the page says so. The resistance is the DC value with the same rho and alpha as `ct-burden` (copper 0.017241 ohm mm2/m, aluminium 0.02826 ohm mm2/m) and the densities of the formula guide (8960 and 2700 kg/m3).
- 46 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 3,739 unchanged): 40 random (both materials, nine widths, six thicknesses, five k values, five temperatures) and 6 named, including the worked examples of the busbar guide (2,000 A on 100 x 10 mm copper needs two bars, 246 mm2 for 40 kA for 0.5 s, 8.96 kg/m and 2.7 kg/m). Written in exact rational arithmetic without reading the engine; the number of bars for the fault is the smallest integer n with (n Ab k)^2 >= Isc^2 t, so no irrational number enters the rounding. The engine agreed with all 46 on the first run. 6 built-in self-tests.
- Library card TL-137 links to the calculator. Guides linked: busbar rating, busbar temperature rise, conductor force, short-circuit level. Stage 3: Scope screened against IEC 61439-1:2020 Ed 3.0 (public catalogue information, not a clause review; IEC 60865-1 is not implemented). The registry now has 55 scope screened plus 116 Not applicable = 171; the hard-coded Stage 3 test now expects 55. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 171 calculators, 3,785 vectors, 143 calculator pages, 536 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.86.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.87.0
- Fifteenth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-002 "AC vs DC: Electrical Principles and Practical Applications", tool "AC/DC Quantity Converter"; the audit status was "Partial", with no RMS-versus-DC equivalence converter). New calculator `ac-dc-equivalent` in division 03 AC Circuits (an existing division, so the division count does not change). Pick the waveform (sine, symmetrical square, symmetrical triangle, full-wave rectified sine or half-wave rectified sine), say which value is known (peak, RMS or the average of the absolute value) and enter it with a load resistance. Gives the peak, the RMS (the equivalent DC value that heats a resistor the same), the average, the form factor (RMS / average) and the crest factor (peak / RMS), the RMS current and the power in the load, the power that would wrongly be found by taking the peak as DC and its ratio to the true power (the crest factor squared), and the reading of an average-responding meter scaled for sine waves with its error against the true RMS value.
- Warnings: a rectified waveform (its average is a DC component and its RMS also holds the ripple, so the heat follows the RMS value); an average-responding meter that would be more than 5% in error for the chosen waveform. Rejected by the input limits: a zero or negative value or resistance.
- Scope: ideal waveforms with no distortion or extra DC offset and a purely resistive load. The page says so, and that a distorted or phase-controlled waveform needs a true-RMS meter or the RMS computed from samples.
- 46 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 3,785 unchanged): 40 random (all five waveforms and all three known values) and 6 named, including 325 V peak sine (229.81 V RMS, 5,281 W on 10 ohm), the 11.07% error of an average-responding meter on a square wave and the half-wave rectified average case. Written from the definitions (mean square and mean absolute value over a period); the generator first checks each closed form against a 20,000-sample midpoint integral of the waveform itself to 1e-6. The engine agreed with all 46 on the first run. 6 built-in self-tests.
- Library card TL-002 links to the calculator. Guides linked: RMS waveform, duty-cycle RMS, AC circuit, rectifier bridge. Stage 3: Not applicable (textbook circuit identity, no governing standard): the registry now has 55 scope screened plus 117 Not applicable = 172. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 172 calculators, 3,831 vectors, 144 calculator pages, 537 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.87.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.88.0
- Sixteenth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-015 "KCL and KVL: Complete Circuit Analysis Guide", tool "Kirchhoff Circuit Solver"; the audit status was "Partial", with no circuit-level KCL/KVL solver). New calculator `kirchhoff-solver` in division 04 DC Circuits (an existing division, so the division count does not change). A two-loop network of three branches between the same two nodes, each branch with a resistor and, optionally, a source (E1 and E2 into the top node, E3 in the load branch, normally 0). It writes the current equation (KCL at node A) and the two loop equations (KVL), solves the three branch currents by Cramer's rule, and gives the node voltage, the voltage across each resistor, the power of each source (negative when it is absorbing) and the total resistor power. Default example: 12 V and 9 V sources of 1 ohm each into a 2 ohm load, giving 3.6 A, 0.6 A and 4.2 A and 8.4 V at the node.
- Checks shown with every result: the current sum at node A, the voltage round the outer loop (not used in the solution), and the source power minus the resistor power. Warning: a source that absorbs power (a battery being charged). Rejected by the input limits: a zero or negative resistance, or a source voltage beyond 1e6 V.
- Scope: a linear resistive DC network of exactly this shape. A network with more nodes or meshes needs the matrix or simultaneous-equations calculator, and the page says so.
- 46 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 3,831 unchanged): 40 random (signed sources, a source in the load branch in about a third) and 6 named, including the default case, a battery on charge (12 V / 2 ohm and 6 V / 3 ohm into 6 ohm, 8 V at the node and 4 W absorbed) and a three-source case. Written by the node-voltage route in exact fractions, not by the equations the engine solves. 4 built-in self-tests.
- Library card TL-015 links to the calculator. Guides linked: Kirchhoff's laws, Ohm's law, resistors, Thevenin and Norton. Stage 3: Not applicable (textbook circuit identity, no governing standard): the registry now has 55 scope screened plus 118 Not applicable = 173. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 173 calculators, 3,877 vectors, 145 calculator pages, 538 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.88.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.89.0
- Seventeenth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-017 RLC circuit article, tool "RLC Circuit Calculator"; the audit status was "Partial", with no RLC transient or damping response). New calculator `rlc-transient` in division 03 AC Circuits (an existing division, so the division count does not change). Choose a series RLC circuit with a voltage step (output: capacitor voltage) or a parallel RLC circuit with a current step (output: inductor current), enter R, L, C, the step size and a time. Gives the damping coefficient, natural angular frequency, damping ratio, Q, natural and damped frequency, the critical resistance, the overshoot, the 2% settling time (4 / slowest decay rate) and the output at the chosen time, and says whether the response is underdamped, critically damped or overdamped.
- Warnings: overshoot above 20% (with the peak value); a damping ratio below 0.1 (long ringing). Rejected by the input limits: a zero or negative R, L, C or step size, or a negative time.
- Scope: ideal L and C, one step from rest, the capacitor voltage (series) or inductor current (parallel) only. Winding resistance, capacitor ESR, source impedance and non-linear loads are outside it, and the page says so.
- 50 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 3,877 unchanged): 44 random (series and parallel, damping ratios from 0.05 to 8, times up to six decay constants) and 6 named, including the default case, critical damping and an overdamped case. Written by integrating the differential equation with Runge-Kutta, not from the closed forms the engine uses. 5 built-in self-tests.
- Library card TL-017 links to the calculator. Guides linked: resonance, AC circuits, RC filters. Stage 3: Not applicable (textbook circuit identity, no governing standard): the registry now has 55 scope screened plus 119 Not applicable = 174. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 174 calculators, 3,927 vectors, 146 calculator pages, 539 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.89.0. No existing results changed.


## v1.90.0
- Eighteenth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-019 mesh and nodal analysis article, tool "Mesh / Nodal Analysis Solver"; the audit status was "Partial", with no mesh or nodal network entry form). New calculator `mesh-nodal-solver` in division 04 DC Circuits (an existing division, so the division count does not change). A source with an internal resistance feeds a five-resistor bridge network (R1 A to B, R2 B to D, R3 A to C, R4 C to D, R5 B to C). Choose mesh analysis (three mesh currents) or nodal analysis (three node voltages); the page writes the three equations of that method with the user's numbers, solves them by Cramer's rule and gives the node voltages, the current in every resistor, the current drawn from the source, the resistance seen at the bridge input, the power from the source and the power lost. Default example: 24 V, Rs 1 ohm and 10, 20, 30, 40 and 50 ohm, giving 1.0936 A from the source, 22.906 V at the bridge input and 29.56 mA in R5.
- Checks shown with every result: both methods are always solved and the largest current difference between them is shown; the voltage round the outer loop (E, Rs, R1, R2), which is not one of the equations solved directly; and the source power minus the resistor power. Warnings: a balanced bridge (R1 R4 = R2 R3, so R5 carries no current) and a spread of more than a million to one between the largest and smallest resistance (loss of digits). Rejected by the input limits: a zero or negative resistance, a resistance above 1e9 ohm or a source voltage beyond 1e6 V.
- Scope: a linear resistive DC network of exactly this shape with one source. Rs must be above zero (an ideal source is entered as a very small Rs, and an open middle branch as a very large R5), and a network with a different layout, more sources or reactances is outside it; the page says so and points to the matrix and simultaneous-equations calculators.
- 46 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 3,927 unchanged): 40 random (both methods, source of either sign, resistances from 0.05 to 5,000 ohm) and 6 named, including the default case by both methods, a balanced bridge and a bridge with very unequal resistors. Written with the six branch currents as unknowns in exact fractions (Kirchhoff's current law at three nodes and the voltage law round two inner loops and the source loop, solved by Gaussian elimination), not by the mesh or node equations the engine solves; the generator also asserts that the node-voltage equations hold exactly for each case. The engine agreed with all 46 on the first run. 4 built-in self-tests.
- Library card TL-019 links to the calculator. Guides linked: Kirchhoff's laws, Wheatstone bridge, Thevenin and Norton, resistors. Stage 3: Not applicable (textbook circuit identity, no governing standard): the registry now has 55 scope screened plus 120 Not applicable = 175. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 175 calculators, 3,973 vectors, 147 calculator pages, 540 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.90.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.91.0
- Nineteenth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-039 transformer turns ratio article, tool "Transformer Turns Ratio Calculator"; the audit status was "Partial", with no dedicated turns-ratio / voltage-ratio calculator). New calculator `transformer-turns-ratio` in division 13 Transformers (an existing division, so the division count does not change). Choose single-phase or three-phase, the primary and secondary winding connections (star or delta), the rated line voltages, the kVA rating, the primary turns per phase and a load impedance across one secondary winding. Gives the line voltage ratio, the turns ratio per phase (which is the line ratio only when both sides have the same connection), the secondary turns (exact and rounded to a whole turn, with the ratio error that rounding causes), volts per turn, the rated line and winding currents on both sides, the load impedance referred to a primary winding (a squared times the load) and a kVA balance check. Default example: 11,000/415 V, 1,000 kVA, delta primary and star secondary, 2,295 primary turns: line ratio 26.506, turns ratio 45.907, 49.99 secondary turns, 52.49 A and 1,391 A.
- Warnings: a delta-star (or star-delta) transformer, where the turns ratio differs from the line ratio; a secondary turns count that is not a whole number and shifts the ratio by more than 0.5% when rounded. Rejected by the input limits: a zero or negative voltage, rating, turns or impedance.
- Scope: the ideal transformer. Regulation under load, tappings, phase shift and the vector group are outside it and the page points to the regulation and vector group calculators.
- 46 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 3,973 unchanged): 40 random (single- and three-phase, all four connection pairs) and 6 named, including the default case, a 230/115 V single-phase 2:1 case and delta-delta and star-delta cases. Written from the winding definitions (rating shared by the windings, winding current from winding voltage, line current from the connection), not from the engine's line-current formula. The engine agreed with all 46. 5 built-in self-tests.
- Library card TL-039 links to the calculator. Guides linked: transformer formulas, transformer EMF, vector groups, parallel transformers. Stage 3: Not applicable (textbook identity): the registry now has 55 scope screened plus 121 Not applicable = 176. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 176 calculators, 4,019 vectors, 148 calculator pages, 541 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.91.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.92.0
- Twentieth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-053 "Short-Circuit Current Calculation in Power Systems", tool "Short-Circuit Calculator"; the audit status was "Partial", with a transformer-only fault calculator and no source + transformer + cable impedance build-up). New calculator `short-circuit-impedance` in division 17 Short Circuit (an existing division, so the division count does not change). Enter the system voltage and voltage factor c (1.10, 1.05, 1.00 or 0.95), the upstream network fault level (MVA) and X/R ratio, an optional supply transformer (kVA, uk %, copper loss kW) and a cable run (resistance at 20 °C and reactance in Ω/km, length, number in parallel, copper or aluminium, conductor temperature). It gives the initial symmetrical three-phase short-circuit current at the fault and at the busbar before the cable, the fall caused by the cable, the peak current (IEC 60909 method (a) peak factor from R/X), the line-to-line current, the fault level in MVA, the total R, X, Z and X/R, and the network, transformer and cable impedances.
- Warnings: a voltage factor below 1.05 (a minimum-style figure, not for choosing breaking capacity); a cable conductor above 20 °C (the higher resistance lowers the current). Rejected by the input limits: a zero or negative voltage, fault level, rating, impedance, resistance or length; and, with a transformer, a copper loss whose resistive part Pk / St reaches or exceeds uk.
- Scope: a balanced three-phase bolted fault in a radial network, network, transformer and cable in series at one voltage. The IEC 60909 transformer correction factor KT is not applied (it would raise the maximum current by a few percent for a typical transformer); motor contribution, generators, arc resistance, decay of the current with time and earth-fault (zero-sequence) currents are outside it, and the page says so and points to the fault-type, fault-level and cable withstand calculators.
- 50 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,019 unchanged): 44 random (all four voltage factors, with and without a transformer, 1 to 4 cables in parallel, copper and aluminium, conductor temperatures 0 to 90 °C) and 6 named, including the default case (16.17 kA at the end of one 50 m cable, 22.76 kA at the busbar), a network-only case and a 690 V aluminium case. Written by the per-unit route with complex impedances on a 100 MVA base, not by the ohm-by-ohm sums the engine uses. The engine agreed with all 50 on the first run. 6 built-in self-tests.
- Library card TL-053 links to the calculator. Guides linked: short-circuit level, per-unit, transformer through-fault, cable I²t withstand. Stage 3: scope_screened against IEC 60909-0 in `tools/review_records.json` (the registry now has 56 scope screened plus 121 Not applicable = 177); the standard name and edition string are copied from the existing IEC 60909-0 records and no licensed clause text was reviewed. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 177 calculators, 4,069 vectors, 149 calculator pages, 542 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.92.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.93.0

- Twenty-first calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-067 "Earthing, Grounding and Protective Bonding", tool "Earthing Conductor Sizing Tool"; the audit status was "Partial", with a cable short-circuit withstand calculator but nothing aimed at earthing conductors, mechanical minimums or the phase-conductor table rule). New calculator `earthing-conductor-size` in division 18 Earthing (an existing division, so the division count does not change). Enter the earth fault current, the disconnection time (up to 5 s), the conductor material, insulation and installation (11 choices: copper and aluminium with PVC or XLPE / EPR, separate or in a cable, and bare copper, aluminium and steel), the mechanical-protection case and, optionally, the phase conductor size. It gives the minimum cross-section S = I√t / k, the mechanical minimum, the next IEC 60228 standard size, the material constant k, the current density at that size, the let-through energy I²t and the size by the phase-conductor table rule (same size to 16 mm², 16 mm² to 35 mm², then half).
- Warnings: a bare conductor (the 500 °C or 300 °C final temperature is only for visible runs and restricted areas); a separate PVC conductor above 300 mm²; no mechanical minimum applied to steel; a table-rule size smaller than the fault needs; a mechanical minimum that governs; a conductor in a cable given a separate-conductor minimum, or a separate conductor given none; and a requirement above 630 mm². Rejected by the input limits: a zero or negative current or time, and a time above 5 s.
- Scope: adiabatic heating with published k values (the physical formula reproduces every one within 0.65%). PVC in a cable above 300 mm² uses the 140 °C value (103 copper, 68 aluminium) as the existing cable withstand calculator does. Corrosion, joints, earth-fault loop impedance and heating of a buried conductor are outside it.
- Library card TL-067 links to the calculator. Guides linked: earthing, cable I²t withstand, fault loop and RCD, short-circuit level. Stage 3: scope_screened against IEC 60364-5-54 and IEC 60364-4-43 in `tools/review_records.json`; the edition strings are copied from the existing records and no licensed clause text was reviewed. Added to `tools/calc_scope.json`. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 178 calculators, 4,129 vectors (60 new, from their own stream), 150 calculator pages, 543 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.93.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.94.0

- Twenty-second calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-069 "Electrical Load Estimation and Maximum Demand Calculation", tool "Load Schedule Calculator"; the audit status was "Partial", with a one-line maximum demand calculator and no multi-row schedule). New calculator `load-schedule` in division 29 Industrial Electrical (an existing division, so the division count does not change). A schedule of up to eight load groups: for each row the rated power of one unit, the number of units, the demand factor and the power factor; then the supply voltage and phase, a group diversity factor and a spare margin. It gives the connected load in kW and kVA, the demand of each row, the maximum demand in kW, kVAR and kVA, the overall power factor and overall demand factor, the design load with the margin and the design current. A row with a rated power of 0 is left out.
- Warnings: demand factors and a diversity factor both applied (the reduction may be counted twice); an overall power factor below 0.85; every demand factor equal to 1. Rejected by the input limits: no row with a rated power above zero, a fractional number of units, a demand factor above 1, a power factor outside 0.1 to 1, a diversity factor below 1.
- Scope: balanced loads, lagging power factors, user-entered factors (none built in); motor starting, harmonics and the largest-motor allowance are outside it. Real and reactive demands are summed separately, so the overall power factor comes from the mix of rows.
- Library card TL-069 links to the calculator. Guides linked: demand and load factor, power factor, three-phase power, transformer. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (factors depend on the adopted code and are user-entered; no standard method is implemented), so it is a scope screen to confirm. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 179 calculators, 4,169 vectors (40 new, from their own stream), 151 calculator pages, 544 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.94.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.94.1

- Twenty-third item from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-075 "Protective Earthing, Bonding and Automatic Disconnection", tool "Protective Conductor Calculator"; the audit status was "Partial", with "cable-sc-withstand" as the nearest tool and the gap "no protective-conductor (S = I√t / k) tool aimed at earthing"). That gap was closed in v1.93.0: `earthing-conductor-size` already sizes a protective conductor from S = I√t / k (11 material and installation cases, mechanical minimum, standard size, phase-conductor table rule). Building a second calculator for the same formula would only duplicate it, so no new calculator is added.
- Library card TL-075 now links to `earthing-conductor-size`. The automatic-disconnection half of the article is served by `fault-loop-impedance`, which stays in the calculator's related list. The audit workbook shows TL-075 as Covered and linked.
- Scope note: the article title also covers bonding (main and supplementary equipotential bonding conductor sizing). The calculator does not size bonding conductors by the bonding rules (for example half the largest protective conductor, with minimum and maximum limits); that is a possible later addition and is not claimed.

## v1.94.2

- Twenty-fourth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-079 "Power Measurement Calculator"; the audit status was "Partial", with `three-phase-power` and `power-vi-solver` as the nearest tools and the gap "no power-measurement (meter readings) calculator"). New calculator `power-measurement` in division 32 Testing and Measurement (an existing division, so the division count does not change). From the readings of a voltmeter, an ammeter and a wattmeter, with the voltage and current transformer ratios applied, it gives the primary voltage and current, the apparent, real and reactive power, the power factor and the phase angle. The wattmeter can read the whole load or one phase of a balanced three-phase load (total = 3 × reading). Optional energy meter readings at the start and end of an interval, with a multiplying factor, give the average power from the meter and its difference from the wattmeter in per cent.
- Warnings: wattmeter real power above V × I (impossible; reactive power and angle are then not shown); power factor below 0.3 (wattmeter phase-angle error dominates); energy meter average more than 5% away from the wattmeter. Rejected by the input limits: a one-phase wattmeter reading chosen for a single-phase supply, an end energy reading below the start, and an energy interval with no time.
- Scope: sinusoidal, steady, balanced where a one-phase reading is used; instrument and instrument-transformer errors and burden, harmonics and meter rollover are outside it. The two-wattmeter method is not built in (it has its own audit item, TL-083).
- Library card TL-079 links to the calculator. Guides linked: two-wattmeter, three-phase power, power factor, energy meter multiplier. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook relations; the accuracy classes and test methods of the instrument standards are not implemented), so it is a scope screen to confirm. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 180 calculators, 4,204 vectors (35 new, from their own stream), 152 calculator pages, 545 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.94.2. No existing results changed.

## v1.94.3

- Twenty-fifth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-081 "PT Ratio Calculator"; the audit status was "Partial", with `ratio-calculator` as the nearest tool and the gap "no PT ratio / secondary calculator"). New calculator `pt-ratio` in division 32 Testing and Measurement (an existing division, so the division count does not change). From the rated primary and secondary voltage of a voltage transformer it gives the ratio and converts either way: a primary voltage to the secondary, or a secondary meter reading to the primary, with the primary as a percentage of rated. Optional ratio test voltages give the actual ratio and the ratio error (positive when the secondary is higher than the rated ratio predicts); an optional rated and connected burden give the loading and the secondary current.
- Warnings: primary voltage outside 80% to 120% of rated; ratio error above 1% in size; connected burden above the rated burden or below 25% of it. Rejected by the input limits: only one of the two ratio test voltages entered.
- Scope: rated ratio, the usual ratio-error definition and a simple burden percentage. The accuracy classes, voltage factors, phase displacement and test procedures of the instrument transformer standard are not applied, and a residual (open-delta) winding is not covered. The 80% to 120% and 25% to 100% ranges are common class conditions given for the user to confirm in the project edition.
- Library card TL-081 links to the calculator. Guides linked: transformers, CT burden, energy meter multipliers, measurement uncertainty. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (the instrument transformer accuracy classes are not implemented), so it is a scope screen to confirm. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 181 calculators, 4,233 vectors (29 new, from their own stream), 153 calculator pages, 546 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.94.3. No existing results changed.

## v1.94.4

- Twenty-sixth calculator from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-086 "Diode Circuit Calculator"; the audit status was "Partial", with `led-resistor` and `zener-regulator` as the nearest tools and the gap "no diode circuit (rectifier / clamp / drop) calculator"). New calculator `diode-circuit` in division 24 Semiconductor Components (an existing division, so the division count does not change). Four circuits: a DC series diode and resistor with either a constant forward drop or the Shockley equation (the saturation current is found from the drop at a reference current, the ideality factor and the junction temperature; the result includes the dynamic resistance); a half-wave rectifier and a bridge rectifier on a resistive load with the forward drop included (average and RMS output, ripple factor, peak current, average current per diode, peak inverse voltage, conduction angle, diode loss and efficiency); and a capacitor-diode clamp (DC level, highest and lowest output, peak inverse voltage).
- Warnings: diode off or reverse-biased in the DC circuit; diode drop above 10% of the supply peak in a rectifier; Shockley current more than 10 times the reference current. Rejected by the input limits: a Shockley model with a zero or unreasonably large forward voltage, and an AC supply that is not positive or whose peak does not exceed the diode drop.
- Scope: series resistance, reverse leakage, junction capacitance and breakdown are not modelled; the rectifiers have no smoothing capacitor or source impedance (the Power Converter Calculator covers an ideal rectifier with a capacitor). The clamp assumes an ideal capacitor and no load.
- Engine: `calculate()` now leaves out outputs that a calculator lists in a returned `na` array (not applicable to the chosen circuit). Only `diode-circuit` uses it; no other result changes. The formulas of `power-measurement` and `pt-ratio` showed a literal "\n" on their pages; fixed.
- Library card TL-086 links to the calculator. Guides linked: rectifier bridge, rectifier ripple, zener regulator, LED. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook relations; diode ratings and data-sheet limits are not checked), so it is a scope screen to confirm. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 182 calculators, 4,303 vectors (70 new, from their own stream), 154 calculator pages, 547 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.94.4.

## v1.95.0

- Twenty-seventh item from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-093 "VFD Working Principle and Motor Speed Control", tool "VFD Frequency-Speed Calculator"; the audit status was "Partial", with `motor-slip` and `frequency-period` as the nearest tools and the gap "no V/f and frequency-speed calculator"). New calculator `vfd-speed` in division 12 Motors (an existing division, so the division count does not change). From the motor nameplate (base frequency, voltage, poles, rated speed and rated power) it gives the shaft speed for a chosen drive output frequency, or the frequency needed for a target shaft speed, with the synchronous speed, slip speed and slip, the drive output voltage under V/f control, the volts per hertz, the flux as a percentage of rated, and the torque and power the motor can deliver: the rated torque up to the base frequency (constant torque) and the rated power above it (constant power, field weakening).
- Warnings: a frequency above the base frequency (flux falls, torque falls); above twice the base frequency (mechanical speed and pull-out torque limits); below 30% of the base frequency (cooling of a self-ventilated motor and the low-frequency voltage boost). Rejected by the input limits: an odd pole count, a rated speed at or above the synchronous speed at the base frequency, and a drive frequency at or below the rated slip frequency (the rotor would not turn).
- Scope: open-loop V/f control with the rated slip speed held constant, which is the usual approximation; the real slip at a given load, the low-frequency voltage boost, motor cooling, the drive current limit, pull-out torque and the maximum mechanical speed are outside it, and the page says so. A gearbox or driven-machine speed is not included.
- 41 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,303 unchanged): 34 random (50 and 60 Hz motors with 2 to 12 poles, rated slips of 1% to 6%, frequencies from 15% to 220% of base, targets from 10% to 180% of rated speed, both directions) and 7 named, including the default case (1,170 rpm at 40 Hz, 332 V, 71.46 N·m), the rated point at 50 Hz, a 60 Hz drive on a 50 Hz motor and a 60 Hz 460 V motor. Written by the electrical slip-frequency route (shaft speed = 120 (f - fs) / p with fs the rated slip times the base frequency) and with power as the lesser of rated torque times speed and rated power, not by the slip speed in rpm and the region split the engine uses. The engine agreed with all 41 once a unit slip of my own was fixed (power reaches the calculation in watts, which affected the engine entry, not the vectors). 9 built-in self-tests.
- Library card TL-093 links to the calculator. Guides linked: synchronous speed, soft starter and VFD sizing, inverter-fed motor cables, motor formulas. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook machine relations; the standards for converter-fed motors and adjustable speed drives are not implemented), so it is a scope screen to confirm; the registry now has 57 scope screened plus 126 Not applicable = 183. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 183 calculators, 4,344 vectors (41 new, from their own stream), 155 calculator pages, 548 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.95.0. No existing results changed.

## v1.96.0

- Twenty-ninth item from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-147 "Sensor Range Converter" article, tool "Sensor Range Converter"; the audit status was "Partial", with `current-loop-4-20` and `linear-interpolation` as the nearest tools and the gap "no sensor range converter"). New calculator `sensor-range-converter` in division 31 Instrumentation (an existing division, so the division count does not change). Enter the range low and range high values (0% and 100% of span, any unit, negative or reverse-acting ranges allowed), choose the signal (4-20 mA, 0-20 mA, 0-10 V, 1-5 V, 0-5 V or 2-10 V) and either a process value (to find the signal) or a signal (to find the process value). It gives the percentage of span, the process value, the signal level, the equivalent 4-20 mA loop current for any signal type, and the process units per mA or volt.
- Warnings: a process value outside the sensor range (the signal leaves the normal band); a signal outside the chosen band (the process value is an extrapolation); a 4-20 mA current below 3.8 mA or above 20.5 mA, which is treated as a fault under NAMUR NE43; a zero signal on a zero-based signal type, which cannot be told from a broken wire. Rejected by the input limits: equal range low and range high values.
- Scope: linear transmitters only. Square-root flow, logarithmic and characterised outputs, sensor and transmitter error, loop resistor or burden effects, and the over-range behaviour set in the transmitter itself are outside it, and the scope panel says so.
- 41 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,344 unchanged): 34 random (ranges from -100 to above 600 including reverse-acting ones, all six signal types, both directions, process values and signals from 15% below to 120% of span) and 7 named, including the default case (6.5 on 0 to 10 is 14.4 mA), 12 mA on -50 to 150, 7.5 V on 0-10 V over 0 to 200, a reverse range and a value above range. Written by the straight-line route in exact fractions: the transmitter is the line signal = m x + c through the two end points, a process value gives m PV + c and a signal gives (signal - c) / m, the percentage of span is read off the signal side and the gain is 1 / m, where the engine works with the span fraction. Expected zeros are left out because the comparison is relative.
- Library card TL-147 links to the calculator. Guides linked: 4-20 mA loops, ADC resolution, Pt100 RTDs, thermocouples. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook linear scaling; the NAMUR NE43 fault limits are a recommendation and are not reviewed here), so it is a scope screen to confirm; the registry now has 57 scope screened plus 127 Not applicable = 184. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.

## v1.97.0

- Thirtieth item from the Technical Library audit Build list (P1, TL-174 "Electrical Energy Audits: Data Collection and Loss Identification" article, tool "Energy Audit Calculator"; the audit status was "Partial", with `energy-cost` and `electricity-bill-slabs` as the nearest tools and the gap "no audit calculator (multi-load, baseline)"). This was the last P1 item on the Build list. New calculator `energy-audit` in division 07 Power / Energy (an existing division, so the division count does not change). Enter up to six load groups, each with the rated input power of one unit, the number of units, the average load factor and the operating hours per year (a rated power of 0 leaves a row out), then the metered energy for the audit year, an energy tariff and, optionally, the metered energy of a baseline year. It gives the annual energy of each group, the surveyed total and connected load, the unaccounted energy (metered less surveyed) in kWh and as a share of the metered energy, the average metered demand, the largest user and its share, the cost of the metered, surveyed and unaccounted energy, and the change from the baseline year in kWh and per cent.
- Warnings: surveyed energy above the metered energy (load factors or hours overstated, or a load counted twice); an unaccounted share above 10%, a screening limit used here as a rule of thumb and not taken from a standard. Rejected by the input limits: no row with a rated power above zero, a fractional number of units, a metered energy of zero or below, hours above 8,760 and a load factor above 1.
- Scope: an annual energy balance from a load survey. It does not separate distribution loss from unsurveyed load or survey error, does not model demand charges, time-of-use tariffs or power factor penalties, and the baseline comparison is the plain difference between two metered years, not normalised for production, weather or occupancy. The scope panel says so. Savings measures and payback stay in `led-retrofit-payback` and the other estimators.
- 42 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,385 unchanged): 36 random (one to six rows, power from 0.05 to 250 kW, up to 400 units, load factors 0.2 to 1, hours from 100 to 8,760, metered energy from 80% to 140% of the surveyed total, several tariffs, with and without a baseline) and 6 named, including the default case (918,445 kWh surveyed against 1,000,000 kWh metered, 8.1555% unaccounted), a single row with 20% unaccounted, a surveyed total above the meter and a gap inside 10%. Written in exact fractions from the energy balance and by a different route: each row is turned into an average power n P l first and its yearly energy is that power times its hours, the unaccounted share is 1 - Es / Em (the engine takes (Em - Es) / Em), the average metered demand is Em / 8760 and the largest user is the first row with the greatest energy. Unused rows, the baseline outputs when no baseline is given and any expected zero are left out.
- Library card TL-174 links to the calculator. Guides linked: energy cost, demand and load factor, energy meter multipliers, LEDs. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook energy balance; the 10% limit is a rule of thumb and ISO 50002 is not applied), so it is a scope screen to confirm; the registry now has 57 scope screened plus 128 Not applicable = 185. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.

## v1.97.1

- Thirty-first item from the Technical Library audit Build list and the first P2 item (TL-011 "Derivative and Integral Solver", division Engineering Mathematics; the audit status was "Missing", no calculator and no guide). The engine takes numeric and choice inputs only, so there is no free-text expression input; the calculator covers the four function forms that come up in circuit and signal work instead. New calculator `derivative-integral` in division 37 Engineering Mathematics (existing division, so the division count does not change). Choose a cubic a x³ + b x² + c x + d, a sine a sin(b x + c) + d, an exponential a e^(b x) + d or a power a x^b + d (x above 0, b = -1 gives the logarithm), enter the coefficients, a point and two limits. It gives f, f' and f'' at the point, the definite integral, the mean value over the interval and the integral by Simpson's rule (200 strips) as a check.
- Warnings: the function is positive and negative over the interval (the integral is a signed area); Simpson differs from the exact integral by more than one part in a million. Rejected: equal limits, a zero rate on the sine or exponential, a rate times the largest x above 700 (overflow), and a point or limit at or below 0 for the power function. The scope panel says that sums or products of different forms, RMS (integral of the square) and improper integrals are not covered.
- 45 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,427 unchanged): 38 random across the four forms (including negative and fractional exponents and fast sines) and 7 named, including the default cubic, a half-wave of 2 sin x, an RC decay, 3 x² + 1 and 2/x (the logarithm case). Written without the engine's closed forms: the function is an mpmath expression at 30 digits, derivatives come from numerical differentiation (`mpmath.diff`), the integral from `mpmath.quad`, and the Simpson output from a 200-strip Simpson sum written out in the generator.
- Library card TL-011 links to the calculator. Guides linked: trigonometry, RMS values, capacitor discharge, RC filters (there is no calculus guide). `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. ## v1.97.2

- Thirty-second item from the Technical Library audit Build list and the second P2 item (TL-026 "Induced EMF Calculator", division Electromagnetic Engineering; the audit status was "Missing", no calculator and no guide). New calculator `induced-emf` in division 04 DC Circuits (existing division, as for `magnetic-circuit`, so the division count does not change). Three cases are chosen with one switch: a straight conductor moving across a field (e = B l v sin θ, with the current, force B I l and power e I when it drives a closed loop of resistance R), a coil whose flux density changes from B1 to B2 over a time interval (average e = N ΔΦ / Δt, with ΔΦ and the rate of change of flux), and a coil rotating in a uniform field (ω, frequency, peak Em = N B A ω, rms Em / √2 and the instantaneous EMF at a chosen angle from the neutral plane).
- Warnings: a moving conductor within about 5° of the field direction (almost no flux cut); no change of flux between the initial and final flux density. Rejected: an angle outside 0° to 180° for the conductor, a flux density beyond ±100 T, a non-positive time interval, length, speed, resistance, area or rotation speed. The scope text says that the changing-flux EMF is an average, the coil is in a uniform field at right angles to its plane, a rotating coil has two poles, and saturation, leakage flux and self-induction are not included. Angles are entered in degrees as plain numbers because the engine has no angle unit type for calculator inputs.
- 45 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,472 unchanged): 12 random moving-conductor cases, 12 changing-flux cases, 12 rotating-coil cases and 9 named, including the default, a conductor at 30° and a field reversal. Written without the engine's closed forms: the motional EMF comes from the cross product of the velocity and field vectors, the force from the cross product I (l × B) and the power from its dot product with the velocity; the changing-flux EMF is minus the numerical derivative (`mpmath.diff`) of the flux linkage N A B(t); the rotating-coil EMF is minus the numerical derivative of N B A cos ω t, with the peak found at a quarter period and the rms from `mpmath.quad` of e² over one period.
- Library card TL-026 links to the calculator. Guides linked: transformer EMF, solenoid inductance, RMS waveforms, generators. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook electromagnetic induction), a scope screen to confirm; the registry now has 57 scope screened plus 130 Not applicable = 187. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 187 calculators, 4,517 vectors (45 new, from their own stream), 159 calculator pages, 552 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.97.2. No existing results changed.

## v1.97.3

- Thirty-third item from the Technical Library audit Build list and the third P2 item (TL-034 "Alternator Working Principle, EMF Equation and Regulation", division Electrical Machines; the audit status was "Missing", no calculator and no guide). New calculator `alternator-emf` in division 14 Generators (existing division, so the division count does not change). Two cases are chosen with one switch. The first gives the EMF from the winding: poles and speed give the frequency, the slots per pole per phase and the coil span give the slot angle, the chording angle, the pitch factor Kp = cos (α / 2), the distribution factor Kd = sin (q γ / 2) / (q sin (γ / 2)) and the winding factor, and E = 4.44 Kp Kd f Φ T is found per phase and between lines for a star or a delta winding (the exact constant √2 π is used, so a hand answer with the rounded 4.44 is about 0.06% lower). The second gives the no-load EMF, load angle, regulation and output power by the synchronous impedance method from the terminal line voltage, line current, power factor (lagging or leading), Ra and Xs per phase.
- Warnings: a frequency that is not 50, 60 or 400 Hz; a coil short-pitched by more than 60 electrical degrees; a negative regulation (leading load, terminal voltage rises on load); an armature resistance larger than the synchronous reactance. Rejected: an odd or fractional number of poles, a fractional number of slots per pole per phase, and a coil span that is not a whole number from 1 to the slots per pole. The scope text says that the winding is a three-phase integral-slot winding with a sinusoidal flux, that harmonics, skew and fractional-slot windings are not included, and that the synchronous impedance method ignores saturation and salient-pole effects and is pessimistic for a saturated machine.
- 46 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,517 unchanged): 23 for the winding EMF (2 to 24 poles, 1 to 8 slots per pole per phase, spans from full pitch down by up to four slots, star and delta) and 23 for the regulation (415 V to 22 kV, lagging and leading, Ra from 0, star and delta), including the default cases, a full-pitch winding, a concentrated winding, Ra = 0 and a leading load. Written without the engine's closed forms: the winding factor is summed slot by slot from conductor EMF phasors, the EMF comes from Faraday's law by numerical differentiation of the flux linkage (`mpmath.diff`) with the rms from `mpmath.quad`, and the regulation is found from polar phasors added as complex numbers.
- 17 built-in self-tests with expected values computed separately in Python (`mpmath`, 30 digits) from the same phasor routes; the engine agreed with all of them and with all 46 vectors at the first run.
- Library card TL-034 links to the calculator. Guides linked: generators, synchronous speed, power angle, generator droop. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook synchronous-machine theory; IEC 60034-4 covers the test methods and is not applied), a scope screen to confirm; the registry now has 57 scope screened plus 131 Not applicable = 188. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Page title: the registry title is too long for a 60-character title tag, so `tools/calc_pages_auto.js` has a short name, "Alternator EMF and Regulation".

## v1.97.4

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-095 "PWM Inverters: Modulation, Harmonics and Output Voltage", division Power Electronics; the audit status was "Missing"). New calculator `pwm-inverter` in division 24 Semiconductor Components (existing division, so the division count does not change). The circuit is chosen from a single-phase half bridge, a single-phase full bridge with bipolar switching, a single-phase full bridge with unipolar switching and a three-phase bridge; the reference wave is sinusoidal, or third-harmonic injection / space-vector PWM on the three-phase bridge. It gives the frequency modulation ratio mf = fc / f1, the linear range of the modulation index (1, or 2 / sqrt 3 with injection), the fundamental output voltage (peak, RMS, line to line and the RMS at the end of the linear range), the carrier harmonics at mf, mf +- 2, 2mf +- 1, 2mf +- 3, 3mf and 3mf +- 2 as a percentage of the fundamental (naturally sampled sinusoidal PWM, from the double Fourier series with Bessel functions; amplitude / fundamental = 4 / (pi m ma) J_n(m pi ma / 2) when m + n is odd, zero for odd n only on unipolar switching and zero for n a multiple of 3 on the three-phase line voltage), the distortion of those groups, and, for a series R-L load per phase, the fundamental and harmonic current, the total current and the load power.
- Warnings: a fractional mf; a whole mf that is not odd (not an odd multiple of 3 on the three-phase bridge); mf below 9, where carrier groups overlap in frequency. Rejected: a modulation index above the linear range (overmodulation is not covered), injection on a single-phase circuit, a carrier frequency not above the output frequency, a non-positive DC voltage, frequency or load resistance. The harmonic and distortion outputs show a dash with injection, because the sideband sizes differ and are not calculated; the scope text also lists dead time, switching loss, DC bus ripple and the groups above 3mf as not covered.
- 51 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,563 unchanged): 9 each for the half bridge, bipolar, unipolar and three-phase sinusoidal cases, 7 for three-phase injection (fundamental only) and 8 named, including the default and the published ma = 1 table. Written without the engine's Bessel formula: the real PWM waveform is built from exact switching instants and its Fourier coefficients are integrated exactly, so the cancellation rules and the published single-phase table (60.1, 31.8, 18.1, 21.2% at ma = 1) come out of the waveform. Random vectors use whole mf of 27 and above at 1e-8; low-mf named cases use 1e-6. The engine agreed with every vector at the first run, after the vectors were corrected to give the inductance in mH.
- 17 built-in self-tests with expected values from the same waveform reference, including the warnings and the rejections.
- Library card TL-095 links to the calculator. Guides linked: harmonics, RMS waveforms, soft starters and VFDs, inverter-fed motor cables. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook power-electronics theory), a scope screen to confirm; the registry now has 57 scope screened plus 132 Not applicable = 189. ## v1.97.5

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-101 "First- and Second-Order System Response and Time-Domain Specs", division Control Systems; the audit status was "Missing"). New calculator `second-order-response` in division 03 AC Circuits (existing division, next to `rlc-transient`, so the division count does not change). The system is the standard G(s) = K wn^2 / (s^2 + 2 z wn s + wn^2) driven by a unit step, and it is given in one of three ways: the damping ratio and natural frequency (with the DC gain), the percent overshoot and peak time wanted (zeta = -ln (OS / 100) / sqrt (pi^2 + ln^2 (OS / 100)), wd = pi / tp, wn = wd / sqrt (1 - zeta^2)), or the coefficients of b0 / (s^2 + a1 s + a0) (wn = sqrt a0, zeta = a1 / (2 wn), K = b0 / a0). It gives the poles, the damped frequency, the percent overshoot, the peak time, the 10% to 90% rise time, the 2% and 5% settling times, the textbook 4 / (zeta wn) beside the exact 2% settling time (underdamped only), and the final and peak values.
- Rise and settling times are solved exactly from the step response, not from the usual approximations: the underdamped settling time is found on the decaying envelope between the peaks that bracket the band, and the overdamped and critically damped times by bisection on the monotone rise. Warning: a damping ratio below 0.1. Rejected: zeta or wn of 0 or less (an undamped system never settles), an overshoot of 0 or 100% and above, and a1, a0 or b0 of 0 or less (a1 of 0 or less is not stable). Overshoot, peak time and peak value show a dash for critically damped and overdamped systems. The scope text says that a zero in the numerator, higher-order poles, delay and saturation are not included and that this is a time-domain calculator only.
- 51 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,614 unchanged): 30 random zeta and wn (0.06 to 8 and 0.5 to 500 rad/s) with a gain, 8 from overshoot and peak time, 8 from coefficients and 5 named. Written without the engine's closed forms: the system is propagated as a state-space matrix exponential (one formula for every damping ratio), the peak is the root of the derivative, and the rise and settling times are scanned on a fine grid and refined with `brentq`; the overshoot case finds zeta as the root of exp(-pi z / sqrt(1 - z^2)) = OS / 100. Tolerance 1e-6. The generator now needs numpy and scipy for this block.
- 14 built-in self-tests with expected values from the same reference, including the warning and the rejections.
- Library card TL-101 links to the calculator. Guides linked: resonance, RC filters, thermal time constants, motor acceleration time. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook control theory), a scope screen to confirm; the registry now has 57 scope screened plus 133 Not applicable = 190. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Page title: `tools/calc_pages_auto.js` has a short name, "Second-Order Step Response".
- Counts: 190 calculators, 4,665 vectors, 162 calculator pages, 555 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.97.5. No existing results changed.

## v1.97.6

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-102 "Control System Stability: Poles, Zeros and Routh-Hurwitz", division Control Systems; the audit status was "Missing"). New calculator `routh-hurwitz` in division 03 AC Circuits (existing division, so the division count does not change). It takes a characteristic polynomial of degree 2 to 6 with real coefficients (a6 to a0; the degree is chosen with a switch) and builds the Routh array in exact arithmetic (the coefficients are read as decimal fractions with BigInt rationals, so a zero in the table is a true zero). It gives the number of roots in the right half plane (the sign changes in the first column), on the imaginary axis and in the left half plane, a stable / marginally stable / unstable verdict, the degree of the auxiliary polynomial when there is a zero row, and the first-column entries.
- Special cases: a zero row is replaced by the derivative of the auxiliary polynomial (roots on the imaginary axis = degree of the auxiliary polynomial - 2 x the sign changes from the auxiliary row down); a zero first entry with the rest of its row not zero is replaced by a small positive epsilon (exactly 10^-80 in rational arithmetic, up to three times), and the first-column values from that row on, which depend on epsilon, are not shown. Warnings: not every coefficient has the same sign and is non-zero (the system cannot be stable); a second all-zero row (repeated roots on the imaginary axis, unstable but not counted: all counts show a dash). Rejected: a zero leading coefficient for the chosen degree. The scope text says that the characteristic equation is not formed from a block diagram, that a gain range is not found and that polynomials above degree 6 or with complex coefficients are not covered.
- 51 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,665 unchanged): 45 random polynomials of degree 2 to 6 (products of real roots, complex pairs, imaginary pairs with distinct frequencies and at most one root at 0, with an integer leading coefficient of either sign) and 6 named. The counts are taken from the roots of the polynomial (`numpy.roots`), not from a table, and the first column is built with exact Fractions and compared only when the table has no special case. The first run found one failure that the checks of this release then fixed: a zero first entry cannot be removed by multiplying by s + a when the polynomial has a mirror pair of real roots (here 2 and -2), so the first version of the engine, which used that alternative, gave no answer; it now uses the epsilon method. The generator now also needs numpy for this block (scipy was already needed).
- 14 built-in self-tests (numpy-root reference for the counts, exact fractions for the first column), including the mirror-pair case, a zero row, a root at 0, a zero first entry, a second zero row and the rejection of a zero leading coefficient.
- Library card TL-102 links to the calculator. Guides linked: RC filters, resonance, AC circuits. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook control theory), a scope screen to confirm; the registry now has 57 scope screened plus 134 Not applicable = 191. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Page title: `tools/calc_pages_auto.js` has a short name, "Routh-Hurwitz Stability".
- Counts: 191 calculators, 4,716 vectors, 163 calculator pages, 556 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.97.6. No existing results changed.

## v1.97.7

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-106 "Ladder Logic Fundamentals: Contacts, Coils and Timers", division Industrial Automation; the audit status was "Missing"). New calculator `ladder-logic-simulator` in division 29 Industrial Electrical (existing division, so the division count does not change). It evaluates one scan of the standard motor start/stop ladder: rung 1 is the seal-in circuit (START and the holding contact in parallel, in series with the normally closed STOP and the normally closed overload contact) driving the motor contactor, and rung 2 is a TON (on-delay) or TOF (off-delay) timer enabled by the contactor. The inputs are the timer type, START, STOP, the overload, the state of the holding contact from the previous scan, the timer preset, the time the timer has been running and the PLC scan time. It gives the contactor state, the timer enable, the accumulated time, the done bit that drives the delayed output, the time left, the percent complete and the number of scans in the preset.
- Behaviour: STOP and the overload win over START (a warning says so when START is pressed with either), a held-in contactor drops out when STOP or the overload opens, a time above the preset is limited to the preset, and a preset shorter than one scan is flagged. Rejected: a preset or scan time of zero or below, and a negative running time. The scope text says that only this rung pair is evaluated (no other rungs, counters, one-shot or retentive timers), that the timer is continuous (no time base, contact bounce or scan jitter) and that a safety stop is a hardwired circuit, not a PLC rung.
- 78 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,716 unchanged): all 16 combinations of the four contact bits for both timer types (32), 40 random cases (presets 0.1 to 20 s, running times from none to 1.4 times the preset, scan times 0.5 to 50 ms) and 6 named. Written without the engine's Boolean expression: rung 1 is a wiring network and the coil is energised when a breadth-first search finds a path of closed contacts from the left rail; the timers are a tick-by-tick state machine in whole milliseconds; the scan count is an exact Fraction ceiling. The engine agreed with every vector at the first run.
- 15 built-in self-tests, including the seal-in, STOP over START, a tripped overload, the TOF hold and drop-out, a time above the preset, the rounding up of scans and the three rejections.
- Library card TL-106 links to the calculator. Guides linked: contactor sizing, relay coils, motor starting, soft starters and VFDs. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook relay and PLC ladder logic; IEC 61131-3 defines the languages and timer blocks but no clause is applied), a scope screen to confirm; the registry now has 57 scope screened plus 135 Not applicable = 192. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.

## v1.97.8

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-154 "Fourier Series and Harmonic Analysis", division Communication & Signal Systems; the audit status was "Missing"). New calculator `fourier-series` in division 03 AC Circuits (existing division, so the division count does not change). It takes a waveform (square, triangle, sawtooth, half-wave rectified sine, full-wave rectified sine, or a rectangular pulse train with a duty cycle), the peak amplitude and frequency, a harmonic number n to inspect, a number of harmonics N for a partial sum and a time. It gives the DC value, the RMS and AC RMS values, the fundamental amplitude, the cosine and sine coefficients and the amplitude of harmonic n, that harmonic as a percentage of the fundamental, the total harmonic distortion over all harmonics, the share of the AC power in harmonics 1 to N, and the partial sum at the chosen time against the exact waveform value and their difference.
- Conventions: the square, triangle and sawtooth start at 0 at t = 0, the pulse is centred on t = 0, the half-wave rectified sine starts at its zero crossing, and the full-wave rectified sine is described with the frequency of its own output (so its fundamental is at twice the mains frequency). The exact value at a jump is the midpoint of the jump. Warnings: a waveform with jumps overshoots by about 9% of the jump in a partial sum (Gibbs), and a harmonic that is absent by symmetry or by the duty cycle is flagged. Rejected: an amplitude or frequency of zero or below, a duty cycle of 0% or 100% for the pulse train, and a harmonic number or number of harmonics that is not a whole number in range.
- 58 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,794 unchanged): 8 random cases for each of the six waveforms and 10 named. Written without the engine's closed forms: the coefficients come from numerical integration of the piecewise waveform (`mpmath.quad` at 30 digits, split at the breakpoints), THD, power share and partial sum are built from them, and the exact value is the piecewise function. The engine agreed with every vector at the first run, after the engine was corrected to give an exact zero for a pulse harmonic whose sine factor is zero (a rounding residue of 8e-17 had been returned by the first version).
- 14 built-in self-tests, including the textbook THD values (square 48.34%, triangle 12.12%, sawtooth 80.31%, half-wave 43.52%), an absent harmonic, the midpoint at a jump and the rejections.
- Library card TL-154 links to the calculator. Guides linked: harmonics, RMS waveforms, rectifier ripple, AC circuits. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook Fourier analysis; harmonic limits are not applied), a scope screen to confirm; the registry now has 57 scope screened plus 136 Not applicable = 193. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.

## v1.97.9

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-182 "Lighting Uniformity Calculator", division Lighting Engineering; the audit status was "Missing"). New calculator `lighting-uniformity` in division 28 Lighting (existing division, so the division count does not change). It takes up to 12 illuminance readings in lux, the number of points actually used (2 to 12; later fields are ignored), the required average illuminance and the required uniformity Uo. It gives the average, minimum and maximum illuminance, Uo = Emin / Eavg, U1 = Emin / Emax, Emax / Eavg, the population standard deviation and coefficient of variation, the number of the lowest and highest point (the first one if two tie), whether the average and the uniformity meet the requirement (1 or 0), the lux the lowest point would need for the required Uo, and the shortfall.
- Conventions: Uo is Emin / Eavg and the mean is the simple mean of the points (no area weighting); the standard deviation divides by n, not n − 1. The required average and uniformity are the user's own figures: no lighting standard is looked up. Warnings: average below the requirement, uniformity below the requirement (with the lowest point and its shortfall), a reading of 0 lux, and non-zero readings beyond point n that are ignored. Rejected: a number of points that is not a whole number from 2 to 12, a negative reading among the points used, all used readings at 0, a required average of 0 or below, and a required uniformity of 0 or below or above 1.
- 30 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,852 unchanged): 24 random cases (2 to 12 points, readings to two decimals, junk beyond point n) and 6 named (exact tie at a limit, equal readings, two points, a point at zero, twelve points, uneven readings). Written from the definitions in exact fractions (`fractions.Fraction`), with the square root and the 100 factor taken last, and the pass flags decided by exact comparison. The engine agreed with every vector at the first run.
- 17 built-in self-tests, including the default (Eavg 432.5 lux, Uo 0.8092), a uniformity failure with a 140 lux shortfall, an average failure, ties, an ignored tail, a zero reading and nine rejections.
- Library card TL-182 links to the calculator. Guides linked: lighting, point illuminance, illumination converter. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook statistics of readings; required values are entered by the user), a scope screen to confirm; the registry now has 57 scope screened plus 137 Not applicable = 194. ## v1.97.10

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-035 "Synchronous Motor Calculator", division Electrical Machines; the audit status was "Guide only", with the power-angle guide). New calculator `synchronous-motor` in division 12 Motors (existing division, so the division count does not change). It takes the electrical input power, terminal line voltage, poles, frequency, power factor with its type (leading or lagging), armature resistance and synchronous reactance of a three-phase cylindrical-rotor machine. It gives the synchronous speed, phase voltage, line current, apparent power, reactive power (positive taken, negative supplied), the back EMF per phase and line equivalent and its ratio to the terminal voltage, the load angle, the armature copper loss, the developed power and torque, the pull-out power at the same excitation with its angle, and the pull-out margin.
- Conventions: the back EMF is the phasor V − Ia (Ra + jXs) with the current at +φ (leading) or −φ (lagging); the developed power is the input less copper loss, so core, friction and windage and field power are not taken off. Pull-out is Pd at δ = atan(Xs / Ra) with the excitation held, Pmax = 3 Eb (V − Eb cos θ) / Zs, which is 3 V Eb / Xs when Ra = 0. Warnings: a load angle past the pull-out angle (unstable side), copper loss taking most of the input, and a leading power factor with a back EMF below the terminal voltage. Rejected: input power, voltage or frequency of 0 or below, an odd or out-of-range pole count, a power factor outside (0, 1], a negative Ra, an Xs of 0 or below, and a copper loss as large as the input.
- 33 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,882 unchanged): 30 random cases (415 V to 11 kV, 2 to 24 poles, 16.7 to 60 Hz, leading and lagging, reactance scaled to the machine) and 3 named. Written without the engine's closed forms: the back EMF is a `cmath` phasor, the developed power is checked two ways (input less copper loss, and 3 Re(Eb conj Ia)), and the pull-out power is found by scanning the power-angle curve numerically and refining by golden-section search rather than by the closed form at δ = θ. The vector file holds the units shown (kW, kVA, kvar). The engine agreed with every vector.
- 12 built-in self-tests, including the default (back EMF 5,276 V = 1.385 × V at 25.0°, pull-out 2.34 × the load), a lagging case, Ra = 0 (pull-out at 90° and 3 V Eb / Xs), a 60 Hz 4-pole machine at 1800 rpm, an unstable-side warning and six rejections.
- Library card TL-035 links to the calculator. Guides linked: power angle, synchronous speed, generators, motors. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry (salient poles, saturation, field current and losses are not covered). Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook machine theory; IEC 60034 is not applied), a scope screen to confirm; the registry now has 57 scope screened plus 138 Not applicable = 195. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.

## v1.97.11

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-045 "Transformer Parallel Operation Checker", division Transformers; the audit status was "Guide only", with the parallel-transformer load sharing guide). New calculator `transformer-parallel` in division 13 Transformers (existing division, so the division count does not change). It takes two three-phase transformers: the vector group of each (the nine groups of the vector group calculator), kVA rating, percentage impedance on its own rating and X/R ratio, the difference in no-load secondary voltage (-20% to 20%), and the total load with its lagging power factor. It gives the phase displacement difference, the impedance difference and rating ratio, the simple rating over impedance share of unit 1, the kVA, kW, kvar and percentage loading each unit carries, the circulating current (in kVA at rated voltage and as a percentage of each rating), the largest total load before a unit reaches 100%, that load as a percentage of the two ratings added, the unit that is full first and the headroom over the present load.
- Conventions: each unit is a series impedance (Sn / z at the angle -atan(X/R), in kVA at one per-unit voltage) and the load current is fixed at the lagging power factor, so the shares are complex current division, with the circulating current (E1 - E2) Y1 Y2 / (Y1 + Y2) added to unit 1 and taken off unit 2. The loading is a unit's current over its rated current with the busbar voltage taken as rated; real and reactive power are referred to E1. The default (1,000 kVA 5% with 630 kVA 4.5%, 1,400 kVA, equal X/R) reproduces the worked example of the parallel-transformer guide: 823.5 kVA and 576.5 kVA, 82.35% and 91.50%, and a largest load of 1,530 kVA. The largest load is the smallest S with |S u + c| = Sn for each unit, u the load share coefficient and c the circulating current; it is 0 when the circulating current alone fills a unit. Warnings: a phase displacement difference (do not connect, with the circulating current as a percentage of the smaller unit), a ratio difference above 0.5%, impedances more than 10% apart (of the lower), ratings more than 3 to 1 apart, a unit above 100%, and a circulating current that fills a unit. The 0.5%, 10% and 3 to 1 limits are common practice guidelines, not standard clauses, and are labelled so in the assumptions. Rejected: a rating of 0 or below, an impedance of 0 or below or above 100%, an X/R of 0 or below, a voltage difference outside -20% to 20%, a load of 0 or below and a power factor outside (0, 1].
- 45 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,915 unchanged): 36 random cases and 9 named (the guide example, equal units, different X/R, a 2% ratio difference, Dyn11 against Dyn1, an overload, a 20% ratio difference, Yd11 with Dyn11 at unity power factor, Yy0 with Dd0). Written without the engine's admittances: each unit is a physical impedance in ohms per phase, the busbar voltage comes from the nodal equation and each current from (Ei - Vb) / Zi, and the largest load is found by bisection on the loading. The engine agreed with every vector at the first run.
- 15 built-in self-tests, taken from the named vectors, including the guide example, equal units, a 2% ratio difference (200 kVA of circulating current in 1,000 kVA units, 20%), a 60 degree phase mismatch (10 times rated current), an overload, a circulating current that fills a unit, and seven rejections.
- Library card TL-045 links to the calculator. Guides linked: parallel transformers, transformer vector groups, transformers, three-phase power. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry (polarity and phase sequence, three or more units, cable impedance between each unit and the busbar, the magnetising branch, taps and protection are not covered). Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook parallel-operation theory; IEC 60076-1 tolerances and utility paralleling rules are not applied), a scope screen to confirm; the registry now has 57 scope screened plus 139 Not applicable = 196. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Page title: `tools/calc_pages_auto.js` has a short name, "Transformer Parallel Operation".

## v1.97.12

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-046 "Transformer Inrush Estimator", division Transformers; the audit status was "Guide only", with the transformer inrush formula guide). New calculator `transformer-inrush` in division 13 Transformers (existing division, so the division count does not change). It takes the three-phase rating in kVA, the line voltage of the winding being energised, the inrush multiple k of rated current, a decay time constant, a time after energising to check and an instantaneous overcurrent setting as a primary current. It gives the rated current, the rated peak (rated current times the square root of 2), the peak inrush (k times rated current) and its multiple of the rated peak, the time for the peak to fall to the rated peak, the peak left after the chosen time, and the setting as a multiple of the peak inrush and of rated current.
- Conventions: the default (1,000 kVA at 11 kV, k = 10) reproduces the worked example of the formula guide (52.49 A rated, 524.9 A peak, 74.23 A rated peak, 7.071 times). The decay is one exponential, Ipeak(t) = Ipeak e^(-t/tau), and the time to the rated peak is tau ln(k / sqrt 2), 0 when k is below sqrt 2. The setting check compares the pickup with the peak value; an RMS-measuring or second-harmonic-restrained relay can ride through a peak above its setting and that is not modelled. Warnings: k below sqrt 2 (not an inrush), k above the 6 to 12 usual for distribution transformers (a common practice guide, labelled so in the assumptions) and a setting below the peak inrush. Rejected: a rating, voltage, time constant or setting of 0 or below, k of 0 or below or above 40, a time constant above 60 s and a negative time.
- 38 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 4,960 unchanged): 30 random cases (50 to 10,000 kVA, 230 V to 132 kV, k 2 to 20) and 8 named. Written without the engine's closed forms: the current is taken per phase (S/3 at VL / sqrt 3), the rated peak is found by sampling one cycle of the sinusoid, the decay is worked from the half-life and the time to the rated peak is found by bisection. The engine agreed with every vector.
- 16 built-in self-tests: the guide example, k of 6 and 12, a larger unit with a slower decay, a setting below the peak, k above the usual range, k below sqrt 2, time 0, a small unit, and seven rejections.
- Library card TL-046 links to the calculator. Guides linked: transformer inrush, transformers, overcurrent relay, capacitor switching inrush. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry (the multiple and time constant are user data; real decay shape, harmonics, RMS relay response, sympathetic inrush and supply impedance are not covered). Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook energisation theory; IEC 60076 and relay or fuse manufacturer inrush data are not applied), a scope screen to confirm; the registry now has 57 scope screened plus 140 Not applicable = 197. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Page title: `tools/calc_pages_auto.js` has a short name, "Transformer Inrush".
