## v1.98.9

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-090 "Power Supply Ripple Calculator", article "Linear Power Supplies: Rectification, Filtering and Regulation", division Electronics Engineering; audit status "Guide only": the static guide `converters/rectifier-ripple-formulas.html` exists, no interactive calculator). New calculator `power-supply-ripple` in division 23 Electronics beside `zener-regulator` and `led-resistor` (existing division, so the division count does not change). It follows the guide's formulas for a capacitor-filtered rectifier feeding a constant DC load: half-wave, centre-tap full-wave or bridge; peak Vp = Vs x sqrt 2 less one or two diode drops; ripple frequency f (half-wave) or 2f (full-wave); peak-to-peak ripple I / (fr C); lowest voltage Vp - dV; average Vp - dV / 2; RMS ripple dV / (2 sqrt 3); ripple factor; and the capacitor for a ripple target I / (fr dV). An optional linear regulator (output, dropout, ripple rejection in dB) gives the headroom of the lowest input over output plus dropout and the RMS ripple left at the output. Warnings: ripple above 20% of the peak (the constant-current formula is then a rough estimate) and a lowest voltage short of output plus dropout. Rejected: diode drops at or above the peak, and a capacitor so small that the ripple would equal the peak.
- Default: bridge, 12 V rms, 50 Hz, 0.7 V drops, 0.5 A, 4,700 uF, 0.5 V ripple target, 12 V regulator with 2 V dropout and 60 dB rejection. It reproduces the guide's worked example: peak 15.571 V, ripple 1.064 V peak to peak at 100 Hz, minimum 14.507 V, average 15.039 V, RMS ripple 0.3071 V (ripple factor 2.04%), 10,000 uF for 0.5 V, headroom 0.507 V and 0.307 mV rms at the regulator output.
- 40 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 6,402 unchanged), 34 random and 6 named. Written as a different route from the closed forms: the peak comes from the RMS by integrating (A sin wt)^2 over a cycle, the ripple frequency is the number of charging peaks per mains cycle counted on the rectified waveform, the capacitor voltage is followed as a linear fall from Vp at I / C volts per second over one ripple period, the lowest and average voltage and the RMS ripple come from mpmath.quad over that period, the capacitor for a target is found by bisection on the end-of-period drop, and the rejection is exp(-rr ln 10 / 20). Random cases keep the ripple below 80% of the peak and the headroom at least 10 mV from zero. The calculator agreed with every vector at the first run. The same author wrote the engine and the vectors, so the independence is in the method.
- 37 built-in self-tests (7 reference cases, 4 rejections, the unit and invalid-input checks). A first draft of the output list gave the regulator ripple in mV from a value already in volts; the built-in test caught it before release.
- Library card TL-090 links to the calculator; guides linked: rectifier ripple, bridge rectifier, linear regulator, zener regulator. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook rectifier and capacitor-filter relations; diode drop, dropout and rejection are the user's data-sheet figures), a scope screen to confirm. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`. Page short name "Power Supply Ripple".

## v1.99.0

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-099 "Semiconductor Loss Calculator", article "Power Semiconductor Switching Losses and Thermal Design", division Power Electronics; audit status "Guide only": the static guide `converters/mosfet-loss-formulas.html` exists, no interactive calculator for one device). New calculator `semiconductor-loss` in division 24 Semiconductor Components beside `switching-loss-comparison` and `heatsink-thermal` (existing division, so the division count does not change). It covers one MOSFET, IGBT or diode in a hard-switched stage, where `switching-loss-comparison` compares two or three MOSFETs and says an IGBT is not covered. You enter the switched voltage, the on-state current, the duty cycle and the frequency, a conduction offset voltage (0 for a MOSFET, Vce0 or the diode knee voltage otherwise), the on-resistance at 25 C with a linear temperature coefficient, the switching loss either from the rise and fall times or from the data-sheet turn-on and turn-off energies with their test voltage and current, the gate charge and voltage, Coss, Qrr, the junction-to-ambient thermal resistance, the ambient temperature and the maximum junction temperature. It gives the on-resistance at the junction temperature, the conduction loss, the energy per cycle of each switching part, the switching, Coss, recovery and gate loss, the loss inside the device and the total with the gate drive, the junction temperature (solved in closed form with the on-resistance taken at that temperature), the margin to the limit, the highest loss the junction allows and the highest switching frequency that keeps the junction within its limit, and the share of the total that is not conduction.
- Default: the guide's worked example (48 V, 10 A, D 0.5, 100 kHz, 10 mOhm, 20 ns edges, 50 nC at 10 V) with a 30 C/W thermal resistance at 40 C ambient: 0.5 W conduction, 0.96 W switching, 0.05 W gate, 1.46 W inside the device and 1.51 W in all as in the guide, junction 83.8 C (66.2 C inside 150 C), highest loss 3.667 W, highest frequency 329.86 kHz. Warnings: a junction over the limit, no frequency that can meet it (conduction alone is at or above the allowed loss), rise and fall times as long as the shorter of the on and off intervals, and data-sheet energies scaled above their test voltage or current. Rejected: a maximum junction temperature not above the ambient, thermal runaway (the self-heating of the on-resistance has no steady point), and the shared range checks. Not checked: any device data (nothing is embedded), ringing, the Miller plateau, the IGBT tail unless the data-sheet energies are used, dead time and third-quadrant conduction, soft switching, parallel devices, transient or pulsed heating, and any derating or safe-operating-area rule.
- 40 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 6,442 unchanged), 34 random and 6 named, in 30-digit mpmath by a different route from the engine's closed forms: each switching energy is the integral of v i dt over its transition, the data-sheet energies are scaled as an energy per volt-ampere, the Coss energy is the integral of C v dv, the recovery energy the integral of V dq, the conduction loss the on-state power integrated over the on-time, the junction temperature the fixed point of Tj = Ta + Rth (Pc(Tj) + other loss) where the engine solves the linear equation, and the highest frequency a bisection on the frequency at which the iterated junction temperature reaches the limit. The engine agreed with every vector on the first run. The same author wrote the engine and the vectors, so the independence is in the method.
- 10 built-in reference and rejection cases (their expected numbers come from the same independent reference) plus the shared unit and invalid-input checks: 62 self-tests for the calculator.
- Library card TL-099 links to the calculator; guides linked: MOSFET losses, heat sinks, buck and boost converters, thermal time constants. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook device-loss relations, every device figure is the user's data-sheet value), a scope screen to confirm. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`. Page short name "Semiconductor Loss Calculator".

## v1.99.1

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-009 "Resistance and Resistivity Calculator", article "Conductance, Resistivity and Specific Resistance Explained", division Basic Electrical Engineering; audit status "Partial": `cable-resistance` and `conductor-temp-resistance` exist, but no solver for resistivity, conductance or specific resistance). New calculator `resistivity-conductance` in division 02 Ohm's Law (existing division, so the division count does not change). It works on a uniform conductor, R = rho L / A, in four directions chosen by "Solve for": the resistance from the material, length and cross-section; the resistivity of a sample from a measured resistance; the length that gives a target resistance; the cross-section that gives a target resistance. The material is copper, aluminium, silver, gold, nickel or nichrome (the `MAT` table the other conductor calculators use) or a custom resistivity and temperature coefficient; the section is an area or the diameter of a round solid conductor (A = pi d^2 / 4); the conductor temperature is entered (rho_T = rho_20 (1 + alpha (T - 20))). It gives the resistance, the conductance (1 / R, in siemens), the resistance at 20 C, the resistance per kilometre, the resistivity at the conductor temperature and at 20 C (ohm metres and ohm mm2 per metre), the conductivity in MS/m, the conductivity at 20 C as a percentage of annealed copper (% IACS, against 1.7241e-8 ohm metres), and the length, area and equivalent round-wire diameter. For a measured resistivity it also says whether the value is within 5% of one of the six presets or which is nearest.
- Default: 100 m of 16 mm2 copper at 20 C, the `cable-resistance` default: 0.10775625 ohm, 9.2802 S, 1.7241e-8 ohm metre (0.017241 ohm mm2/m, 100% IACS, 58.001 MS/m), 1.0776 ohm/km, a 4.5135 mm round-wire equivalent. At 70 C it is 0.12893 ohm (resistivity 2.0629e-8). Warnings: a measured resistivity at 20 C below silver (1.5e-8 ohm metres, so something is wrong with the length, section or temperature), a measured resistivity above 1e-5 ohm metres (beyond the metals and alloys listed, so the linear coefficient may not apply), a conductor temperature more than 100 C from 20 C (the linear fit), and a custom coefficient of 0 used away from 20 C. Rejected: any of length, area, diameter, resistance or custom resistivity at or below zero, and the shared range checks (temperature -50 to 250 C, coefficient 0 to 0.01). Not checked: skin and proximity effect, stranding, joint and contact resistance, data-sheet cable resistance, material grade and purity, the measuring method and its accuracy, and any alloy-specific or low-temperature behaviour.
- 40 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 6,482 unchanged), 34 random and 6 named, in 30-digit mpmath by a different route from the engine's R = rho L / A: the conductivity at temperature is sigma_20 / (1 + alpha (T - 20)) (the engine multiplies the resistivity instead), the conductance is sigma A / L and the resistance is its reciprocal, a round wire's area comes from its circumference (A = C^2 / 4 pi), the resistivity, the length and the area for a target resistance are each found by bisection on the conductance, the resistivity at 20 C is the root of rho_20 (1 + alpha (T - 20)) = rho_T, and % IACS is the conductivity against 1 / 0.017241 ohm mm2/m. The material constants are the same textbook figures, so the independence is in the method. The engine agreed with every vector at the first run. 15 built-in cases (11 reference and warning cases, 4 rejections) plus the shared unit and invalid-input checks: 33 self-tests for the calculator. Their expected numbers were recomputed in exact fractions after three hand-typed figures in a first draft were found to be wrong (the engine was right in each case).
- Library card TL-009 links to the calculator; guides linked: conductor resistance, Ohm's law, resistors, skin effect. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook resistance, resistivity and conductance relations; IEC 60228 and IEC 60468 / ASTM B193 are named as not applied), a scope screen to confirm. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`. Page short name "Resistance and Resistivity Calculator".

## v1.99.2

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-024 "Three-Phase Circuit Calculator", article "Balanced and Unbalanced Three-Phase Circuit Analysis", division Electrical Circuit Theory; audit status "Partial": `three-phase-power`, `line-phase` and `neutral-current-unbalance` exist, but there is no balanced or unbalanced three-phase network solver). New calculator `three-phase-network` in division 06 Three Phase (existing division, so the division count does not change). It takes a balanced supply (line-to-line voltage, phase order a-b-c, phase a at 0 degrees) feeding three independent load impedances, each a resistance and a reactance (reactance negative for a capacitive load), connected in star with the neutral wire, in star with no neutral wire, or in delta, with an optional equal impedance in each line and an optional neutral-wire impedance (0 is a solid neutral). It gives the line currents with their angles, the neutral current, the shift of the load star point, the current and voltage in each load impedance (the branch currents of a delta), the line voltages at the load, the real power in each impedance and in all, the load reactive and apparent power and power factor, the loss in the line and neutral wires, the supply real, reactive and apparent power and power factor, the line current unbalance (largest deviation from the average), the negative-sequence share |I2| / |I1| and the line voltage unbalance and drop at the load. The working shows the delta-to-star conversion, the neutral-point voltage, each current and a power-balance check (load power plus line and neutral loss equals supply power).
- Method: the star is solved with the neutral-point (Millman) equation Vn = sum(Ek Yk) / (sum(Yk) + Yn) with Yk = 1 / (Zk + Zl); a delta is converted to its equivalent star so the line impedance can be added, and the branch voltages and currents then follow from the line voltages at the load terminals (Jab = Vab / Zab). Default: balanced star 8 + j6 ohm per phase on 400 V with a solid neutral: 23.094 A in each line, no neutral current, 12.8 kW and 9.6 kvar at 0.8 power factor; the same impedances in delta give 40 A in each branch, 69.282 A in each line and 38.4 kW. Warnings: a star point that moves more than 10% of the phase voltage (three-wire star or a neutral impedance), a neutral current larger than the largest line current, a current unbalance above 10%, a line voltage unbalance above 2%, an average line voltage at the load more than 5% below the supply or more than 2% above it. Rejected: a zero impedance, a delta whose impedances add to zero, a phase that resonates with the line impedance with no resistance, a supply voltage of 0 or below, a negative resistance.
- 40 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 6,522 unchanged), 34 random and 6 named, in 30-digit mpmath by a different route from the engine's Millman and delta-star solution: the loop (KVL) equations of the circuit as drawn are written and solved as a linear system (four-wire star: Ek = Ik (Zk + Zl) + (Ia + Ib + Ic) Zn; three-wire star: two KVL loops plus KCL; delta: three mesh equations in the branch currents with the line currents from KCL at the corners), and the powers, unbalance and sequence figures are formed from the solved currents. The 10 built-in reference cases use the same independent reference, plus 4 rejection cases: 44 self-tests for the calculator.
- Library card TL-024 links to the calculator; guides linked: three-phase power, star-delta, unbalance, phasor addition. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook three-phase circuit analysis on a balanced source and user impedances; IEC 60364, IEC 61000-2-2 / EN 50160 and IEC 60034-1 are named as not applied), a scope screen to confirm. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`. Page short name "Three-Phase Circuit Calculator".

## v1.99.3

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-029 "Inductance Calculator", article "Self-Inductance and Mutual Inductance in Coils", division Electromagnetic Engineering; audit status "Partial": `inductance-series-parallel` and `magnetic-circuit` exist, but there is no self or mutual inductance or coupling calculator). New calculator `coupled-inductance` in division 03 AC Circuits (existing division, so the division count does not change). The self-inductances L1 and L2 are entered or found from the turns on one closed core of constant permeability (L = N^2 / reluctance, mu0 = 4 pi 1e-7); the coupling is given as a coefficient k, as a mutual inductance M, or from a series-aiding and a series-opposing reading (M = (La - Lo) / 4). It gives L1, L2, M, k, the turns-ratio equivalent sqrt(L1 / L2), the inductance in series aiding and opposing and in parallel aiding and opposing, the leakage inductance seen with the other coil shorted, L (1 - k^2), the T-equivalent arms L1 - M and L2 - M, the flux linkages, the stored energy and its mutual term, the reactances at the frequency entered, the voltages induced for a rate of change of coil 1 current, and (turns on a core only) the core reluctance and flux density.
- Default: L1 100 mH, L2 25 mH, k 0.9: M 45 mH, 215 mH series aiding, 35 mH opposing, 13.5714 mH parallel aiding, 2.2093 mH parallel opposing, leakage 19 mH and 4.75 mH, T-equivalent arms 55 mH and -20 mH, stored energy 0.3025 J at 2 A and 1 A. Parallel aiding with k = 1 and L1 = L2 is 0 / 0 in the formula; its limit L1 (1 + k) / 2 is used. Warnings: series readings whose average differs from L1 + L2 by more than 5%, and a core flux density above 1.6 T. Rejected (in the cross-field check): a mutual inductance, entered or implied by the series readings, above sqrt(L1 L2); a series-opposing reading above the series-aiding reading; and the shared range checks (k 0 to 1, positive inductances and frequency).
- 40 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 6,562 unchanged), 34 random and 6 named, in 30-digit mpmath by a different route from the engine's closed forms: the coupled-coil circuit equations (v1 = L1 x1 + sM x2, v2 = sM x1 + L2 x2) are solved for the parallel connections, the series connections are one current through both coils, the leakage inductance comes from shorting the other coil (L1 - M^2 / L2), the linkages and energy from the inductance matrix, and the coils on a core use L = mu0 mur Ac N^2 / lc directly. The perfect-coupling pair of equal coils makes the parallel system singular, so its two limits (aiding L, opposing 0) are written out. The engine agreed with every vector at the first run once two badly chosen named inputs in the vector file were corrected. 17 built-in cases (14 reference and warning cases, 3 more rejections) plus the shared unit and invalid-input checks.
- Library card TL-029 links to the calculator; guides linked: solenoid inductance, inductors, transformer EMF. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook coupled-inductor relations; IEC 60076, IEC 61558 and IEC 62024 are named as not applied), a scope screen to confirm. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`. Page short name "Inductance Calculator".

## v1.99.4

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-037 "Motor Nameplate Calculator", article "Motor Nameplate Data: How to Read and Apply Ratings", division Electrical Machines; audit status "Partial": `motor-current` and `motor-power-output` exist, but there is no nameplate-decoding calculator). The earlier P1 and P2 items, and the P3 items before this one, are built. New calculator `motor-nameplate` in division 12 Motors (existing division, so the division count does not change). It takes the figures printed on the plate of a three-phase induction motor: the rated output, line voltage, frequency, number of poles, rated speed, rated current, power factor, efficiency, service factor, the locked-rotor current ratio ILR / In (entered, not decoded from a code letter) and the winding connection at that voltage (star or delta). It gives the synchronous speed, the rated slip, the slip speed and the rotor frequency, the rated torque, the input power and losses at rated output, the apparent and reactive power, the current worked out from the output, voltage, power factor and efficiency, how far the plate current is from it and the efficiency the plate current implies, the voltage across and the current in each winding, the locked-rotor current, and the continuous output at the service factor with its torque at the rated speed. For the supply you have it gives the voltage and frequency deviation from the plate, volts per hertz against rated, the synchronous speed at the supply frequency and the current for the rated output at that supply voltage (In V / Vs, constant output); with a measured running current it gives Im / In as a rough loading.
- Default: the 15 kW, 415 V, 50 Hz, 4-pole, 1460 rpm, 27.3 A, 0.85, 90% plate with a delta winding on a 400 V supply: 1500 rpm synchronous, slip 2.667% (40 rpm, 1.333 Hz in the rotor), torque 98.11 N·m, input 16.667 kW and losses 1.667 kW, 19.62 kVA and 10.34 kvar, 27.28 A from the ratings against the plate 27.3 A (0.08% apart, implied efficiency 89.93%), 415 V and 15.76 A in each winding, 191.1 A locked-rotor, a supply 3.61% low (96.39% volts per hertz) and 28.32 A for the rated output. Warnings: a plate current more than 5% from the current the other ratings give, a rated slip outside 0.5% to 8%, a supply voltage more than 10% from the plate, a supply frequency more than 5% from the plate, volts per hertz above 110%, a measured current above the rated current times the service factor, and a measured current below 50% of the rated current (the loading is overstated at light load, because the magnetising current is most of what is drawn). Rejected: an odd or out-of-range number of poles, a rated speed at or above the synchronous speed, and the shared range checks (a power factor above 0 and up to 1, an efficiency above 0 and up to 100%, a service factor from 1 to 1.5, a locked-rotor ratio from 1 to 15). Not checked: the insulation class, temperature rise, altitude and ambient derating, enclosure, duty type and efficiency class, any code-letter or kVA-per-horsepower table, single-phase, synchronous and wound-rotor motors, a motor on a drive, dual-voltage plates (one rating is entered at a time), the efficiency, power factor and temperature rise at the service-factor output and at part load, unbalance, harmonics, and the starting, protection and cable sizing that follow from the plate.
- 40 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 6,602 unchanged), 34 random and 6 named, in 30-digit mpmath by a different route from the engine's closed forms: the slip comes from the electrical side (the rotor frequency is the supply frequency less (p / 2) N / 60, and the slip is that over f), the torque is the output over 2 pi times the speed in revolutions per second, the input power is the output plus P (1 - eta) / eta, the current from the ratings is taken from the delta winding (a third of the input power at the line voltage) with the line current as the difference of two winding-current phasors, 2 Iw sin 60 degrees, the star and delta winding quantities follow from the line quantities the same way, the reactive power is S sqrt(1 - cos^2), and the current at another supply keeps the apparent power. The engine agreed with every vector at the first run. The same author wrote the engine and the vectors, so the independence is in the method. 13 built-in cases (8 reference and warning cases, 5 rejections), whose expected numbers come from the same independent reference, plus the shared unit and invalid-input checks.
- Library card TL-037 links to the calculator; guides linked: motor formulas, synchronous speed, motor efficiency, motor code letters. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook induction-motor relations on the user's plate figures; IEC 60034-1, IEC 60034-30-1 and NEMA MG 1 are named as not applied), a scope screen to confirm. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`. Page short name "Motor Nameplate Calculator".

## v1.99.5

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-038 "Motor Selection / Load Calculator", article "Selecting an Industrial Motor for Load and Duty Requirements", division Electrical Machines; audit status "Partial": `motor-current`, `motor-power-output` and `motor-torque` exist, but there is no duty or load selection check). The earlier P1 and P2 items, and the P3 items before this one, are built. New calculator `motor-selection-load` in division 12 Motors (existing division, so the division count does not change). It takes the power the driven machine needs at its shaft, the speed ratio and efficiency of the drive, a design margin, a derating factor, the motor rated speed, the locked-rotor, average accelerating and breakdown torque ratios (multiples of rated torque), the terminal voltage at start and while running, the breakaway torque and the average accelerating torque of the load (per cent of its full-load torque), the peak running load (multiple of normal), a required torque margin, the load and rotor inertias, and the line voltage, efficiency and power factor at the operating load. It gives the power the motor must deliver, the design power, the nameplate rating needed after derating, the smallest rating at or above it in the IEC 60072 preferred list (0.18 kW to 1,000 kW), the loading against the rating and against the derated output, the spare power, and the next smaller size with its loading. It then gives the load torque at the load and motor shafts, the rated torque of the chosen size, the starting torque (locked-rotor torque times the voltage ratio squared) against the breakaway torque, the breakdown torque at the running voltage against the peak running torque, the total inertia at the motor shaft, the average accelerating torque and the time to full speed, and the input power, line current and reactive power at the operating load.
- Default: an 18 kW load at 98% drive efficiency with a 10% margin, a 4-pole 1,470 rpm motor and a 90% start voltage: 18.367 kW at the motor, 20.204 kW needed, 22 kW standard (loaded to 83.5%, 3.63 kW spare, 18.5 kW next size down at 99.3%), load torque 119.3 N·m and rated torque 142.9 N·m, starting torque 231.5 N·m against a breakaway torque of 47.7 N·m (ratio 4.85), breakdown torque 314.4 N·m against a peak of 143.2 N·m (ratio 2.20), 1.8 kg·m² total inertia and 1.90 s to speed, 20.18 kW and 33.4 A at the operating load. Warnings: a starting torque that does not exceed, or is less than the margin required over, the breakaway torque; a breakdown torque that does not exceed, or is less than the margin over, the peak running torque; no accelerating torque; an acceleration time above about 10 s; a loading of the derated output below 40%; a start voltage below 80%; and a need above the largest listed rating. Rejected: a need above 1,000 kW, and the shared range checks (a derating factor from 0.3 to 1, a drive efficiency above 0 and up to 100%, a start or running voltage above 0 and up to 120%, a peak load from 1 to 5 times normal). Not checked: any sizing rule, service factor practice, duty-type derating or efficiency class of IEC 60034-1, IEC 60034-30-1 or NEMA MG 1 (the margin, derating factor and torque margin are the user's figures), the enclosure, insulation class, temperature rise, cooling, protection, starter, drive and cable, motors on a drive, other motor types, frequent starts (no thermal check of the starts per hour or of the locked-rotor withstand time), and the full torque-speed and load curves (one constant average torque for the motor and one for the load, torque proportional to the voltage squared, efficiency, power factor, rated speed and torque ratios the same for the size chosen and its neighbours).
- 40 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 6,642 unchanged), 34 random and 6 named, by a different route from the engine's closed forms in exact fractions and 30-digit mpmath: the load torque is found at the load shaft and carried to the motor shaft as a torque transformation through the drive (divided by the speed ratio and the efficiency), where the engine divides the power by the efficiency first; the rated torque of the chosen size is the load torque at the motor shaft scaled by the ratio of the two powers; the size is the first rating found by an exact cross-multiplied scan of the preferred list; the acceleration time comes from energy (1/2 J w^2 over the mean power T w / 2 of a constant torque), where the engine uses J w / T; the current is taken from the complex power. The engine agreed with every vector at the first run. The same author wrote the engine and the vectors, so the independence is in the method. 17 built-in cases (12 reference and warning cases, 5 rejections), whose expected numbers come from the same independent reference, plus the shared unit and invalid-input checks.
- Library card TL-038 links to the calculator; guides linked: motor formulas, motor efficiency, motor acceleration time, motor starting, synchronous speed. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook induction-motor sizing and torque relations on the user's figures; IEC 60034-1, IEC 60034-30-1 and NEMA MG 1 are named as not applied), a scope screen to confirm. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`. Page short name "Motor Selection and Load Calculator".

## v1.99.6

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-063 "Protective Device Selection Tool", article "Fuses, MCBs, MCCBs and ACBs: Selection and Coordination", division Protection Engineering; audit status "Partial": `breaker-size` and the circuit-breaker formula guide exist, but there is no device selection checker). New calculator `protective-device-selection` in division 16 Protection (existing division, so the division count does not change). For one circuit and one device it takes the kind of device (miniature, moulded-case or air circuit-breaker, or gG fuse link), the design current Ib, the cable current-carrying capacity Iz, the conventional operating factor k2 of the device, the prospective fault current Isc, the rated breaking capacity of the device to be used, the smallest fault current at the far end, the highest instantaneous trip setting (multiples of the rating), the let-through energy I2t, the conductor area and the adiabatic constant k. It gives the highest rating the cable allows (the smaller of Iz and 1.45 Iz / k2), the smallest standard rating at or above Ib, the largest standard rating the cable allows, the loading Ib / In, the conventional operating current k2 In and its ratio to Iz, the smallest standard breaking capacity at or above Isc and the ratio of the device's capacity to Isc, the highest instantaneous trip m In and the smallest fault over it, the cable withstand k2 S2 and its ratio to the let-through, and the number of checks not met. The rating lists (miniature 6 to 125 A, moulded-case 16 to 1,600 A, air circuit-breaker 630 to 6,300 A, gG fuse 2 to 1,250 A) and the breaking capacity steps (3 to 150 kA) are common catalogue values, not a maker's range.
- Default: an 85 A circuit on a 105 A cable with a moulded-case breaker (k2 1.3) and a 12 kA fault: the cable allows up to 105 A, 100 A is the smallest standard rating and the largest the cable allows (85% loaded, conventional operating current 130 A, 1.238 times Iz), 15 kA is the smallest standard breaking capacity against the 25 kA device (2.08 times the fault), the highest instantaneous trip is 1,000 A against a smallest fault of 1,800 A (ratio 1.80), and the cable withstands 8,265,625 A2s (25 mm2, k 115) against a let-through of 400,000 A2s (ratio 20.66): no check is missed. Warnings: no standard rating between the load and the cable limit, a breaking capacity below the fault current, a fault above 150 kA, a smallest fault below the highest trip setting, a let-through above the cable withstand, and a smallest fault above the prospective fault. Rejected: a design current above the largest rating in the list for the kind of device, and the shared range checks (k2 from 1 to 3, positive currents). A fuse is not checked for instantaneous trip. Not checked: any clause of IEC 60364-4-43, IEC 60898-1, IEC 60947-2 or IEC 60269 beyond the textbook two-part condition (the figures k2, the rating lists, the trip bands and k are conventions or the user's data), discrimination and back-up protection, voltage and pole rating, temperature derating of the device, motor starting and inrush, loop impedance and disconnection time, arc-flash energy, and the energy let-through curve itself (I2t is entered).
- 42 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 6,682 unchanged), 36 random and 6 named, by a different route from the engine's closed forms in exact fractions: every standard rating is tested against each cable condition on its own and the ratings that pass are collected, where the engine forms min(Iz, 1.45 Iz / k2) first and takes the largest rating at or below it; the rating that carries the load is the first in the list at or above Ib, and it fits if it is in the collected set; the breaking capacity step is an exact scan; the trip, withstand and let-through figures are exact products, and the checks not met are counted from the individual yes/no tests. The engine agreed with every vector at the first run. The same author wrote the engine and the vectors, so the independence is in the method. 15 built-in cases (11 reference and warning cases, 4 rejections), whose expected numbers come from the same independent reference, plus the shared unit and invalid-input checks.
- Library card TL-063 links to the calculator; guides linked: circuit breakers, cable sizing, short-circuit level, cable I2t withstand, fault loop and RCD. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: this is a standards-governed protection calculator, so it is not classed Not applicable. `tools/review_records.json` has a scope_screened record for IEC 60364-4-43 and IEC 60947-2 with the same editions as the `breaker-size` record (public scope screen of 2026-11-12); it was not re-checked against the publisher catalogue for this calculator and no licensed clause text was reviewed. IEC 60898-1 and IEC 60269 are named in the scope note as relevant but their editions are not screened. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`. Page short name "Protective Device Selection Tool".

## v1.99.7

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-085 "Measurement Uncertainty Calculator", article "Measurement Uncertainty, Accuracy, Precision and Calibration", division Electrical Measurements; audit status "Partial": the `percentage-error` calculator and the measurement uncertainty formula guide exist, but there was no uncertainty budget calculator). New calculator `measurement-uncertainty` in division 32 Testing and Measurement (existing division, so the division count does not change). For one measurement it takes the measured value x, the standard deviation s of the repeated readings and their number n (the Type A source, uA = s / sqrt(n)), up to four other sources each as a figure of a chosen kind (standard uncertainty; rectangular limit, divided by sqrt 3; triangular limit, sqrt 6; U-shaped limit, sqrt 2; expanded uncertainty at k = 2, divided by 2; resolution step d, divided by 2 sqrt 3) with a sensitivity coefficient, a coverage choice (k = 2, k = 3, or a Student t factor for 95% or 99%) and an optional tolerance. It gives the Type A uncertainty, each source's contribution |c| u and its share of the variance, the combined standard uncertainty (root sum of squares), the largest contributor, the effective degrees of freedom (Welch-Satterthwaite, Type B sources taken as infinite, blank when infinite), the coverage factor, the expanded uncertainty U = k uc, the interval x - U to x + U, U as a percentage of the reading, and the test uncertainty ratio T / U. The Student t factor is found in `engine.js` by a regularised incomplete beta function and bisection; the effective degrees of freedom are truncated to a whole number before the lookup, as the GUM advises, and one million or more is treated as infinite.
- Default: 230.4 with ten readings (s 0.3), a certificate figure of 0.35 (k = 2), a meter limit of 0.6 (rectangular), a 0.1 resolution step and a temperature effect of 5 with a coefficient of 0.02: uA 0.0949, contributions 0.175, 0.3464, 0.0289 and 0.0577, combined standard uncertainty 0.4047 (the meter limit supplies 73.3% of the variance), effective degrees of freedom 2,980 (truncated, so the 95% t factor is 1.9608), U = 0.7935 (0.344% of the reading, 229.61 to 231.19) and a test uncertainty ratio of 12.6 against a tolerance of 10. Warnings: one source supplying 80% or more of the variance, a standard deviation from fewer than 3 readings, k = 2 or 3 with fewer than 30 effective degrees of freedom (the t factor for 95% is given), a test uncertainty ratio below 4 and an expanded uncertainty above the tolerance. Rejected: a fractional number of readings, a standard deviation with fewer than 2 readings, a budget with no source (no scatter, and every value 0 or every coefficient 0), and negative source values or fewer than 1 reading (shared range checks). Not checked: any clause of JCGM 100 (the GUM), ISO/IEC 17025 or a calibration certificate reporting rule, correlation between sources, a non-linear measurand (the coefficients are entered, not derived), Type B sources with finite degrees of freedom, other distributions, the Monte Carlo method, correction of known systematic errors, and any guard band or pass/fail decision rule.
- 42 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 6,724 unchanged), 36 random and 6 named, by a different route from the engine's closed forms in 30-digit mpmath and scipy: each Type B source is given its probability density and its standard uncertainty is the square root of the integral of x^2 f(x) (rectangular, triangular, arcsine, a resolution rectangle on -d/2 to d/2, a certificate figure U / 2), where the engine divides the limit by sqrt 3, sqrt 6, sqrt 2, 2 sqrt 3 or 2; the variances are summed before any root is taken and the shares are variance over total; the coverage factor is `scipy.stats.t.ppf` (95% or 99% two-sided) where the engine uses the incomplete beta function and bisection. Random cases keep the effective degrees of freedom at least 1e-4 from a whole number and away from the one million edge, and an expected zero (an unused source, a share of zero, repeatability as the largest contributor) is left out because the comparison is relative. The engine agreed with every vector at the first run. The same author wrote the engine and the vectors, so the independence is in the method. 20 built-in cases (15 reference and warning cases, 5 rejections), whose expected numbers come from the same independent reference, plus the shared unit and invalid-input checks.
- Library card TL-085 links to the calculator; guides linked: measurement uncertainty, percentage error, mean and standard deviation, ADC resolution. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: classified Not applicable in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (textbook uncertainty-budget arithmetic on the figures the user enters; JCGM 100, ISO/IEC 17025 and certificate reporting rules are named as not applied), a scope screen to confirm. Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`. Page short name "Measurement Uncertainty Calculator".

- Next item from the Technical Library audit Build list (TL-117 "PV Performance Ratio Calculator", article "Solar PV Performance Ratio, Losses and Degradation", division Renewable Energy; audit status "Partial": `pv-energy` multiplies an assumed performance ratio into a yield estimate, but nothing worked the PR out from metered energy, and there was no loss waterfall or degradation step). New calculator `pv-performance-ratio` in division 25 Solar PV (existing division, so the division count does not change). It takes the AC energy at the meter, the array nameplate DC power, the plane-of-array irradiation and the days of the period, and gives the final yield Yf = E / P0, the daily final yield, the reference yield Yr = H / 1 kW/m2, the performance ratio PR = Yf / Yr (the IEC 61724-1 energy ratio), the ideal energy P0 x H and the energy lost against it, and a temperature-corrected PR, PR / (1 + gamma (Tm - 25)), from one average module temperature. It then runs a seven-step design loss waterfall applied one step after another to the energy left by the step before (degradation (1 - d)^years, module temperature, soiling, DC wiring and mismatch, availability and curtailment, other losses, inverter efficiency) and gives the kWh lost at each step, the expected energy, the expected PR and the PR when new, the gap between the expected and the metered energy (kWh and per cent), and an optional target PR check (energy at the target, shortfall, margin in points).
- Default: 15,000 kWh from 100 kWp in 190 kWh/m2 over 30 days, module 42 C, gamma -0.35 %/C, losses 3, 3, 1, 2 %, inverter 97.5 %, 3 years at 0.5 % a year, target 80 %: Yf 150 kWh/kWp (5.0 a day), Yr 190 h, PR 78.95 % (83.94 % corrected to 25 C), ideal energy 19,000 kWh, 4,000 kWh lost; waterfall 283.6, 1,113.6, 528.1, 512.2, 165.6, 327.9 and 401.7 kWh, expected energy 15,667 kWh and expected PR 82.46 % (83.71 % when new), so 667 kWh (4.26 %) is not explained by the losses entered; 200 kWh and 1.05 points below the target. Warnings: a PR above 100 % or above 95 %, below 60 %, irradiation above 10 kWh/m2 a day, a period under 7 days, a corrected PR above 100 %, a module colder than 25 C (the temperature step is a gain), a measured energy more than 5 % below or 3 % above the waterfall, and a PR below the target. Rejected: a positive temperature coefficient (custom message), and zero or negative energy, array power, irradiation or days (shared range checks). Not checked: any clause of IEC 61724-1, IEC 61724-2 or IEC 62446-3 or a contract acceptance protocol, the monitoring class, sensor calibration and data filtering, the interval-by-interval temperature correction, inverter clipping, trackers, bifacial gain, snow, a soiling model and first-year light-induced degradation as a separate step. The kWh given to each waterfall step depends on the order of the steps; the expected PR does not.
- 42 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 6,766 unchanged), 36 random and 6 named, by a different route from the engine's closed forms in 30-digit mpmath: the energy and irradiation are taken in Wh and Wh/m2 and the array in W so the yields are Wh per Wp and hours at the 1000 W/m2 standard irradiance; the temperature step is the module's derated power over its nameplate, P(Tm) / P0; degradation is applied one whole year at a time with the part year by exp(frac ln(1 - d)); the waterfall is built as the cumulative retained fraction after each stage and the loss at a stage is the cumulative energy before it less the cumulative energy after it. An expected zero (a step with no loss, no temperature effect at 25 C) is left out because the comparison is relative. The engine agreed with every vector at the first run. The same author wrote the engine and the vectors, so the independence is in the method. 8 reference cases (including 5 warning cases and 2 rejections) plus the shared unit and invalid-input checks.
- Library card TL-117 links to the calculator; guides linked: solar yield and degradation, solar PV, solar panel temperature, solar DC to AC ratio. `tools/calc_scope.json` has a checked and not-checked entry. Stage 3: IEC 61724-1 is relevant (it defines the performance ratio), so the calculator is NOT classed Not applicable: `tools/review_records.json` has a `scope_screened` record (same standard and edition as `pv-energy`, official catalogue and scope metadata only, no licensed clause text reviewed). Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`. Page short name "PV Performance Ratio Calculator".
- Counts: 240 calculators, 6,808 vectors, 212 calculator pages, 605 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.8. Test files that hold page counts as literals were updated (sitemap 308 + conversion pages, 227 cached calculator slugs, 212 calculator pages, 193 generated pages, 61 scope-screened standards; Not applicable reasons stay at 179). The committed `sitemap.xml` and `sw.js` gained the new URL; `docs/API.md` and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md` were regenerated. No existing results changed.

## v1.99.17
- Sitemap `lastmod` (item 6 of the v1.99.11 external audit). The workflow used to stamp every sitemap URL with the date of the last commit anywhere in the repository, so a change to one page made all 607 pages look newly updated. New `tools/add_lastmod.js` runs after the build and gives each URL the date of the last commit that changed that URL's own file (one `git log --name-only` pass; clean directory URLs map to their `index.html`). A URL whose file has no Git history, or whose date is in the future or malformed, gets no `<lastmod>`. It refuses a shallow clone, where every file would carry the checkout commit's date; the workflow already uses `fetch-depth: 0`.
- `.github/workflows/pages.yml`: `SITE_LASTMOD` and `test -n "$SITE_LASTMOD"` removed; the step is now build, `node tools/add_lastmod.js sitemap.xml`, then the guard with `REQUIRE_LASTMOD=1`.
- `tools/check_deploy.js`: under `REQUIRE_LASTMOD` the guard no longer demands a date on every URL (an undated URL is the honest answer when no date is known); it fails when the sitemap has no `lastmod` at all, or when any `lastmod` is malformed or in the future.
- `SITE_LASTMOD` (one date for every URL) still works in `tools/build_pages.js` for tests and special cases; the comment and `DEPLOY.md` say not to use it for the public site.
- Tests: 6 new checks (per-URL dating, clean-URL mapping, replacement of an old date, undated/future/malformed dates, idempotence, a real two-commit repository where a one-page commit changes only that page's date, shallow-clone refusal) and 3 rewritten (workflow order and full history, guard with no dates at all, one undated URL allowed). Trial run on a temporary repository with a later one-page commit: 1 URL dated 2026-11-12, 606 dated 2026-10-01.
- Docs: `DEPLOY.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `STANDARDS.md` version line. No calculator, vector or page count changed. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.17.

## v1.99.13
- No calculator, vector or page count changed. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.13.
- Engineering fix (audit finding B, high priority): the Feeder Designer far-end fault used the transformer and cable impedance only, as if the supply behind the transformer were an infinite bus. A weaker real supply gives a lower fault current, so the displayed far-end fault was not the lowest and the instantaneous pick-up check could show a misleading pass. New optional input `Ssc` (minimum upstream fault level at the transformer primary, MVA or kVA): its impedance V²/Ssc is added to the transformer and cable impedance. Without it the far-end result is labelled an infinite-bus upper bound (headline, sentence and check text no longer call it the lowest fault current), and a pick-up at or below it is shown as CONDITIONAL ON INFINITE BUS (status pending, listed in the overall status line, not counted as a pass); a pick-up above even the infinite-bus fault still fails, because the real fault is lower still. With `Ssc` entered the check passes or fails as before on the lower current. A zero or negative `Ssc` is rejected. The calculation sheet states whether an infinite bus was assumed. The fault-level and breaking-capacity step still uses the infinite bus, which is the conservative case for Icu. Cable temperature and fault type still need the project study.
- Tests: the two pick-up tests that expected a pass on the infinite-bus figure now expect conditional; 8 new checks (upstream impedance value, kVA/MVA conversion, conditional status and status text, upper-bound labels, a 10 MVA supply turning the pass into a fail, zero and negative `Ssc` rejected). Nothing else changed; no vector changed.
## v1.99.12
- No calculator, page or sitemap count changed. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.12. Vectors 6,892 to 6,895.
- Engineering fix (audit finding A, high priority): the Overcurrent Relay Setting Calculator (`relay-setting`) rounded the exact time multiplier setting to the NEAREST 0.025, which could pick a setting below the one the grading time needs. For its own default (standard inverse, 4,000 A, 0.4 s) the exact TMS is 0.16054, the old code set 0.150 and the relay then operated in 0.37374 s, 0.02626 s faster than the 0.4 s asked for, with no warning. The setting is now rounded UP to the next relay step (0.175 for that case, 0.43603 s). The step is a new input (`tstep`, default 0.025) because not every relay uses 0.025, and the relay maximum is a new input (`tmax`, default 1.5). A new output `tdiff` gives the achieved time minus the required time (negative means too fast). If the exact TMS is above the relay maximum the setting is held at the maximum and a warning states that the required time cannot be reached and by how much the relay is faster; if it is below the smallest step a warning says the relay will be slower than asked. A maximum below the step is rejected. The old test that expected 0.150 (and the 1.425 and 11.6 settings of the very-inverse and extremely-inverse cases) was wrong on the engineering requirement and is replaced: 37 self-tests for the calculator (was 34), including the 0.05 and 0.01 step cases and the above-maximum warning.
- Independent vectors: `tests/gen_vectors.py` now takes the next step above the root-found TMS by exact decimal ceiling (capped at the relay maximum) and adds the step, maximum and `tdiff`; the relay-setting vectors are regenerated (random cases now draw a step of 0.01, 0.025, 0.05 or 0.125 and a maximum of 1.5, 3 or 10) plus 3 named cases. All other 6,852 vectors are byte-for-byte unchanged. `docs/API.md`, `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md` and the generated calculator pages regenerated.

## v1.99.18
- Resistor Colour Code Calculator (`converters/resistor-color-code.html`), build step 1 of the CEO product decision for the resistor tool: the IEC 60062:2016 + AMD1:2019 **pink multiplier (x0.001)**. Bands 3 (3 and 4 band) and 4 (5 and 6 band) now accept pink, so four bands read down to 10 milliohm (brown, black, pink) and five bands down to 0.1 ohm. Yellow, violet, pink, gold = 47 mOhm +-5% (44.65 to 49.35 mOhm).
- Display: values below 0.1 ohm are shown in milliohms; 0.1 ohm and above are unchanged. The reverse lookup (ohms to colours) now picks pink when it is the lowest multiplier that fits and refuses values below 10 milliohm (4 bands) with the new limit in the message.
- Page: FAQ, colour table (pink row), worked examples (47 mOhm) and the note under the table say that pink is a 2016/2019 addition and that an older resistor's pink band may not be a multiplier, so confirm with the datasheet or a meter.
- Tests: 4 new checks (pink decode and range, encode/refusal limits, milliohm display, 90 two-digit pink codes plus 24 E24 milliohm values round trip = 114) written by hand from the standard's table, independent of the engine; 3 existing checks updated (multiplier list has 13 colours, 0.05 ohm is now encodable so 0.005 ohm is the too-small case, pink is no longer the unknown colour in the band-3 refusal test).
- Not done in this step (next in the plan): SMD marking decoder (3-digit, 4-digit, R notation, EIA-96), E-series finder, tolerance-band design notes, and the temperature-coefficient colours that the 2016 edition adds (black, green, grey) - the table still has the six ppm/K colours it had; check them against your edition before adding. No calculator, vector or page count changed. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.18.

## v1.99.19
- Resistor Colour Code page (`converters/resistor-color-code.html`), build step 2 of the resistor tool plan: an **SMD resistor code decoder** (Mode 03). A second card on the same page, so no new URL and no sitemap, page-count or service-worker list change. Reads 3-digit (472 = 4.7 k), 4-digit (1002 = 10 k), R/K/M decimal-point notation (4R7, R100, 4K7, 1M2), EIA-96 (10B = 1.24 k) and zero-ohm links (000, 0000, 0R0), names the marking system, and explains the reading.
- EIA-96 table: the 96 values of the E96 series with the multiplier letters Z, Y/R, X/S, A, B/H, C, D, E, F. Verified by rounding 100 x 10^(k/96) at all 96 points (no exceptions). The page prints all 96 codes in 24 rows and the letters, generated from the data.
- Ambiguities handled in the open: `47R` is read as 47 ohm in R notation and the note gives the EIA-96 reading (3.01 ohm); a 3-digit code ending in 8 or 9 is read as x0.01 or x0.1 with a note that this is a maker convention; the page never states a tolerance or power rating (the marking does not carry one).
- Page: four new FAQ questions (SMD codes, 4R7, EIA-96, whether the code shows tolerance or power), new description, SMD tables and the decoder script. Production build and deploy guard run clean (610 pages with canonical and JSON-LD).
- Tests: 16 new checks written by hand, independent of the decoder: the six known markings and 18 others, the E96 rounding rule, 1,056 EIA-96 code and letter pairs, 920 numeric codes, zero-ohm links, the 47R ambiguity, input cleaning, 11 refusals, the page script (4 states) and the page HTML (decoder and 24-row table). Total 28,356. No calculator, vector or sitemap count changed. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.19.
- Still to do in the resistor plan: E-series finder (E6 to E192), resistor power and series/parallel checks, the LED workflow, and the extra 2016 temperature-coefficient colours (needs your edition).

## v1.99.20
- Resistor Colour Code page (`converters/resistor-color-code.html`), build step 3 of the resistor tool plan: an **E-series standard value finder** (the preferred-value part of Mode 04). A third card on the same page, so no new URL and no sitemap, page-count or service-worker list change. Enter a resistance (500, 4.7k, 2.2M, 0.01 ohm to 1 G) and pick E6, E12, E24, E48, E96 or E192: the page shows the nearest standard value with its percentage difference, the next value below and the next value above (so you can round up or down on purpose), whether the value is already standard, and the colour bands of the nearest value. 500 ohm gives 510 ohm in E24 (+2%) and 470 ohm in E12 (-6%).
- Data (`ES_T` in `tools/reference_logic.js`): E6, E12 and E24 are the published IEC 60063 tables, typed as tables because they are not plain roundings (E24 has 2.7, 3.0, 3.3, 3.6, 3.9, 4.3). E48, E96 and E192 are round(100 x 10^(k/N)); no point is within 0.001 of a rounding edge, E48 is every second E96 value and E96 is the list the SMD decoder uses. Typical tolerances shown with each series: E6 20%, E12 10%, E24 5%, E48 2%, E96 1%, E192 0.5% and tighter. E24 is also made in 1% parts, so the page tells the reader to check the datasheet.
- Rules stated in the open: a value exactly between two standard values goes to the lower one; the value shown is the nearest by ohms, and the neighbours on both sides are always shown; the bands are 3 for E6 (no tolerance band means 20%), 4 for E12 and E24 and 5 for E48 and above, and five bands cannot show a value under 0.1 ohm, so the page says that instead of guessing.
- Page: one new FAQ question (nearest standard value), the finder, and the old "E12 and E24" section is now "Preferred values: the E series" with all six lists printed as digits and a note on how E48, E96 and E192 are derived. Description kept at the 160-character limit.
- Tests: 22 new checks written by hand, independent of the finder: counts and ordering, the three published tables, the nesting of the series, the rounding rule at 336 points, spot values, the worked 500 ohm examples, exact values, decade edges, the tie rule, input cleaning, about 2,400 lookups against a brute-force search over every decade, every value of every series round-tripped through its colour bands, refusals, the page script (5 states) and the page HTML. No calculator, vector or sitemap count changed. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.20.
- Not done in this step (next in the plan): series and parallel resistor calculation, power dissipation and rating checks, then the LED workflow that joins them. The temperature-coefficient colours the 2016 edition adds (black, green, grey) are still not added: check them against your edition first.

## v1.99.21
- Resistor Colour Code page (`converters/resistor-color-code.html`), build step 4 of the resistor tool plan: **series and parallel resistors** and **resistor power and rating** (the rest of Mode 04). Two more cards on the same page, so no new URL and no sitemap, page-count or service-worker list change.
- Series and parallel: 2 to 20 resistances (330 180, 4.7k 10k; spaces, commas or semicolons), total in ohm, kilohm or megohm, the working written out, and the nearest standard value of the chosen E series next to the total (330 + 180 = 510 ohm, exact in E24; 1k || 1k = 500 ohm, nearest E24 510 ohm). Each value must be one word, so "4.7 k" is refused with a message instead of being misread. As on the rest of the page, m and M both mean mega; milliohm values are not accepted in this box.
- Power and rating: resistance plus the voltage across it (volts) or the current through it (milliamperes), a share of the rating to use (50, 60, 70 or 100%), and the page gives current, voltage, power, the minimum rating (power divided by the share) and the next common rating from 1/16, 1/10, 1/8, 1/4, 1/2, 1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 25, 50 and 100 W. 10 V across 510 ohm = 19.61 mA, 196.1 mW, needs 392 mW at half rating, so 1/2 W. Above 100 W the page says no listed rating is enough. The page states that the rating depends on ambient temperature, pulses and enclosure and that the datasheet decides; the colour code never carries a power rating.
- Page: two FAQ questions (series and parallel totals, what power rating) and two cards. The E-series test now counts six options in each of the two series selectors (12).
- Tests: 25 new checks written by hand, independent of the code: ten worked totals, 300 random sets checked against the sum and reciprocal-sum laws, grouping and bounds, 20-resistor limit, 8 refusals, suffix handling, prefix formatting, the LED power example, rating boundaries (exactly 5 W, exactly 250 mW at 100%), above-list loads, 300 random power cases checked against V x I = I^2 R = V^2 / R, 11 refusals, both page scripts (10 states) and the page HTML. Two of the new tests first failed because the expected values were mistyped (a 2-resistor grouping case and 4.7 k at 10 mA, which is 0.47 W and needs 1 W); the code was right and the tests were corrected. No calculator, vector or sitemap count changed. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.21.
- Not done in this step (next in the plan): the LED workflow that joins resistance, preferred value, colour bands, current and power in one place. The temperature-coefficient colours the 2016 edition adds (black, green, grey) are still not added: check them against your edition first.

## v1.99.22
- Resistor Colour Code page (`converters/resistor-color-code.html`), build step 5 (the last planned step): the **LED series resistor workflow**, which joins the earlier cards in one calculation. A fifth card on the same page, so no new URL and no sitemap, page-count or service-worker list change.
- Inputs: supply voltage, LED forward voltage, wanted current (mA), number of LEDs in series (1 to 20), E series (E6 to E192), next value above or nearest value, and the share of the power rating to use. Steps shown: (1) required R = (Vs - n x Vf) / I, (2) the standard value, (3) its colour bands, (4) the actual current and its difference from the target, (5) the resistor power P = V^2 / R, (6) the next common rating, plus the current range if the resistor is at the edge of its series tolerance. 12 V, 2 V, 20 mA gives 500 ohm, 510 ohm (E24), Green-Brown-Brown-Gold, 19.61 mA (-1.96%), 196.1 mW and 1/2 W, which is the document's worked example.
- Choices stated in the open: the default is the next standard value above, because it can never give the LED more current than asked for; "nearest" is offered and, when it rounds the current up, the page says the LED gets more than asked and to check its maximum current. A supply that is not above the LED total is refused. The tolerance range uses the series tolerance (E24 5%) on the nominal resistor only. The page states that forward voltage varies with current, temperature and the individual LED and that the LED and resistor datasheets are the limits; it does not model them.
- It reuses the tested functions `esFind`, `esBands` and `pwCalc`, so the standard values, bands and ratings come from the same code as the earlier cards.
- Tests: 17 new checks written by hand, independent of the workflow: the worked example and its tolerance range, E12 nearest versus next above, three LEDs in series, floating-point noise (5 - 3.2 at 10 mA is exactly 180 ohm), E96 and E6 band counts, the rating share, 1,800 random cases in all six series against a brute-force search with the laws V = I R and P = I^2 R = V I, 10 and 8 refusals, five-band limits, the page script (5 states) and the page HTML. Two of the new tests first failed because the expected values were wrong (a case that really needs 0.1 ohm, which is in range, and a five-band case I had labelled wrongly); the code was right and the tests were corrected. The E-series page test now counts 18 options (six in each of three selectors). No calculator, vector or sitemap count changed. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.22.
- Plan status: steps 1 to 5 are done (pink multiplier, SMD decoder, E-series finder, series/parallel and power, LED workflow). Still open from the CEO document: voltage divider design, a printable calculation report, and the temperature-coefficient colours the 2016 edition adds (black, green, grey), which need checking against your edition first.
