## v1.99.24
- Resistor Colour Code page (`converters/resistor-color-code.html`): the **LED series resistor** and **Voltage divider** cards now carry a note with links to the Ohm's Law Calculator and the Ohm's Law Formulas page, so the V = I x R and P = V x I checks are one click from the workflow (`tools/build_reference.js`). No new URL, so no sitemap or page count change.
- Plan status: still open from the CEO document is the 2016 temperature-coefficient colours (black, green, grey), not added until the values are checked against your edition of IEC 60062. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.24.

## v1.99.25
- Resistor Colour Code page (`converters/resistor-color-code.html`): the colour table now follows **IEC 60062:2016 Table 1**, read from the standard itself (the owner's PDF). Temperature coefficient colours added: black 250, green 20, grey 1 (with brown 100, red 50, orange 15, yellow 25, blue 10, violet 5 already present), so the sixth band offers 9 colours. Tolerance colours added: orange +-0.05%, yellow +-0.02%. **Correction:** grey was +-0.05% and is now +-0.01% as Table 1 gives it (older charts show +-0.05%, so the page tells the reader to check the datasheet of an older part). White has neither a tolerance nor a TCR; black has no tolerance (`tools/reference_logic.js`, `RES_T`).
- Page text: FAQ lists the nine TCR values and says the sixth band is no longer required to be the wider one (IEC 60062:2016 3.4 note: the tolerance band is the single wider band); the note under the table names the source table. Decoder, 6-band selector and the colour table all read the same `RES_T`.
- Tests: 1 new check written by hand from Table 1 (all nine TCR colours and values, the three new tolerance colours, white refused as a TCR, black refused as a tolerance, list lengths 9 and 10); 4 existing checks updated (orange is now a valid tolerance, so the refusal cases use white; the band 5 and band 6 selectors have 10 and 9 colours). Version bumped to 1.99.25 in engine, service worker, ROADMAP, API doc; STANDARDS.md software version corrected from 1.99.22.
- Not done: Table 16 (TCR letter codes), the RKM code with the L letter for milliohm (L10 = 0.1 mOhm, 10L = 10 mOhm) and the date codes are in the same standard but outside the CEO document's plan; the SMD decoder reads R, K and M only.

## v1.99.26
- SMD decoder (`smdDecode` in `tools/reference_logic.js`) now reads the RKM milliohm letter: L is the decimal point in milliohm (4L7 = 4.7 mOhm, L100 = 0.1 mOhm, 10L = 10 mOhm). Page: input hint, two table rows (4L7, L100) and the FAQ answer updated; the error text names L.
- Tests: new hand-written checks for L notation (4L7, L100, L10, 10L, 100L, lower case, page script); no existing expected value changed.
- Still not done: Table 16 (TCR letter codes) and the date codes. The E96 letter table is still verified by rounding, not against Annex A (not in the PDF text received).

## v1.99.27
- Resistor Colour Code page: the LED series resistor and voltage divider cards now calculate with Ohm's Law instead of only linking to it. LED: the voltage across the resistor, I = V / R, P = V x I with the numbers filled in. Divider: I = Vin / (R1 + R2), then V = I x R across each leg (with a load, R2 in parallel with it), and the two drops are shown adding to the input voltage. The lines are in the printable report. Code: `ohm` field of `ledCalc` and `vdCalc` in `tools/reference_logic.js`.
- Tests: 7 new checks with the arithmetic done by hand (12 V / 2 V / 20 mA = 10 V / 510 ohm = 19.61 mA; 3 LEDs = 6 V / 300 ohm; 12 V across 5.6 k + 3.9 k = 1.263 mA, 7.074 V + 4.926 V; loaded divider 6.8 k in parallel with 10 k = 4.04762 k), the page scripts, the built page and the report rows.
- Still not done: Table 16 (TCR letter codes), the date codes and the check of the E96 letter table against Annex A. All three need pages of IEC 60062 beyond page 15 of the PDF text received.

## v1.99.28
- SMD decoder checked against IEC 60062:2016 clause 4.2.1 and Tables 2 to 4 (pages 11 to 13 of the owner's PDF). The L reading from v1.99.26 is confirmed (L10 = 0.1 mOhm, 10L = 10 mOhm). Two gaps found and closed: the RKM letter G (x10^9, 1G0 = 1 GOhm up to 332G) was not read, and the 5-character codes for four significant numerals (Table 4: 59R04, 590R4, 5K904, 59K04) were refused. Page: two new table rows (1G0, 59R04), FAQ text and error message updated.
- Tests: all 48 codes of Table 2, 22 fixed-length codes of Table 3 and the four codes of Table 4, with the values printed in the standard; five-character codes that are not RKM codes are still refused.
- Not done, and not possible from the PDF held: it is the 15-page preview of the standard (pages 1 to 15 of 30). Table 16 (TCR letter codes), the date codes (clause 8, Tables 17 to 20) and Annex A (the E96 letter table, Tables A.1 and A.2) are on later pages. The three-character system of 4.2.2 starts on page 15 and its Table 5 is cut off.


## v1.99.29
- Resistor Colour Code page (`converters/resistor-color-code.html`): the last open item of the CEO document that needs no standard, **resistor tolerance impact analysis**. Two new cards, so no new URL and no sitemap or service-worker list change.
- **Tolerance impact: LED current** (`tolLed` in `tools/reference_logic.js`): supply voltage and tolerance, LED forward voltage and its possible change, resistance and tolerance, optional LED maximum current. It shows the nominal current, the worst-case range (supply low, LED voltage up and resistor high for the lowest current, the opposite for the highest), each source on its own, the largest single source, a root-sum-square estimate of the typical spread, and the resistor power nominal and worst case (Vmax squared over the lowest resistance). It warns when the worst case exceeds the LED limit and when the supply can fall to the LED voltage.
- **Tolerance impact: voltage divider output** (`tolDiv`): input and its tolerance, R1 and R2 with their own tolerances, optional load (taken as exact). Same outputs: nominal, worst-case range, each source alone, largest source, root-sum-square spread. Default example: 12 V +-5% over 5.6k and 3.9k at +-5% gives 4.407 to 5.48 V.
- Both cards are in the printable report (`RP_SECTIONS`, four sections now) and a FAQ answer was added. Blank tolerances count as 0; tolerances above 50% are refused. The worst case is stated as a bound, not an expectation; temperature, age and the LED voltage-current curve are named as outside the calculation.
- Tests: 20 new checks. Expected values worked by hand (17.18 to 22.29 mA, 240.7 mW, 8.23% root-sum-square, 4.407 to 5.48 V, the 1:1 divider at +-1%), two 500-case random searches that compare the worst case with all eight corner combinations written independently of the code, 21 refusals, the page script and the built page.
- Still not done, and not possible from the files held: Table 16 (TCR letter codes), the date codes (clause 8, Tables 17 to 20) and the check of the E96 letter table against Annex A. The files sent so far are previews that stop before those pages; an unsourced Table 16 list was not entered.


## v1.99.30
- First build from the "Electricity Cost Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note, taken one mode at a time. The note was written against v1.5.17; the project already had Mode 01 (`energy-cost`, Appliance Running Cost), the flat-slab bill (`electricity-bill-slabs`), `energy-audit` and `energy-savings`, so nothing was duplicated. **Mode 02, Multiple Appliance Cost** is the gap the note named as the highest priority, and it is built here as the new calculator `multi-appliance-cost` in division 07 Power / Energy (no new division).
- Up to eight appliance rows. Each row: power of one unit (W, 0 leaves the row out), number of units, hours per day (0 to 24) and the share of its rating drawn while on (%, for cycling loads such as a refrigerator or air conditioner; the note says not to assume rated power continuously). Common: days of use per month (30 default), one flat tariff per kWh, optional currency symbol.
- Outputs: energy and cost per day, month and year (year uses 365 days, as the note's 4,380 kWh example does), energy, cost and percentage share of each row, the largest user (first row on a tie), the connected load and the average power over the day. Default list is the note's example: 1,500 W AC 8 h, 75 W fan 10 h, 100 W lights 6 h, 100 W TV 5 h, 150 W refrigerator 24 h = 523.5 kWh a month, 4,188 at 8 per kWh; the AC is 69% of the energy. A row at full rating for 24 hours raises a warning about cycling loads. Rejected: no row with power, hours above 24, negative power, a fractional number of units.
- Not in this calculator, by design: slabs, fixed charge, duty, tax, demand, time-of-use (Mode 03, next) and savings payback (Mode 04). `led-retrofit-payback` and `energy-savings` already cover much of Mode 04. Text outputs name the limits.
- 30 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, earlier 6,909 unchanged): six named (the note's example, the refrigerator at 40%, 1 kW for 24 h = 8,760 kWh, a zero-hour row in a 31-day month, nothing used, a tie) and 24 random, in exact fractions from the plain definitions. 10 self-tests in the definition.
- Registered: guide links (energy cost, demand and load factor, energy meter multiplier), page short name "Multiple Appliance Cost", scope entry in `tools/calc_scope.json`, Stage 3 Not applicable reason in `tools/standard_applicability.json` (a scope screen to confirm; ISO 50002 and IS 14161 named as not applied). Not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 243 calculators, 6,939 vectors, 215 calculator pages, 608 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.30. Test files holding page counts as literals were updated (sitemap 311 + conversion pages, 230 cached calculator slugs, 215 calculator pages, 196 generated pages, 181 Not applicable reasons). `sitemap.xml` and `sw.js` gained the one new URL; `docs/API.md` and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md` regenerated. No existing results changed.


## v1.99.31
- Second build from the "Electricity Cost Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note, one mode at a time: **Mode 03, Electricity Bill Calculator**, as the new calculator `electricity-bill-meter` in division 07 Power / Energy. `electricity-bill-slabs` (units typed in, three slabs, telescopic only) is kept unchanged.
- Inputs: previous and current meter readings and a meter multiplier; a method switch for telescopic (each block at its own rate) or non-telescopic (one rate on all units, from the slab the total falls in, limits inclusive), as the note asks, with the method stated in the steps and a warning for non-telescopic; four slabs (three limits, four rates); sanctioned load times a fixed charge per kW; a fixed monthly charge; a per-unit surcharge; duty and tax each as a percent of the same subtotal (added, not compounded); other charges; optional currency symbol.
- Outputs: units billed, the slab reached, units and charge in each slab, energy charge, fixed charges, surcharge, subtotal, duty, tax, total bill and average cost per kWh. Default: 450 units, telescopic, energy 2,225, total 2,609.25 (non-telescopic energy would be 2,925). Rejected: current reading below previous, slab limits out of order, negative rates.
- Not modelled, and stated on the page: demand charges on recorded demand, minimum charges, power factor penalties, time-of-day rates, subsidies, rebates and arrears. No real tariff is embedded, so no utility's table (the note names the CEA Haryana table) is claimed; the user enters the values from the tariff order.
- 34 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own stream, earlier 6,939 unchanged), 8 named (both methods, exactly on a slab limit, zero units, slab 4, multiplier 40) and 26 random, in exact fractions from the plain bill definition. 10 self-tests in the definition. Guide links, short name "Electricity Bill from Meter Readings", scope entry and Stage 3 Not applicable reason added; not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 244 calculators, 6,973 vectors, 216 calculator pages, 609 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.31. Test literals, `sitemap.xml`, `sw.js`, `docs/API.md` and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md` updated. No existing results changed.
- Fix found by the vectors: units on a slab limit must stay in that slab, but a float product such as 1.525 × 40 gave 61.00000000000001. The units are now rounded to 9 decimals before the slab split. `energy-cost` and `electricity-bill-slabs` gained a "next" link to the new calculator.


## v1.99.32
- Third build from the "Electricity Cost Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note: **Mode 04, Energy Savings Analysis**, as the new calculator `replacement-savings` in division 07 Power / Energy. Checked against what existed first: `led-retrofit-payback` is lighting only, by fitting count with one shared hours figure, and `energy-savings` is a three-measure estimator with discounting. The gap was a plain existing-against-new comparison for any load, with its own hours and share of rating for each side, a percent saving, monthly and yearly figures and a payback in months. Both existing calculators are unchanged and are named in the page notes.
- Inputs: number of units; existing and new power of one unit, hours per day and share of rating for each; days per month and days per year (the note's 2,304 a year is 12 months of 30 days, its 4,380 kWh example is 365 days, so the days per year is an input: 360 gives 2,304, 365 gives 2,336); one flat tariff; cost of one new unit and other cost; life in years; optional currency symbol.
- Outputs: existing and new energy and cost per month, the saving in kWh, money and percent, the yearly saving, the investment, simple payback in months and years (0 when there is no saving), and the undiscounted net saving over the life. The default is the note's example: 100 W to 20 W, 10 h, 30 days, 8 per kWh: 24 kWh, 192 a month, 80%. Warnings: no saving, payback beyond the life, different hours. The page says that only equal useful output (light, flow, cooling) is a like-for-like comparison, as the note requires, and that this calculator cannot check it.
- 32 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own stream, earlier 6,973 unchanged), 6 named and 26 random, in exact fractions from the plain definitions. 10 self-tests in the definition. Guide links, short name "Replacement Energy Savings", scope entry and Stage 3 Not applicable reason added (ISO 50001, ISO 50002 and efficiency classes named as not applied); not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`. `led-retrofit-payback` and `energy-cost` link to it.
- Counts: 245 calculators, 7,005 vectors, 217 calculator pages, 610 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.32. All four modes of the note now exist (Mode 01 `energy-cost`, 02 `multi-appliance-cost`, 03 `electricity-bill-meter`, 04 `replacement-savings`). Still open from the note and not built: time-of-use and demand-charge tariffs, a three-phase load-to-energy input, PDF and CSV report of the whole analysis.


## v1.99.33
- Fourth build from the "Electricity Cost Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note, one item at a time: the **three-phase load-to-energy input**, as the new calculator `load-energy-cost` in division 07 Power / Energy. It joins what `amps-to-kw` (power only) and `energy-cost` (power in, no electrical inputs) already do: voltage, running current and power factor go in, kW, kVA, kWh and cost come out. Both existing calculators are unchanged and link to it.
- Inputs: supply type (single-phase, balanced three-phase, DC) with line-to-line or line-to-neutral voltage for three-phase; voltage; running current of one load; power factor; number of identical loads; average load while running as a percent of the current entered; hours per day, days per month and days per year; one flat tariff; optional currency symbol.
- Outputs: real power and apparent power of all loads while running, energy per day, month and year, and cost per hour, day, month and year. Default: 415 V, 10 A, PF 0.8, three-phase gives 5.7504 kW (the note's "about 5.75 kW"), 46.003 kWh a day at 8 h. Rejected: non-positive voltage or current, hours above 24 a day, a fractional number of loads. Warnings: power factor below 0.7, a part-load share (power factor falls at part load), and a part-year of days.
- Not modelled, and stated on the page: demand, fixed, duty, tax and time-of-use charges and slab tariffs (use `electricity-bill-meter`), unbalanced or non-sinusoidal loads, and power factor change with load. The note's point that connected load, rated input power, average power and maximum demand are different quantities is stated in the page notes.
- 33 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own stream, earlier 7,005 unchanged), 7 named and 26 random, written as a different route from the engine's closed form: each phase is taken on its own in 50-digit mpmath (phase voltage VL / sqrt3, P = sum of the three phase products), not sqrt3 x V x I x pf. 10 self-tests in the definition; one expected value there (5.750371) was a slip in the author's hand arithmetic and was corrected to 3320 x sqrt3 = 5.750408681 kW, with the engine unchanged. Guide links, short name "Load Energy and Cost", scope entry and Stage 3 Not applicable reason added (IEC 61557-12, IEC 62053 meter classes and tariff orders named as not applied); not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.
- Counts: 246 calculators, 7,038 vectors, 218 calculator pages, 611 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.33. Test literals, `sitemap.xml`, `sw.js`, `ROADMAP.md`, `DEPLOY.md`, `tests/README.md`, `docs/API.md` and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md` updated. No existing results changed. Still open from the note and not built: time-of-use and demand-charge tariffs, PDF and CSV report of the whole analysis.

## v1.99.34
- Fifth build from the "Electricity Cost Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note, one item at a time: **time-of-use and demand-charge tariffs**, as the new calculator `tou-demand-bill` in division 07 Power / Energy. `electricity-bill-meter` (slabs from meter readings, a per-kW fixed charge) and `electricity-bill-slabs` are kept unchanged and link to it.
- Inputs: energy in the peak, shoulder and off-peak periods with a rate for each; recorded maximum demand; contract demand with a minimum billed percentage; demand rate per kW; days in the period; fixed monthly charge; duty and tax each as a percent of the same subtotal (added, not compounded); other charges; optional currency symbol.
- Outputs: total energy, the energy charge of each period and in total, billed demand (the larger of the recorded demand and the minimum percent of the contract demand), the demand charge, subtotal, duty, tax, total bill, average cost per kWh, peak share of energy and load factor on the recorded demand. Default: 12,000 kWh, billed demand 40 kW, energy charge 85,000, demand charge 12,000, total 101,850, average 8.4875 per kWh, load factor 41.7 %. Rejected: negative energy, rates or demand and a minimum percent above 100. Warnings: recorded demand below the minimum billed demand or above the contract demand, load factor above 100 %, a peak or shoulder rate below the off-peak rate.
- Not modelled, and stated on the page: how a utility measures demand (kW or kVA, averaging interval, ratchets over several months), excess-demand and power factor penalties, minimum charges, seasonal and slab rates, subsidies and arrears. No real tariff is embedded: the example rates are illustrative and the user enters the values from the tariff order.
- 33 independent vectors in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own stream, earlier 7,038 unchanged), 8 named (default, demand below the minimum, minimum 0, exactly on the minimum, duty and tax on one subtotal, nothing used, load factor 60 %, demand above contract) and 25 random, in exact fractions from the plain bill definition. 10 self-tests in the definition. Guide links, short name "Time-of-Use and Demand Bill", scope entry and Stage 3 Not applicable reason added; not in `tools/team_review_approval.json`.

## v1.99.36
- Check of v1.99.35 against the "Electricity Cost Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note, with three fixes.
- **Zero hours of use.** Appliance Running Cost (`energy-cost`) now accepts 0 hours a day, week or month and returns zero energy and zero cost. It used to reject 0. Negative hours and more than 24 hours a day are still rejected. Two new self-tests (zero hours, negative hours) and one for the note's example (1.5 kW, 8 h, 30 days, 8 per kWh: 360 kWh and 2,880 over the days entered; 4,380 kWh and 35,040 for the 365-day year).
- **Monthly and yearly rule kept, labels and example clarified.** The per-month result stays an average month of 365 / 12 = 30.42 days, so twelve months equal the 365-day year; the note's table mixes a 30-day month with a 365-day year. The two month outputs are now labelled "per average month (365 ÷ 12 days)" and the year output "(365 days)", and the calculator notes show the 30-day and average-month figures side by side. The days-to-total result is the one to use for a billing period of a set number of days. Equipment Replacement Energy Savings is unchanged: its yearly saving uses the days per year entered, so 360 reproduces twelve 30-day months.
- **New page: Electricity Cost Calculator** at `calculators/electricity-cost/`, a hand-written entry in `tools/calc_pages_data.js` on the `energy-cost` engine. It runs Appliance Running Cost live and introduces the four steps of the note (one appliance, a list of appliances, a bill from meter readings, replacement savings) with links to the existing calculators and FAQ schema. No new calculator and no duplicated logic. The title is "Electricity Cost Calculator | Elecmetric" and the description is 139 characters, because the generator's own guard limits titles to 60 and descriptions to 160 characters and the note's SEO text was longer. Option 2 of the note (one page that runs several calculators from one set of inputs) is still not built.
- Counts: 247 calculators, 7,071 vectors, 220 calculator pages (20 hand-written), 613 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.36. Test literals, `sitemap.xml`, `sw.js`, `ROADMAP.md`, `DEPLOY.md`, `tests/README.md`, `docs/API.md` and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md` updated.

## v1.99.37
- Follows up the open items from the v1.99.36 check against the "Electricity Cost Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note.
- **Seasonal use (engine change, Multiple Appliance Electricity Cost, calculator 1.1.0).** Each of the eight rows has a new input, months of use per year (0 to 12, default 12). The yearly energy and cost sum each row over its own months: row energy per day x 365 x months / 12. Daily and monthly results are unchanged and describe a month in which every appliance is in use. At the default of 12 every existing result is identical (all 30 independent vectors still pass). Four new self-tests: 6 months of a 1,500 W, 8 h air conditioner gives 2,190 kWh and 17,520 a year at 8 per kWh while the month of use stays 360 kWh; a seasonal row mixed with an all-year row; 0 months gives no yearly energy; 13 months is rejected. The assumptions text no longer says seasonal use is not modelled; it says only whole months per appliance are.
- **Appliance rows on the page (`calculators/multi-appliance-cost/`).** The page shows the rows in use and has Add an appliance and Remove this appliance buttons (up to the engine's eight rows; the last remaining row cannot be removed). Each row has an optional name. Names are labels only: they go to the chart and the CSV, never to the engine.
- **Chart of energy per month by appliance.** A horizontal bar chart, largest user first, with kWh and share of the total, drawn as inline SVG with a text alternative. It is part of the live result and is hidden with the buttons in print.
- **Live result shows the answer.** Generated calculator pages showed the first three registry outputs, which for five electricity calculators were not the ones a reader needs (for the meter-reading bill: units, slab and slab 1 units, not the total). A per-calculator headline list in `tools/calc_pages_auto.js` now shows: multiple appliances, energy and cost per month and per year; meter-reading bill, units, total bill and average cost per kWh; load energy and cost, real power and energy and cost per month and year; time-of-use and demand bill, energy, total bill and average cost; replacement savings, energy and cost saved per month, cost saved per year and payback in years. A headline key that is not in the registry fails the build.
- **Several calculators from one set of inputs.** The Electricity Cost Calculator page now offers Use these numbers next: Add more appliances carries the power, hours, run-at percentage, days, tariff and currency into the multiple-appliance page (which then shows only that appliance), and Turn the units into a bill carries the energy over the days entered in as the units on the meter-reading bill (previous reading 0, multiplier 1). Numbers travel in the address (`?P1=...`); each value is read only for an input the page has, must be a number, and goes through the normal validation, so a bad address leaves the defaults. This is a hand-off between pages, not a single combined calculator.
- **Search overlap decided: keep both pages, cross-link.** `pages/energy-cost.html` (one appliance, formula and worked example) and `calculators/electricity-cost/` (the four steps) serve different searches, so neither is folded into the other. The conversion page now links to the four-step page.
- **Hub link.** LED Retrofit Payback is now listed in the Power and Energy hub as well as the Lighting and Facilities hub (`also` in `tools/category_hubs.js`).
- Opened in a real browser (Chromium, desktop and a 390 px phone width): no console errors or page errors, no sideways scrolling, defaults give 523.5 kWh and 4,188 a month and 6,369 kWh and 50,954 a year, and the carry links, the address prefill, a malformed address, add and remove rows and the chart all behave as described.
- Still not built: more than eight appliance rows, a different number of hours in each month of use, and one combined page that runs every calculator at once.
- Counts: 247 calculators, 7,071 vectors, 220 calculator pages, 613 sitemap URLs (no page added). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.37. `ROADMAP.md`, `STANDARDS.md`, `sw.js`, `docs/API.md` and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md` updated.

## v1.99.38
- Closes two of the three items left open in v1.99.37.
- **Twelve appliance rows (engine change, Multiple Appliance Electricity Cost, calculator 1.2.0).** The list now takes up to twelve appliances (rows 9 to 12 are new, default off). Each has its own energy, cost and share output. The page's Add an appliance button follows the engine, so it now goes up to twelve; the page builder reads row keys with more than one digit. Existing results are unchanged because the new rows default to 0 W.
- **Hours in the months not in use (engine change).** Each row has a new optional input, hours of use per day in the months not in use (0 to 24, default 0). Yearly energy is row energy per day x 365 x (months x hours / 12 + (12 - months) x other-month hours / 12) at the row's share of rating. At the default of 0, and whenever months of use is 12, the yearly figure is exactly what v1.99.37 gave. This is two levels per appliance, not a different value for each of the twelve months.

## v1.99.39
- Builds the last two items left open from the CEO list: hours for each month, and one place that runs the electricity calculators from one set of inputs.
- **Hours for each month (engine change, Multiple Appliance Electricity Cost, calculator 1.3.0).** Each of the twelve rows has a new choice, hours pattern, and twelve inputs, hours per day in January to December. With the pattern set to monthly hours, the yearly energy of that row is the sum over the months of hours x days of that calendar month (31, 28, 31 and so on, 365 days in all) x quantity x power x share of rating; the months-of-use and other-month fields of that row are then ignored. The daily and monthly results still use the hours-per-day field. The default pattern is unchanged, so every earlier result is the same. On the page each row has a button that opens the twelve months and one that fills all twelve with the hours per day above.
- **Electricity Cost Planner (new calculator `electricity-cost-planner`, 248th).** Up to six appliance rows, the slab tariff (telescopic or non-telescopic, fixed monthly charge, duty, tax, other charges) and one row to replace. It does not repeat any calculation: it calls Multiple Appliance Electricity Cost for the monthly energy, Electricity Bill from Meter Readings for the bill, and Equipment Replacement Energy Savings for the energy saved, then prices the bill again at the reduced units. The saving is the difference of the two bills, so it uses the slab the saved units were billed in; payback and net saving follow from it. Page at `calculators/electricity-cost-planner/`; linked first in the steps of the Electricity Cost page.
- Tests: four for monthly hours (hand-calculated 4,929 kWh a year in the browser check), nine inline for the planner, and 17 independent reference vectors for the planner written in exact fractions in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (earlier vectors unchanged). Scope and standards classification entries added; the Not applicable list is now 186.
- Still not built: a separate planner for more than six appliance rows (use Multiple Appliance Electricity Cost for up to twelve), time-of-use and demand charges inside the planner (see Time-of-Use and Demand Bill), and a PDF export (CSV and print remain).
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,088 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 614 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.39. `ROADMAP.md`, `STANDARDS.md`, `DEPLOY.md`, `sw.js`, `docs/API.md` and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md` updated.

## v1.99.40
- Closes the planner items left open in v1.99.39: more than six appliance rows, and time-of-use and demand charges inside the Electricity Cost Planner.
- **Twelve appliance rows (Electricity Cost Planner).** The list takes up to twelve appliances, the same as Multiple Appliance Electricity Cost, and the row to replace can be any of the twelve. Rows 7 to 12 default to 0 W, so every earlier result is unchanged.
- **Time-of-use and demand tariff (engine change).** A new choice, tariff type, switches the bill from the slab tariff to time-of-use energy with a demand charge. The list energy is split by the share used at peak and the share used in the shoulder period (the rest is off-peak), priced at the three rates, and the demand charge is billed demand x the demand rate, with billed demand the larger of the recorded demand and the minimum percentage of the contract demand. Fixed charge, duty, tax and other charges are the same inputs as the slab tariff. The bill is calculated by Time-of-Use and Demand-Charge Bill, so it gives the same answer as that calculator alone. Two new outputs, billed demand and demand charge, are zero on the slab tariff. After a replacement the saved energy is taken off in the same shares; the recorded demand after the replacement is an optional input (0 keeps the recorded demand, and a warning says so), because a replacement can lower the demand but the planner cannot work it out from a list of appliances. Slab limits and rates are ignored, and not validated, when the time-of-use tariff is chosen; peak and shoulder shares above 100 % are rejected.
- Tests: 10 inline self-tests (row 12, replacing row 12, row 13 rejected, the default time-of-use bill of 16,606.45875 worked by hand, saving at unchanged demand, saving with the demand falling to the minimum, slab limits ignored, shares above 100 %, no demand charge on the slab tariff) and 14 independent reference vectors written in exact fractions in `tests/gen_vectors.py` (own random stream, up to twelve rows). The 17 earlier planner vectors now list rows 7 to 12 as inputs (0 W); their expected results are unchanged. 7,102 vectors.
- Still not built: a separate hours-of-use pattern per appliance in the planner (use Multiple Appliance Electricity Cost for monthly hours), time-of-use periods per appliance, and a generated PDF file (CSV and Print / PDF through the browser remain).
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,102 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 614 sitemap URLs (no page added). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.40. `ROADMAP.md`, `STANDARDS.md`, `sw.js`, `tests/README.md`, `docs/API.md` and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md` updated.

## v1.99.41
- Per-appliance time-of-use in the Electricity Cost Planner (engine change). A new choice, time-of-use split, lets each of the twelve appliances carry its own share of energy at peak and in the shoulder period (the rest is off-peak); the default stays one set of shares for the whole list, so every earlier result is unchanged. The peak, shoulder and off-peak energy is the sum over the rows and is billed by Time-of-Use and Demand-Charge Bill. A replacement takes the saved energy off in the shares of the replaced appliance. Shares are a fixed fraction of the energy of an appliance, not a schedule of hours. Shares above 100 % are rejected on rows that have a power entered, and ignored on empty rows.

## v1.99.45
- **Random Number Generator, Mode 01 (Random Integers).** New standalone page `calculators/random-number-generator/`, built by `tools/rng_page.js` from `tools/rng_engine.js` (no DOM, BigInt) and `tools/rng_client.js`, listed in `STANDALONE_TOOLS` in `tools/build_pages.js` and cached by `sw.js`. Inputs: minimum, maximum, quantity (1 to 10,000), duplicates allowed or not, sort (none, ascending, descending) and an optional seed. Minimum and maximum may be negative and up to 1,000 digits, and results are exact.
- Randomness: unseeded numbers come from `crypto.getRandomValues` with rejection sampling (no modulo bias); unique numbers use a sparse Fisher-Yates draw without replacement; a seed switches to a documented reproducible generator (cyrb128 into sfc32) and is labelled as not secure. The page says it is not for passwords, keys or tokens. Results can be copied or downloaded as TXT, CSV or JSON, and the working steps are shown.
- Tests: `tests/rng_test.js` (374 checks, run in CI) compares the seeded output with `tests/rng_vectors.json`, which `tests/gen_rng_vectors.py` writes with an independent Python implementation, and also checks validation, uniqueness, sorting, exports and chi-square uniformity of the Web Crypto output.
- Not built yet (CEO document, Modes 02 to 05): Random Decimal Generator, Unique Number Generator as its own mode, Statistical Random Sampling and Engineering Test Data Generator (synthetic data labelled as such).
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 621 sitemap URLs (244 under `calculators/`). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.45.

## v1.99.46
- **Random Number Generator, Mode 02 (Random Decimals).** The page at `calculators/random-number-generator/` gains a Number type switch (Integers or Decimals) and a Decimal places field (0 to 15). `Rng.decimals()` in `tools/rng_engine.js` draws from the grid of numbers with that many places between the minimum and maximum, by mapping the grid to consecutive integers and reusing the exact integer engine, so every grid value is equally likely and no floating-point value is generated or rounded. A bound that falls between grid points is moved inward and the working says so. Seed, unique numbers, sorting, copy and TXT, CSV and JSON export work as in Mode 01; the JSON export records the decimal places.
- Tests: `tests/rng_test.js` now runs 516 checks. `tests/gen_rng_vectors.py` adds 10 decimal cases computed independently with Python `Decimal`, and the tests cover validation, inward rounding, unique grids, negative ranges (no negative zero) and chi-square uniformity of the decimal grid.
- Still to build (CEO document): Mode 03 Unique Number Generator as its own mode, Mode 04 Statistical Sampling, Mode 05 Engineering Test Data Generator, generation history and multiple sets.
- Counts unchanged: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 621 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.46.

## v1.99.47
- **Random Number Generator, Mode 03 (Unique Numbers).** The page at `calculators/random-number-generator/` gains a Mode switch (Random numbers, or Unique numbers) and a "Numbers to exclude" field. `Rng.uniques()` in `tools/rng_engine.js` always draws without replacement, for integers or for a decimal grid (0 to 15 places). Excluded numbers are removed from the pool first (duplicates in the list count once; numbers outside the range or off the decimal grid are ignored and listed), a partial Fisher-Yates shuffle picks distinct positions in the pool, and each position is mapped back to a value by skipping the excluded ones, so every remaining number is equally likely. With no exclusions the draw is identical to `integers({unique:true})`. A quantity equal to the pool size gives a complete random shuffle.
- Results show the draw order when sorted, a pool table (numbers in range, excluded, pool size, chance a given number is picked, possible sets and possible orderings, exact when the pool is below 10^40 and the quantity is 2,000 or fewer) and step-by-step working. Errors are explicit: everything excluded, quantity above the pool, a bad excluded number, an exclude list over 10,000 entries. The JSON export records the excluded numbers and pool size. New presets: raffle, shuffle 1 to 10, 5 of 1 to 50 skipping 7 and 13, unique decimals 0.0 to 1.0 and a seeded sample; every preset now sets the mode explicitly.
- Tests: `tests/rng_test.js` now runs 731 checks. `tests/gen_rng_vectors.py` adds 9 unique-number cases with exclude lists computed independently (the allowed values are listed explicitly and indexed, with Python `Decimal` for grids). Tests also cover C(49,6) and P(49,6), ignored and duplicate exclusions, full-pool shuffles, validation, JSON export and chi-square uniformity of an excluded pool (Web Crypto).
- Still to build (CEO document): Mode 04 Statistical Sampling, Mode 05 Engineering Test Data Generator, generation history and multiple sets.
- Counts unchanged: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 621 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.47.

## v1.99.48
- **Random Number Generator, Mode 04 (Statistical Sampling).** The Mode switch on `calculators/random-number-generator/` gains "Statistical sampling (distributions)". `Rng.sample()` in `tools/rng_engine.js` draws from normal (mean, standard deviation), uniform (minimum, maximum), exponential (rate), triangular (minimum, mode, maximum), Poisson (mean, up to 700) and binomial (trials up to 1000, success probability). Every distribution starts from uniform numbers u = (k + 0.5) / 2^53 built from 53 random bits (strictly between 0 and 1): normal by Box-Muller, exponential and triangular by the inverse CDF, Poisson and binomial by inversion with a sequential search (binomial with p above 1/2 counts failures and subtracts from n). Continuous values are rounded to 0 to 15 decimal places and the statistics and histogram describe the rounded values.
- Results show the list, a statistics table (count, mean, median, sample standard deviation, smallest, largest, sum), the theoretical mean and standard deviation with the sample difference, a histogram (inline SVG that follows the light and dark themes, with a data table under it; automatic bin count is about log2(n) + 1 between 5 and 30, one bin per whole number for discrete data, or a chosen 2 to 100) and the working. Sort and seed work as in the other modes. The page and the JSON export label the output as synthetic data from a simulated distribution, not measured data and not a validated physical model. The JSON export records the distribution, parameters, statistics and theoretical values.
- Parameter errors name the field to highlight (for example "The standard deviation must be greater than 0", "The mode must lie between the minimum and the maximum", "The number of trials must be a whole number from 1 to 1000"). Presets added: standard normal, mains voltage 230 V with sd 5 V, uniform 210 to 250, exponential lifetime, triangular 0, 2, 10, Poisson faults per month and binomial defective units.
- Tests: `tests/rng_test.js` now runs 1,062 checks. `tests/gen_rng_vectors.py` adds 21 sampling cases computed with an independent Python implementation (the same documented methods written separately with the `math` module, `fsum` and its own histogram rule); the tests compare the rounded values exactly and the statistics and histogram counts. They also check validation per distribution, extreme random words (all zero and all one bits stay finite), no negative zero, sorting, export, histogram rules, and statistical behaviour of the Web Crypto output (mean and standard deviation within limits, the 68-95-99.7 shape of the normal, Kolmogorov-Smirnov for uniform, exponential and triangular, chi-square against exact Poisson and binomial probabilities).
- Still to build (CEO document): Mode 05 Engineering Test Data Generator, generation history and multiple sets.
- Counts unchanged: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 621 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.48.

## v1.99.49
- **Random Number Generator, Mode 05 (Engineering Test Data).** The Mode switch on `calculators/random-number-generator/` gains "Engineering test data". `Rng.engineering()` in `tools/rng_engine.js` builds a synthetic dataset of up to 8 parameters (columns) and 1 to 10,000 observations (rows). Each parameter has a name, an optional unit, a minimum and maximum, 0 to 15 decimal places, a distribution, an optional typical value and an optional measurement tolerance. Distributions: uniform; normal (centre = typical value, default the middle of the range, standard deviation = range / 6, a draw outside the limits is thrown away and redrawn, so the values never leave the range); triangular (peak = typical value); log-uniform (equal chance per decade, minimum above 0, for resistance and insulation readings spanning decades). Values are written on the decimal grid and checked against the limits exactly (BigInt); a limit between grid points is moved inward as in Mode 02.
- Tolerance is an amount in the unit (`0.5`) or a percentage of each value (`1%`). It adds a "measured" column: measured = value + e, with e uniform over [-tolerance, +tolerance], rounded to the same places, modelling an instrument that stays within its stated band (measured values may lie slightly outside the limits and are counted). A seeded run gives each parameter its own stream (seed text + `|pN` for values, `|pN|e` for error), so changing one parameter, or switching tolerance on, never changes another column.
- Page: a parameter-row editor (add and remove, typical value disabled where a distribution does not use it, per-field error highlighting with the parameter named), an optional dataset name, ten presets from the CEO table (voltage 210 to 250 V, current 0 to 100 A, resistance 1 to 1,000 ohm, frequency 45 to 55 Hz, power factor 0.70 to 1.00, temperature 20 to 80 C, energy 0 to 500 kWh, insulation resistance, a four-parameter mains set and all eight), a summary table (mean, median, sample sd, smallest, largest), a tolerance table, the first 50 rows, a histogram per parameter and the working. Copy gives tab-separated text for a spreadsheet; TXT, CSV and JSON download.
- Synthetic labelling (CEO document): a banner above the results, a `Data label` column holding SYNTHETIC on every CSV, TSV and TXT row, a SYNTHETIC line at the top of the TXT, and in the JSON a `data_label`, a note saying the data are not field measurements, equipment test results or compliance evidence, and a `data_label` field on every record. Parameter names and units may not start with = + - or @ (spreadsheet formula injection) and CSV headings are quoted when needed.
- Tests: `tests/rng_test.js` now runs 1,393 checks. `tests/gen_rng_vectors.py` adds 10 dataset cases (22 parameter columns) computed with an independent Python implementation (own stream per column, Box-Muller with redraw, exact `Decimal` grid check, tolerance column); the tests compare the rounded values and measured values exactly and the statistics numerically. They also cover exact limits and grid for every distribution, off-grid limits, no negative zero, tolerance bands (absolute and percent), column independence, validation (field and row named), extreme random words, export labelling in every format, and Web Crypto statistics (uniform, truncated normal, triangular and log-uniform by Kolmogorov-Smirnov, equal chance per decade by chi-square, tolerance error uniform over its band).
- Still to build (CEO document): generation history and multiple sets. All five modes are now built.
- Counts unchanged: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 621 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.49.

## v1.99.50
- **Random Number Generator: multiple independent sets, Clear results and generation history (the two extras from the CEO document).** All five modes are unchanged when Number of sets is 1.
- Multiple sets: a Number of sets field (1 to 50, up to 100,000 values in all, counting every parameter in Engineering mode) works in every mode. `Rng.multiple({ kind, sets, ... })` in `tools/rng_engine.js` runs the chosen generator once per set. Unseeded, every set draws fresh Web Crypto bits. Seeded, set 1 uses the seed exactly as typed (so it equals a normal run) and set k, from 2, uses the seed followed by `|set k`, so adding sets never changes earlier ones. With several sets the seed is limited to 190 characters. The page lists every set, gives a statistics table per set (count, smallest, largest, sum, mean, median, sample sd, seed used), and a picker shows the full working, histograms and tables for any one set. Engineering sets share one SYNTHETIC banner.
- Exports for several sets: copy and TXT label each set; CSV is long format (`set,n,value`, or `Set,Observation,...,Data label` for Engineering, every row labelled SYNTHETIC); JSON hoists the generator, note and data label, adds `sets_requested`, `seed_rule` and, per set, `seed_used`, statistics and values or records.
- Statistics in Modes 01 to 03: the CEO document asks for sum, average, median, minimum, maximum and standard deviation, and these modes showed only count, minimum, maximum and sum. They now also give mean, median and sample standard deviation (n − 1), computed exactly in whole-number arithmetic (BigInt, integer square root), rounded half away from zero to places + 4 decimals, so they stay exact for 1,000-digit numbers. They appear in the page, in the working and in the JSON `statistics` block.
- Clear results button: empties the result and working and disables copy and download; the history is kept.
- Generation history: the last 20 results (fewer when very large, 300,000 values in all) listed newest first with a description and preview; Show result re-displays one and restores its settings without generating; Run again restores the settings and generates. Clear history empties the list. It is held in memory only, never saved to storage or sent anywhere, and a reload clears it.
- Tests: `tests/rng_test.js` now runs 1,631 checks. `tests/gen_rng_vectors.py` adds independent references: mean, median and sd for every integer, decimal and unique case (Python `Decimal` at 250 digits, a different algorithm from the engine), four multi-set integer cases (including a 30-digit range and unique draws) and two multi-set Engineering cases with the set-seed rule written separately. Other tests cover set validation and caps, growth stability, unseeded independence, and every export format with the SYNTHETIC label.
- Counts unchanged: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 621 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.50.

## v1.99.51
- **Fraction Calculator, Mode 01 (Fraction Arithmetic).** New standalone page `calculators/fraction-arithmetic/`, built by `tools/fraction_page.js` from `tools/fraction_engine.js` (BigInt rational arithmetic, no DOM) and `tools/fraction_client.js`, listed in `STANDALONE_TOOLS` in `tools/build_pages.js` and cached by `sw.js`. It follows the "Fraction Calculator" CEO decision note (five modes; this is the first release). The existing two-fraction `calculators/fraction-calculator/` registry calculator is unchanged.
- Engine: a fraction is a pair of BigInt with a positive denominator, reduced by the greatest common divisor; no floating-point number is used. One expression can hold several terms, brackets, unary minus, whole numbers, proper and improper fractions, mixed numbers (`2 1/3` or `2_1/3`; a leading minus negates the whole mixed number), terminating decimals read exactly, and negative denominators (`3/-4`). Division is ÷ or a colon because `/` is the fraction bar; a second `/` after a fraction is refused with a message instead of being guessed. Limits: 400 digits a number, 2,000 characters, 200 parts. Errors: zero denominator, division by zero (also a divisor that evaluates to zero), malformed input.
- Output: lowest-terms fraction, mixed number, decimal and percentage. The decimal is marked exact or repeating and the repeating block is shown in brackets (`0.58(3)`), found by following remainders; a block longer than 5,000 digits is shown cut off and said to be. Working is given step by step: mixed-number and decimal conversion, least common denominator for + and −, cancellation before multiplying, the reciprocal for division.
- Interface: expression field with a symbol keypad, a two-fraction builder (whole, numerator, denominator, operation) that writes the expression, example chips, copy of the fraction or the whole calculation, print, and a history of the last ten calculations kept in this browser only (localStorage, with a Clear history button).
- Tests: `tests/fraction_test.js` (2,425 checks, run in CI) compares the engine with `tests/fraction_vectors.json`, which `tests/gen_fraction_vectors.py` writes by letting Python evaluate each expression over `fractions.Fraction` (369 expressions with mixed, negative, decimal and unreduced inputs and several spellings of the operators, 40 numbers of 20 to 300 digits, 27 invalid inputs). Decimal expansions come from number theory (strip the factors 2 and 5; the block length is the multiplicative order of 10), not long division. The engine agreed with every vector at the first run.
- Not in this release (later modes of the same note): Fraction Simplifier page, Decimal & Fraction Converter, Mixed Number Calculator, and the Advanced Fraction & Ratio Tools (comparison, LCD, LCM, GCD, ratio, continued fractions); engineering fraction conversions (fractional inches to mm) are Phase 3.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 622 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool, not a registry calculator). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.51.

## v1.99.52
- **Fraction Calculator, Mode 02 (Fraction Simplifier).** New standalone page `calculators/fraction-simplifier/`, built by `tools/fraction_simplifier_page.js` from the same `tools/fraction_engine.js` as Mode 01 (new `simplify`, `euclid` and `factor` functions) and `tools/fraction_simplifier_client.js`, listed in `STANDALONE_TOOLS` in `tools/build_pages.js` and cached by `sw.js`. A shared mode strip (`tools/fraction_modes.js`) now links Mode 01 and Mode 02 on both pages. It follows the Mode 02 section of the Fraction Calculator CEO decision note: the user enters a numerator and a denominator and sees the original values, the greatest common divisor, the simplified numerator, denominator and fraction, with the process explained.
- Input: separate numerator and denominator fields, or one fraction field (`18/24`); the two stay in step. Also accepted: negative numerator or denominator (the sign moves to the top), mixed numbers (`2 6/8`), decimals (both parts are scaled by the same power of 10, so `1.5/2.5` is `15/25 = 3/5`), a lone whole number, and zero over any non-zero denominator (the gcd of 0 and d is d). Limits: 400 digits a number, 2,000 characters. Errors: zero denominator, empty or malformed parts, a second `/` (one fraction only), commas, two bare numbers with no fraction bar.
- Output: the table from the note (original, GCD, simplified numerator, denominator and fraction) plus the mixed number, the decimal (exact or repeating) and the percentage, and a flag that says whether the fraction was reduced or was already in lowest terms. Working: the Euclidean algorithm with every division shown (the table is capped at 40 rows, the text says how many more), the prime factorisation of both numbers and the common factors when the numbers are small enough to factor within a fixed trial-division budget (a large prime is left unfactored rather than looped on; Euclid is exact at any size), the division by the gcd, and a cross-product check that the result equals the original.
- Tests: `tests/fraction_simplify_test.js` (5,197 checks, run in CI) compares the engine with `tests/fraction_simplify_vectors.json`, which `tests/gen_fraction_simplify_vectors.py` writes from Python `math.gcd` and `fractions.Fraction` (238 plain, 66 decimal, 50 mixed-number and 40 large (up to 300 digits, with a planted common factor of up to 60 digits) cases, 59 prime factorisations from a separate trial-division routine, 34 invalid inputs). Every Euclid row is also checked to be an exact division, the chain to end in remainder 0, and the last divisor to equal the gcd. The vectors found one bug in the first draft (a sign class `[+-\u2212]` in a regular expression was read as a range and swallowed the first digit of a whole number with two or more digits); fixed before release. A second case (`2 6`, two numbers and no fraction bar) was being read as a mixed number over 1 and is now refused.
- Pages: Mode 01 gets the mode strip and a wrapping rule for very long numbers in its working text; Mode 02 uses the same rule (a 100-digit input no longer widens a phone screen).
- Not in this release (later modes of the same note): Decimal & Fraction Converter, Mixed Number Calculator, and the Advanced Fraction & Ratio Tools (comparison, LCD, LCM, GCD, ratio, continued fractions); engineering fraction conversions (fractional inches to mm) are Phase 3.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 623 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool, not a registry calculator). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.52.

## v1.99.53
- **Fraction Calculator, Mode 03 (Decimal & Fraction Converter).** New standalone page `calculators/decimal-fraction-converter/`, built by `tools/fraction_converter_page.js` from the same `tools/fraction_engine.js` as Modes 01 and 02 (new `convert`, `roundDec`, `sci` and `limitDen` functions) and `tools/fraction_converter_client.js`, listed in `STANDALONE_TOOLS` in `tools/build_pages.js` and cached by `sw.js`. The mode strip (`tools/fraction_modes.js`) now links Modes 01 to 03 on all three pages. It follows the Mode 03 section of the Fraction Calculator CEO decision note: both directions, percentages, and repeating decimals that are labelled exact or rounded instead of being passed off as finite.
- Input (one field, the tool reads the form): a terminating decimal (`0.125`, `.5`, `-2.5`, `5.`), a repeating decimal with the block in brackets (`0.58(3)`, `.(3)`, `12.8(99)`), a fraction (`3/8`, decimals allowed in both parts, a negative denominator), a mixed number (`2 1/3`, `-1 3/4`), a whole number, scientific notation (`1.25e-3`, exponent up to 300) and a percentage (`37.5%`, `33.(3)%`, `1/3%`, `8 2/8 %`). Limits: 400 digits a number, 2,000 characters. Errors: zero denominator, commas, `0.333...` (dots are not a repeating block), unbalanced or empty brackets, a block with no decimal point, a doubled or misplaced `%`, a second `/`, two bare numbers with no fraction bar, more than one sign, an exponent beyond 300, decimals or signs inside a mixed number.
- Output: lowest-terms fraction, mixed number, decimal, percentage, each with an exact or repeating flag. A repeating decimal shows its block in brackets (`0.58(3)`) with the digits before the block and the block length; a decimal rounded to 0 to 50 places (default 6) is always marked as rounded and approximate, for the decimal and for the percentage. A typed decimal with three or more places that is a rounded version of a simple fraction (`0.3333`, `0.66667`, `2.79167`) also shows the simplest fraction that rounds to it (found by continued fractions, restricted to denominators whose square times 100 is at most 10^k, so a match is not a coincidence), clearly labelled as a guess and not as the value typed. An optional largest denominator gives the nearest fraction up to that denominator (the same rule as Python `Fraction.limit_denominator`) with the signed difference in scientific notation. Working: the digits-over-a-power-of-ten step and its gcd for decimals, the two-multiplication subtraction for repeating decimals, the improper-fraction step for mixed numbers, the denominator's prime factors (only 2 and 5 means the decimal ends) and the length of the block, the percentage step, and a long-division table with every remainder, where the repeating block starts (marked) and the point where it closes (the table is capped at 60 rows and says so).
- Tests: `tests/fraction_convert_test.js` (15,980 checks, run in CI) compares the engine with `tests/fraction_convert_vectors.json`, which `tests/gen_fraction_convert_vectors.py` writes from Python `fractions.Fraction` and `decimal.Decimal` by routes that differ from the engine: repeating decimals are read as `ip.fp + rp / (10^a (10^b - 1))`, decimal expansions come from number theory (factors 2 and 5 of the denominator give the digits before the block, the multiplicative order of 10 gives the block length), the rounded-repeating approximation is found by brute force over denominators, the largest-denominator approximation is `Fraction.limit_denominator`, and the difference is formatted with `Decimal`. 276 terminating decimals, 35 planted rounded-repeating cases (up to 40 digits), 211 repeating decimals, 403 fractions, mixed numbers and whole numbers, 130 percentages, 50 scientific-notation inputs, 92 limit-denominator cases, 84 scientific-notation formats, 104 rounding cases and 60 invalid inputs, plus the product-note table (0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.375, 0.625, 0.75, 1.25, 2.5), 1/3 as a repeating decimal, 37.5% and 400-digit limits. Every long-division row is checked to be an exact division and to spell the decimal digits.
- Docs and pages: version 1.99.53 in `engine.js`, `sw.js`, `ROADMAP.md`, `STANDARDS.md`, `docs/API.md` and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md`; page counts in `README.md`, `DEPLOY.md`, `ROADMAP.md` and the hard-coded counts in `tests/run_tests.js` (one more page; nine standalone tools instead of eight); CI runs `tests/fraction_convert_test.js`.
- Not in this release (later modes of the same note): Mixed Number Calculator and the Advanced Fraction & Ratio Tools (comparison, LCD, LCM, GCD, ratio, continued fractions); engineering fraction conversions (fractional inches to mm) are Phase 3.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 624 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool, not a registry calculator). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.53.

## v1.99.54
- **Fraction Calculator, Mode 04 (Mixed Number Calculator).** New standalone page `calculators/mixed-number-calculator/`, built by `tools/fraction_mixed_page.js` from the same `tools/fraction_engine.js` as Modes 01 to 03 (new `mixedCalc`) and `tools/fraction_mixed_client.js`, listed in `STANDALONE_TOOLS` in `tools/build_pages.js` and cached by `sw.js`. The mode strip (`tools/fraction_modes.js`) now links Modes 01 to 04 on all four pages. It follows the Mode 04 section of the Fraction Calculator CEO decision note: mixed number to improper fraction, improper fraction to mixed number, addition, subtraction, multiplication and division of mixed numbers, and negative mixed numbers, with the working shown step by step (the note's example, 2 1/3 − 5/6 = 7/3 − 5/6 = 14/6 − 5/6 = 9/6 = 3/2 = 1 1/2, is a fixed test).
- Input: three boxes (whole, numerator, denominator) for each number with an operation between them, or one expression field (`2 1/3 − 5/6`); the two stay in step, a clean expression fills the boxes and the boxes rewrite the expression. Leave the second number empty to convert the first. Accepted: whole numbers, proper and improper fractions, mixed numbers (`2 1/3` or `2_1/3`), a sign at the front only (a minus belongs to the whole mixed number, so `-1 3/4` is −7/4), a fraction part that is itself improper (`2 7/3` = 13/3, flagged in the working), the operators + − × (also x, * and the middle dot) and ÷ (or a colon). Limits: 400 digits a number, 2,000 characters, one operation between two numbers. Errors: zero denominator, division by zero (also by `0 0/2`), decimals and percentages (pointed to Mode 03), brackets and more than one operation (pointed to Mode 01), a sign after the fraction bar (`3/-4`), a second fraction bar, two bare numbers with no bar, commas, a missing number after the operator. A minus sign on the numerator beside a whole number is refused on the page, because `1 -3/4` would silently read as 1 − 3/4.
- Output: lowest-terms fraction (labelled improper when it is), mixed number, decimal and percentage, each with an exact or repeating flag; the first and second numbers as improper fractions when they were mixed; a task line (Add, Subtract, Multiply, Divide, or the conversion direction). Working: the Mode 01 text for the improper-fraction conversion and the operation (least common denominator, cancelling before multiplying, the reciprocal), then a last step that divides numerator by denominator (`3 ÷ 2 = 1 remainder 1, so 3/2 = 1 1/2`) or says the result is proper or whole.
- Tests: `tests/fraction_mixed_test.js` (17,991 checks, run in CI) compares the engine with `tests/fraction_mixed_vectors.json`, which `tests/gen_fraction_mixed_vectors.py` writes by building every operand from its parts (sign, whole, numerator, denominator), taking its value and the result with Python `fractions.Fraction`, and writing the text in several spellings (hyphen, minus sign and en dash; ×, x, * and the middle dot; ÷ and a colon; underscore; extra spaces) so the engine's reading of the text is checked against the parts (436 operations, 235 conversions, 40 results from 20 to 300 digits, 49 invalid inputs). Decimal expansions come from number theory, not long division, and the mixed number is written separately. The test also checks that every improper result has its division step and that every field the page fills from an expression (sign, whole, numerator, denominator) is right. Modes 01 to 03 still pass.
- Docs and pages: version 1.99.54 in `engine.js`, `sw.js`, `ROADMAP.md`, `STANDARDS.md`, `docs/API.md` and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md`; page counts in `README.md`, `DEPLOY.md`, `ROADMAP.md` and the hard-coded counts in `tests/run_tests.js` (one more page; ten standalone tools instead of nine); the standalone tool list on the All Tools catalogue, the Calculators hub and the Engineering Mathematics hub shows the new page; CI runs `tests/fraction_mixed_test.js`.
- Not in this release (later modes of the same note): the Advanced Fraction & Ratio Tools (comparison, LCD, LCM, GCD, ratio, continued fractions); engineering fraction conversions (fractional inches to mm) are Phase 3.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 625 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool, not a registry calculator). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.54.

## v1.99.55
- **Fraction Calculator, Mode 05 (Advanced Fraction & Ratio Tools).** New standalone page `calculators/fraction-ratio-tools/`, built by `tools/fraction_adv_page.js` from the same `tools/fraction_engine.js` as Modes 01 to 04 (new `advCalc`) and `tools/fraction_adv_client.js`, listed in `STANDALONE_TOOLS` in `tools/build_pages.js` and cached by `sw.js`. The mode strip (`tools/fraction_modes.js`) now links Modes 01 to 05 on all five pages. It follows the Mode 05 section of the Fraction Calculator CEO decision note (compare fractions, LCD and LCM, ratios), with GCD and proportion solving added.
- Tools (one tab each, one input field): **GCD & LCM** of 2 to 20 whole numbers (commas, semicolons or spaces; signs ignored; 0 refused), with the Euclidean algorithm for each step, the LCM one number at a time, a GCD x LCM check for two numbers and the prime factors when every number has at most 15 digits. **LCD** of 2 to 20 values (fractions, mixed numbers, decimals, percentages, repeating decimals): each value is reduced first, so 2/4 and 1/3 give 6, not 12; the table shows the multiplier and the equivalent fraction over the LCD. **Compare** 2 to 12 values: cross-multiplication for two, the LCD and numerators for a list; sorted order with ties kept equal (ranks 1, 2, 2, 4), smallest, largest and their difference. **Ratio** of 2 to 8 terms (colons or the word to; fractions and decimals cleared by the LCM of the denominators, then divided by the GCD), the parts as shares and percentages, the ratio scaled so the first term is 1, and an optional total shared in the ratio. **Proportion** a : b = c : d with one x in any place, by cross-multiplication, with a check line. Limits: 400 digits a number, 2,000 characters; errors for empty items, zero denominators, negative ratio terms, a known proportion term of 0, more or fewer terms than allowed and a bad total.
- Tests: `tests/fraction_adv_test.js` (17,201 checks, run in CI) compares the engine with `tests/fraction_adv_vectors.json`, which `tests/gen_fraction_adv_vectors.py` writes by routes that differ from the engine's: GCD and LCM of small numbers from prime exponents (minimum and maximum), cross-checked against `math.gcd` and `math.lcm`; 60 numbers of up to 40 digits built as a known GCD times random cofactors; every fraction a `fractions.Fraction` written in several spellings (unreduced, mixed, decimal); ratios scaled with Fraction arithmetic; each proportion built from a planted fourth term (d = b c / a) with one term hidden, so no solving formula is shared. 220 GCD/LCM lists, 60 long lists, 240 LCD lists, 260 comparisons, 240 ratios, 240 proportions, 43 invalid inputs; each result is also checked for divisibility, lowest terms, equal value of every equivalent fraction and the step text.
- Docs and pages: version 1.99.55 in `engine.js`, `sw.js`, `ROADMAP.md`, `STANDARDS.md`, `docs/API.md` and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md`; page counts in `README.md`, `DEPLOY.md`, `ROADMAP.md` and the hard-coded counts in `tests/run_tests.js` (one more page; eleven standalone tools instead of ten); the standalone tool list on the All Tools catalogue, the Calculators hub and the Engineering Mathematics hub shows the new page; CI runs `tests/fraction_adv_test.js`.
- Not in this release: continued fractions and the engineering fraction conversions (fractional inches to mm), which are Phase 3. All five modes of the Fraction Calculator note now exist.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 626 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool, not a registry calculator). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.55.

## v1.99.56
- **Fraction Calculator, Mode 06 (Fractional Inch to mm Converter; the "fractional inch conversion" of Phase 3 in the Fraction Calculator CEO decision note).** New standalone page `calculators/fractional-inch-converter/`, built by `tools/fraction_inch_page.js` from the same `tools/fraction_engine.js` as Modes 01 to 05 (new `inchCalc`, exported with `MM_PER_INCH = '127/5'`) and `tools/fraction_inch_client.js`, listed in `STANDALONE_TOOLS` in `tools/build_pages.js` and cached by `sw.js`. The mode strip (`tools/fraction_modes.js`) now links Modes 01 to 06 on all six pages.
- Inch to mm: a fraction, mixed number (a space or the drawing style `1-3/16`) or decimal, with an optional `in`, `inch`, `inches` or `"`. One inch is exactly 25.4 mm = 127/5 mm, so the result is an exact fraction of a millimetre and a finite or repeating decimal; only the display to N decimal places (default 3) is rounded, and that is flagged, with the exact value always listed. Also gives centimetres, decimal inches, the mixed number, whether the denominator is a power of 2 (a mark on an inch rule) and thousandths of an inch.
- mm to inch: exact inches (mm x 5/127, usually repeating), then the closest mark on a 1/2, 1/4, 1/8, 1/16, 1/32, 1/64, 1/128 or 1/256 inch rule, as the nearest mark (halves away from zero), the next mark not above or the next mark not below; reduced to lowest terms and as a mixed number; the error in mm and in inches with its sign; the fraction converted back to mm exactly; and a table of the same length at every graduation up to the chosen one.
- Refused: the wrong unit on a tab (mm on the inch tab, in on the mm tab), percentages, empty or malformed numbers, more than 400 digits, a bad number of decimal places (0 to 20), graduation or rounding.
- Tests: `tests/fraction_inch_test.js` (21,083 checks, run in CI) compares the engine with `tests/fraction_inch_vectors.json`, which `tests/gen_fraction_inch_vectors.py` writes by routes that differ from the engine's: the factor is read from the string `25.4` with `fractions.Fraction(Decimal('25.4'))`, decimal expansions come from the `decimal` module, a graduation is found by comparing the two neighbouring marks with Fraction arithmetic (`math.floor` and `math.ceil` for down and up), and tie cases are planted (a length exactly half way between two marks). 386 inch lengths, 556 mm cases (every graduation, all three roundings), 26 invalid inputs; each result is also checked for the error bound (half a graduation for nearest, under one graduation otherwise), the direction of down and up, a round trip through the other tab and the table rows. Vectors regenerate byte for byte.
- Docs and pages: version 1.99.56 in `engine.js`, `sw.js`, `ROADMAP.md`, `STANDARDS.md`, `docs/API.md` and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md`; page counts in `README.md`, `DEPLOY.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, the committed `sitemap.xml` and the hard-coded counts in `tests/run_tests.js` (one more page; twelve standalone tools instead of eleven); the standalone tool list on the All Tools catalogue, the Calculators hub and the Engineering Mathematics hub shows the new page; CI runs `tests/fraction_inch_test.js`.
- Not in this release: continued fractions, exact rational support for matrix operations and professional calculation reports (rest of Phase 3). A length conversion does not establish a tolerance, fit or nominal trade size; the page says so.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 627 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool, not a registry calculator). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.56.

## v1.99.57
- **Fraction Calculator, Mode 05 gains a Continued fraction tool (the "Continued Fraction Converter" in the Fraction Calculator CEO decision note, Phase 3 of the roadmap).** New tool `continued` in `advCalc` (`tools/fraction_engine.js`), a sixth tab on `calculators/fraction-ratio-tools/` built by `tools/fraction_adv_page.js` and `tools/fraction_adv_client.js`. No new page and no new URL.
- A fraction, mixed number, decimal or percentage gives its continued fraction `[a0; a1, a2, ...]` by the Euclidean algorithm on exact BigInt fractions (decimals are read exactly, so 3.14159 is 314159/100000, not pi), with the division lines, every convergent h(k)/k(k) from the recurrence h(k) = a(k) h(k-1) + h(k-2), its decimal and its signed error. A negative number takes a whole part rounded down, so every later term is positive. A continued fraction typed in brackets, such as `[3; 7, 15, 1]`, gives its exact value by working from the right, and the standard form (last term above 1) is shown with a note when it differs.
- Refused: empty input, zero denominators, non-integer or non-positive later terms, a missing semicolon or bracket, more than 60 typed terms, more than 400 digits per number.
- Tests: `tests/fraction_adv_test.js` now has 21,055 checks (run in CI), against 248 expansion vectors and 120 build vectors in `tests/fraction_adv_vectors.json`, written by `tests/gen_fraction_adv_vectors.py` by a route that differs from the engine's: `math.floor` and reciprocal on `fractions.Fraction`, and each convergent evaluated as a nested fraction from the right on the truncated expansion (no shared recurrence). Earlier vectors regenerate unchanged. Each result is also checked for the last convergent equal to the value, positive later terms, a last term of 2 or more and the convergent row count.
- Docs and pages: version 1.99.57 in `engine.js`, `sw.js`, `ROADMAP.md`, `STANDARDS.md`, `docs/API.md` and `docs/REVIEW_PACK.md`; the five other fraction pages are rebuilt so they carry the same engine.
- Not in this release: exact rational support for matrix operations (the Matrix Calculator already uses exact BigInt fractions) and professional calculation reports (rest of Phase 3). A continued fraction of a typed decimal is the expansion of that decimal, not of an irrational constant; the page says so.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 627 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.57.

## v1.99.58
- **Fraction Calculator, Phase 3: professional calculation reports (the last open item of Phase 3 in the Fraction Calculator CEO decision note).** A "Calculation report" card on all six Fraction Calculator pages (Modes 01 to 06), from one shared script `tools/fraction_report.js`, inlined with its CSS and card by `tools/fraction_report_install.js`, which every fraction page builder runs as its last step (idempotent). No new page and no new URL.
- The report is read from the page's own input fields, selected tab, result, tables and working, so it can never disagree with the calculator and Modes 01 to 06 need no second calculation path. It lists project, reference, prepared by, checked by and notes (the first three are remembered in this browser only), the input, the result, the tables, the numbered working, a method and limits paragraph (exact arithmetic; a number is not engineering accuracy; units, tolerances and measurement precision still apply) and a report number FR-date-hash, where the hash is a checksum of the input and result so two sheets with the same number show the same calculation.
- Output: a live preview, Print or save as PDF (a print rule hides the rest of the page), Copy as text and Save as .txt. Nothing is sent anywhere; a report is refused with a message when the calculator holds no valid result.
- Phase 3 status: fractional inch conversion (v1.99.56), engineering ratio tools (Advanced tool, 240 : 12 = 20 : 1), measurement representation (inch tab), continued fractions (v1.99.57), exact rational matrix support (the Matrix Calculator has used exact BigInt fractions since Division 02) and calculation reports (this release). Integration with the unit converters is the Fractional Inch to mm Converter; the standing converters in `converters/` are unchanged.
- Tests: `tests/fraction_report_test.js` (static, no browser, run in CI): one card, style and script per page, every field and button the script reads, the print rule, the card inside `main`, the inlined script equal to the source, no remote resource, installer idempotent. The report was also exercised in a DOM simulation on every page (all six modes, the continued fraction tab, invalid input) during development; that harness is not part of the repo because the repo has no dependencies.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 627 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.58.

## v1.99.59
- **Percentage Calculator, Mode 01 (Basic Percentage).** New standalone page `calculators/basic-percentage-calculator/`, built by `tools/percent_basic_page.js` from `tools/percent_engine.js` (BigInt rational arithmetic, no DOM) and `tools/percent_basic_client.js`, listed in `STANDALONE_TOOLS` in `tools/build_pages.js`, in `sitemap.xml` and cached by `sw.js`. It follows the "Percentage Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note (five modes; this is the first release). The existing registry calculators `percentage-calculator` (percentage change, increase and decrease from two numbers) and `percentage-error` are unchanged, and the page links to both from its mode strip.
- Engine: a value is a pair of BigInt with a positive denominator, reduced by the greatest common divisor; no floating-point number is used. Three questions: X% of Y (`Y x X / 100`), X is what % of Y (`X / Y x 100`) and X is Y% of what (`X / (Y / 100)`, the reverse calculation), each with the formula, the numbers substituted into it, the numbered working and a check that runs the answer back. Plus four conversions: percent to decimal, decimal to percent, fraction to percent and percent to fraction (lowest terms). Decimals are read exactly, negative values keep their sign (with a plain-language note on a negative percentage, on opposite signs and on a result above 100%), a trailing % is accepted where a percentage is expected, and a decimal percentage such as 12.5% becomes 1/8 exactly.
- Output: Automatic decimal places show the exact decimal when it ends and otherwise six places marked approximate with the exact fraction beside it; a chosen number of places (0 to 12) rounds half away from zero, never shows a negative zero, and says when the value is rounded. Limits: 400 digits a number. Refused with a message: Y = 0 in "what percentage" (division by 0), Y = 0% in "of what" (any number, or none), a zero denominator, commas, exponent notation, a % sign in the wrong field, more than one slash, more than 400 digits. Empty fields give a hint, not an error.
- Interface: four calculation buttons, a live question line, labelled fields, decimal places, example chips (including cable loading, 150 A of 200 A), a result table, copy of the result, the sentence or the whole calculation with working, save as .txt, print, and a history of the last ten calculations kept in this browser only. Worked-example and engineering tables on the page are computed by the engine at build time. The shared Phase 3 calculation report (`tools/fraction_report.js`) is Fraction Calculator text and is not installed on this page.
- Tests: `tests/percent_basic_test.js` (16,135 checks, run in CI) compares the engine with `tests/percent_basic_vectors.json`, which `tests/gen_percent_basic_vectors.py` writes from Python `fractions.Fraction` (exact answer) and `decimal.Decimal` (display text, ROUND_HALF_UP at 3,000 digits): 987 calculations (named cases, 900 random with decimals, negatives and 12-digit numbers, spelling variants), 9 of 100 to 380 digits, 403 conversions, 41 invalid inputs and the empty-input flags, plus the fixed cases from the product note (20% of 150 = 30; 45 of 180 = 25%; 45 is 25% of 180; 0.75 = 75%; 3/4 = 75%; 225 V against 230 V = 97.826%; 920 after a 20% discount = 1,150). The engine agreed with every vector at the first run; the first test run failed only on two faults in the test itself (a swapped pair in one hand-written generator case, and expected values that left out the approximate sign on rounded results). The page was also driven in headless Chromium: every calculation and conversion, error and empty states, history, the .txt download, no console errors, no horizontal scroll at 380 px.
- Not in this release (later modes of the same note): Mode 02 Increase & Decrease, Mode 03 Percentage Difference & Change (including percentage points and a zero starting value), Mode 04 Percentage Error as a mode of this platform, Mode 05 Engineering Percentage Analysis, and a percentage-specific calculation report.
