- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 628 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool, not a registry calculator). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.59.

## v1.99.60
- **Percentage Calculator, Mode 02 (Increase & Decrease).** New standalone page `calculators/percentage-increase-decrease-calculator/`, built by `tools/percent_change_page.js` from `tools/percent_change_engine.js` and `tools/percent_change_client.js`, listed in `STANDALONE_TOOLS` in `tools/build_pages.js`, in `sitemap.xml` and cached by `sw.js`. It follows the "Percentage Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note, Mode 02. The mode strip (`tools/percent_modes.js`) now lists Modes 01 and 02, so the Mode 01 page was rebuilt to show the new link; nothing else on it changed.
- Engine: exact BigInt rational arithmetic, no floating point, built only on the public API of `tools/percent_engine.js` (`parseNum`, `make`, `fmt`), so Mode 01 and its tests are untouched. Five questions: increase a value (`V x (1 + P/100)`), decrease a value (`V x (1 - P/100)`), percentage change from an old to a new value (`(New - Old) / |Old| x 100`, labelled increase, decrease or no change, which covers the note's "percentage increase" and "percentage decrease"), original before an increase (`New / (1 + P/100)`) and original before a decrease or discount (`New / (1 - P/100)`).
- Behaviour worth knowing: each increase or decrease result shows the change needed to get back to the start (a 25% rise needs a 20% fall; a 25% fall needs a 33.333333% rise); each reverse result shows the wrong shortcut beside the right answer (920 after a 20% discount is 1,150, not 1,104). A zero old value, a reverse of a 100% decrease and a reverse of a -100% increase are refused with a message. A negative percentage, a decrease above 100%, a negative old value and a change across zero are accepted with a plain-language warning. For a negative old value the change is divided by its size, so a rise is always an increase; the page says so.
- Tests: `tests/percent_change_test.js` (22,762 checks, run in CI) compares the engine with `tests/percent_change_vectors.json`, which `tests/gen_percent_change_vectors.py` writes from Python `fractions.Fraction` and `decimal.Decimal` (1,242 calculations including 1,100 random ones, 15 of 100 to 380 digits, 35 refused inputs and 7 empty-input flags, plus the note's cases: 1,000 up 15% = 1,150; 920 after a 20% discount = 1,150; 80 to 100 = +25%; 100 to 80 = -20%). It also checks 1,500 exact round trips: reversing an increase or a decrease returns the start, and the percentage change from V to V changed by P is P.
- Not in this release: Mode 03 Percentage Difference & Change (the symmetric difference, percentage points, a zero starting value), Mode 04 Percentage Error as a mode of this platform, Mode 05 Engineering Percentage Analysis, and a percentage-specific calculation report card.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 629 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool, not a registry calculator). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.60.

## v1.99.61
- **Percentage Calculator, Mode 03 (Percentage Difference & Change).** New standalone page `calculators/percentage-difference-calculator/`, built by `tools/percent_diff_page.js` from `tools/percent_diff_engine.js` and `tools/percent_diff_client.js`, listed in `STANDALONE_TOOLS` in `tools/build_pages.js`, in `sitemap.xml` and cached by `sw.js`. It follows the "Percentage Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note, Mode 03. The mode strip (`tools/percent_modes.js`) now lists Modes 01 to 03, so the Mode 01 and Mode 02 pages were rebuilt to show the new link; nothing else on them changed.
- Engine: exact BigInt rational arithmetic, no floating point, built only on the public API of `tools/percent_engine.js`, so Modes 01 and 02 and their tests are untouched. Four questions: percentage difference between A and B, treating them equally (`|A - B| / ((|A| + |B|) / 2) x 100`, the same whichever is called A), percentage change from A to B (`(B - A) / |A| x 100`, labelled increase, decrease or no change), A compared with B (`(A - B) / |B| x 100`, labelled higher, lower or equal, B is the reference) and two percentages (change in percentage points `P2 - P1`, with the relative change `(P2 - P1) / |P1| x 100` beside it).
- Behaviour worth knowing: the symmetric difference is never above 200% and is exactly 200% when one value is 0 or the signs are opposite (the page says so). Both values 0 (0 / 0), an old value of 0 in "change" and a reference value of 0 in "higher or lower" are refused with a message. In percentage points, a first percentage of 0 is accepted: the points are given and the relative change is shown as not defined. Each result table also shows the neighbouring answers (percentage change both ways beside the difference; the symmetric difference and the reverse comparison beside a change), so the choice of reference is visible. Negative values are accepted with a plain-language note; a percentage below 0 or above 100 gets a note in the points question. The page has a section on difference against change and one on points against relative change.
- Tests: `tests/percent_diff_test.js` (33,059 checks, run in CI) compares the engine with `tests/percent_diff_vectors.json`, which `tests/gen_percent_diff_vectors.py` writes from Python `fractions.Fraction` and `decimal.Decimal` (1,330 calculations including 1,200 random ones, 12 of 100 to 380 digits, 32 refused inputs and 7 empty-input flags). It also checks 1,500 random pairs against exact properties (symmetry, never above 200%, never negative, B against A equals the change from A to B, points from P1 to P2 are minus the points from P2 to P1) and against the Mode 02 engine, an independent code route for the same percentage change.
- Not in this release: Mode 04 Percentage Error as a mode of this platform, Mode 05 Engineering Percentage Analysis, and a percentage-specific calculation report card.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 630 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool, not a registry calculator). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.61.

## v1.99.62
- **Percentage Calculator, Mode 04 (Percentage Error).** New standalone page `calculators/percentage-error-calculator/`, built by `tools/percent_error_page.js` from `tools/percent_error_engine.js` and `tools/percent_error_client.js`, listed in `STANDALONE_TOOLS` in `tools/build_pages.js`, in `sitemap.xml` and cached by `sw.js`. It follows the "Percentage Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note, Mode 04. The mode strip (`tools/percent_modes.js`) now lists Modes 01 to 04, so the Mode 01 to 03 pages were rebuilt to show the new link; nothing else on them changed. The older registry calculator `calculators/percentage-error/` (one reading, floating point, unchanged) stays and is linked from the strip as "Quick percentage error (one reading)".
- Engine: exact BigInt rational arithmetic, no floating point, built only on the public API of `tools/percent_engine.js`, so Modes 01 to 03 and their tests are untouched. Three questions: the percentage error of a measured value M against a reference R (`|M - R| / |R| x 100`, with the error, absolute error, relative error, signed deviation, direction above or below, accuracy `100% - error` shown only up to 100%, and an optional tolerance in % of the reference that gives the permitted range, a within/outside verdict and the margin in percentage points; the limit itself counts as within), the measured value for a reference and a signed percentage error (`R + |R| x p / 100`), and the reference for a measured value and a signed percentage error (`M / (1 + p / 100)`, or `M / (1 - p / 100)` for a negative M; the reference is taken to have the same sign as M).
- Behaviour worth knowing: a reference of 0 is refused (error and "find the reading"); a measured value of 0 is refused in "find the reference", as is a percentage error that would put the reading at 0 or across zero; a negative tolerance is refused. Accepted with a plain-language note: a negative reference (divided by its size), opposite signs, a zero reading (a 100% error), and an error above 100% (accuracy shown as "not meaningful"). The result table also shows the neighbouring answers that are often confused with the percentage error: the reading as a percentage of the reference, the Mode 03 percentage difference, and the error divided by the reading instead of the reference. The page explains each and has sections on the terms, tolerance, working backwards, and difference against error against change.
- Tests: `tests/percent_error_test.js` (47,528 checks, run in CI) compares the engine with `tests/percent_error_vectors.json`, which `tests/gen_percent_error_vectors.py` writes from Python `fractions.Fraction` and `decimal.Decimal` (1,593 calculations including 1,500 random ones, 12 of 100 to 380 digits, 42 refused inputs and 7 empty-input flags). It also checks 1,500 random pairs against exact properties: the percentage error is the size of the Mode 02 change from reference to reading; the Mode 03 comparison gives the same signed number (separate code routes); direction follows the sign; the three questions undo each other exactly (reference and error give back the reading, reading and error give back the reference, run on references whose percentage ends as a decimal, and the test fails if too few such pairs ran); and the tolerance verdict matches a separate exact computation. Three deliberate engine bugs (wrong divisor, strict instead of inclusive limit, wrong sign in the reverse) were each caught by the suite.
- Not in this release: Mode 05 Engineering Percentage Analysis (efficiency, voltage deviation, cable loading, derating, load factor, energy savings) and a percentage-specific calculation report card.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 631 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool, not a registry calculator). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.62.

## v1.99.63
- **Percentage Calculator, Mode 05 (Engineering Percentage Analysis).** New standalone page `calculators/engineering-percentage-calculator/`, built by `tools/percent_eng_page.js` from `tools/percent_eng_engine.js` and `tools/percent_eng_client.js`, listed in `STANDALONE_TOOLS`, `sitemap.xml` and `sw.js`. The mode strip now lists Modes 01 to 05, so the Mode 01 to 04 pages were rebuilt to show the new link; nothing else on them changed.
- Engine: exact BigInt rational arithmetic, built only on the public API of `tools/percent_engine.js`. Eleven calculations, each with its own definition and an explicitly named reference (the 100%): efficiency (output / input), loading (actual / rated capacity, for cables, transformers and feeders), voltage drop (drop / source voltage), voltage deviation (signed, against nominal), load factor (average / peak), demand factor (maximum demand / connected load), diversity factor (sum of individual maxima / group maximum, a ratio with no percent sign, plus the coincidence factor), derating (signed, against rated), energy saving (signed, against previous consumption), battery state of charge (remaining / usable) and power loss against a reference power the user names. Every result table has a row that marks the reference quantity, and the steps start by naming it.
- Behaviour worth knowing: inputs are plain non-negative numbers in one consistent unit. A negative input is refused, and a reference of 0 is refused with a message that names the quantity. Accepted with a plain-language note when the definition is breached: efficiency above 100% (output larger than input), loading above 100% (overload), a voltage drop larger than the source, an average load above the peak, a demand factor above 100%, a diversity factor below 1, a derating that is an increase, a saving that is an increase, a state of charge above 100%, and a loss larger than its reference. Signed results (voltage deviation, derating, energy saving) show a direction. The page does only the arithmetic; limits from standards, regulations or manufacturer data are for the user to check.
- Tests: `tests/percent_eng_test.js` (67,154 checks, run in CI) compares the engine with `tests/percent_eng_vectors.json`, which `tests/gen_percent_eng_vectors.py` writes from Python `fractions.Fraction` and `decimal.Decimal` from the definitions in the CEO note (2,297 calculations including 2,200 random ones, 12 of 100 to 380 digits, 167 refused inputs, 33 empty-input flags). It also checks the worked examples of the note (96%, 75%, 4%, 25%, 2.174%), that every row label is unique and exactly one row names the reference, 1,500 random pairs against the Mode 02 and Mode 04 engines (separate code for the same percentages), exact complement identities (efficiency plus loss share is 100%, state of charge plus depletion is 100%), and result times reference giving back the numerator. Three deliberate engine bugs were each caught by the suite. The suite also found a real defect while it was being written: the voltage-drop input row and its result row had the same label, so the result row is now "Voltage drop percentage".
- Not in this release: a percentage-specific calculation report card, and the reverse and basic-percentage entry points the CEO note proposes under `/math/` (the site keeps the existing `calculators/` structure).
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 632 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.63.

## v1.99.64
- **Percentage Calculator: professional calculation report card on all five pages (Modes 01 to 05).** A "Calculation report" card from one shared script `tools/percent_report.js`, inlined with its CSS and card by `tools/percent_report_install.js`, which every percentage page builder (`percent_basic_page.js`, `percent_change_page.js`, `percent_diff_page.js`, `percent_error_page.js`, `percent_eng_page.js`) runs as its last step (idempotent). No new page and no new URL. It is adapted from the Fraction Calculator report (v1.99.58), which stays unchanged.
- The report is read from the page's own input fields, selected calculation, result, formula, tables and working, so it can never disagree with the calculator and the five modes need no second calculation path. It lists project, document reference, prepared by, checked by and notes (project, prepared by and checked by are remembered in this browser only), then: the input; the result with its flags (exact or rounded, above or below, within or outside tolerance) and the statement, plus the named reference quantity in Mode 05; the formula and the user's numbers in it; the result tables; the numbered working; any note the calculator raised (for example an efficiency above 100%); the user's notes; a method and limits paragraph (exact arithmetic; a percentage is only meaningful against a named reference; percentage points are not a relative change; a number is not engineering accuracy; limits from standards or manufacturer data still apply); and a report number PR-date-hash, where the hash is a checksum of the calculation, the inputs and the result, so two sheets with the same number show the same calculation and a changed input changes it.
- Output: a live preview, Print or save as PDF (a print rule hides the rest of the page), Copy as text and Save as .txt named by the report number. Nothing is sent anywhere. A report is refused with a message when the calculator holds no valid result. The sheet's "Document reference" field is named that way so it is not confused with the reference quantity of a percentage.
- Tests: `tests/percent_report_test.js` (static, no browser, run in CI): one card, style and script per page, every field and button the script reads, the print rule, the card inside `main`, the inlined script equal to the source, the page has the containers the report reads, no remote resource, no leftover fraction report, installer idempotent. The report was also exercised in headless Chromium on all five pages during development (invalid input refused with a message, valid report with project and notes, stable report number for the same calculation and a different number after a changed input, Mode 04 tolerance and Mode 05 reference lines present, download named by the report number, print layout showing only the sheet, no console errors, no sideways scroll); that harness is not part of the repo because the repo has no dependencies.
- Not in this release: Phase 2 items that are not Modes 01 to 05 (sequential percentage changes, discount and markup, original price), and the `/math/` restructure proposed in the CEO note.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 7,112 vectors, 221 calculator pages, 632 sitemap URLs. Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.64.

## v1.99.65
- **Percent Error Calculator, Mode 01 (Absolute Percent Error).** New standalone page `calculators/percent-error-calculator/`, built by `tools/pe_page.js` from `tools/pe_engine.js`, `tools/pe_client.js` and `tools/pe_modes.js`, following the CEO product decision for a Measurement Verification & Accuracy Analysis tool (five modes; this release is Mode 01 only). Inputs: observed value, reference value, an optional unit label (a free-text field with common units suggested; label only, nothing is converted) and decimal places. Results in the order of the product brief: observed value, reference value, signed error, absolute error, relative error magnitude, percentage error, measurement direction (below, above or equal), plus the exact fraction when the percentage does not end, the formula, the user's numbers in it, numbered working and a check line.
- `tools/pe_engine.js` is a thin layer over `tools/percent_error_engine.js` (exact BigInt rational arithmetic, kind `err`, unchanged), so Mode 01 and the Percentage Calculator Mode 04 can never disagree on the percentage; the test compares them on 600 random pairs. A reference of 0 is refused (a percentage of 0 is undefined). A negative reference divides by its size, with a note. The page states that percent error is not instrument accuracy and that error, uncertainty, tolerance and accuracy are separate ideas.
- Outputs: copy result, copy sentence, copy with working, save as .txt, save as .csv (cells starting with = + - @ are prefixed so a spreadsheet does not run them), print, a ten-item history kept only in this browser, and the shared calculation report card (`tools/percent_report_install.js`, now also run on this page). Modes 02 to 05 are shown in the mode strip as plain text marked "coming", not as links.
- This page does not replace Percentage Calculator Mode 04 (`calculators/percentage-error-calculator/`), which keeps the reverse questions (find the reading or the reference) and the percentage tolerance check; the two pages link to each other.
- Tests: `tests/pe_test.js` with 444 independent vectors from `tests/gen_pe_vectors.py` (Python fractions and decimal), the brief's cases (225/230, 235/230, 100/100, 7/9, 56.891/62.327, 9.8/10, -9/-10, reference 0), unit-label rules, and 600 random cross-checks; static checks of the built page. `tests/percent_report_test.js` now covers six pages. Added to the CI workflow.
- Not in this release: Mode 02 (signed error analysis), Mode 03 (tolerance verification), Mode 04 (batch measurement analysis with CSV import and a chart), Mode 05 (engineering measurement analysis), the `/math/` and `/engineering/measurement-analysis/` URL structure proposed in the brief (the page uses the existing `calculators/<slug>/` pattern), and measurement uncertainty.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 221 calculator pages, 633 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.65.

## v1.99.66
- **Percent Error Calculator, Mode 02 (Signed Error Analysis).** New standalone page `calculators/signed-error-analysis/`, built by `tools/pe_signed_page.js` from `tools/pe_signed_engine.js`, `tools/pe_signed_client.js` and the shared `tools/pe_modes.js`, following the CEO product decision (Mode 02 of five). Inputs as in Mode 01: observed value, reference value, a unit label (label only, nothing is converted) and decimal places. Results: observed and reference values, signed error (observed minus reference, explicit + or -), absolute error, signed relative error, signed percentage deviation, measurement direction, the correction (reference minus observed) and the sign convention in use, plus the exact fraction when the percentage does not end, the formula, the user's numbers in it, six numbered steps with a check.
- Convention, documented on the page: the signed error follows the BIPM VIM definition (measured value minus reference value), and the percentage divides by the SIZE of the reference, so a reading numerically above the reference is always positive, also for a negative reference (-9 against -10 gives +10%). A reference of 0 is refused. The page shows the other conventions a reader may meet (reference minus observed, division by the observed value, division by a signed reference) so results from other sources can be compared.
- `tools/pe_signed_engine.js` is a thin layer over `tools/pe_engine.js` (Mode 01, unchanged), so the two modes cannot disagree on the size of an error; the test compares them on every vector. Outputs, history, report card, copy and save buttons are the same as Mode 01 (`tools/pe_signed_client.js` is derived from `tools/pe_client.js`). The mode strip on both pages now links Mode 01 and Mode 02; Modes 03 to 05 stay plain text marked "coming".
- Tests: `tests/pe_signed_test.js` with 448 independent vectors from `tests/gen_pe_signed_vectors.py` (Python fractions and decimal), the brief's cases (+2.173913%, 0%, -2.173913%), negative references, the sign rules, 600 random swap and correction properties, and static checks of the built page. `tests/percent_report_test.js` now covers seven pages. Added to the CI workflow.
- Not in this release: Mode 03 (tolerance verification), Mode 04 (batch analysis with CSV import and a chart), Mode 05 (engineering measurement analysis), the `/math/` and `/engineering/measurement-analysis/` URL structure in the brief, and measurement uncertainty. Known from v1.99.65 and not changed: the shared report card's report-number header is clipped at 390 px width.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 221 calculator pages, 634 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.66.

## v1.99.67
- **Percent Error Calculator, Mode 03 (Tolerance Verification).** New standalone page `calculators/tolerance-verification/`, built by `tools/pe_tol_page.js` from `tools/pe_tol_engine.js`, `tools/pe_tol_client.js` and the shared `tools/pe_modes.js`, following the CEO product decision (Mode 03 of five). Inputs: observed value, reference value, a unit label (label only, nothing is converted), a tolerance basis (percentage of the reference, or an absolute value in the unit of the readings), a tolerance shape (symmetrical, or asymmetrical with a lower and an upper tolerance) and decimal places for percentages. Tolerances are entered as sizes without a sign.
- Results: lower limit, upper limit and permitted range; permitted deviation (an absolute tolerance is also shown as a percentage of the reference); signed error and measured deviation (the Mode 02 figures); assessment (within the entered tolerance, on a limit, or outside it above or below); margin to the lower and upper limit (positive inside, negative beyond); nearest limit; tolerance used (size of the error divided by the tolerance on its side of the reference); formula, the user's numbers in it, six numbered steps, a scope row, copy, .txt/.csv, print, history and the report card as in Modes 01 and 02. The brief's case: 225 V against 230 V with 5% gives 218.5 V to 241.5 V, within the entered tolerance, deviation -2.173913%.
- Rules, documented on the page: the limits are included (a reading on a limit passes and is flagged); a percentage tolerance uses the size of the reference, so a negative reference works and a reference of 0 is refused for a percentage tolerance; an absolute tolerance accepts a reference of 0 and the percentage deviation is then shown as not defined. The page states that the tolerance is the one entered, that it reads no standard or datasheet, that measurement uncertainty and guard bands are not applied, and that a pass is never described as compliant, calibrated or approved. A separate section distinguishes error, tolerance, uncertainty and accuracy.
- `tools/pe_tol_engine.js` uses the public API of `tools/percent_engine.js` and calls Mode 02 (`tools/pe_signed_engine.js`, unchanged) for the signed deviation, so Modes 02 and 03 cannot disagree; the test compares them on every vector. Exact BigInt rational arithmetic, no floating point. The mode strip on all three pages now links Modes 01 to 03; Modes 04 and 05 stay plain text marked "coming".
- Tests: `tests/pe_tol_test.js` with 704 independent vectors from `tests/gen_pe_tol_vectors.py` (Python fractions and decimal: named cases, asymmetric cases, and for 500 random references and tolerances a value on each limit, one smallest step beyond each limit, the centre and the reference), the brief's case, boundary rules, refusal and empty-input cases, 600 random properties (agreement with Mode 02, a wider tolerance keeps a pass, a negative margin means outside, equal lower and upper tolerances equal the symmetrical result, the same tolerance entered as a value gives the same limits) and static checks of the built page. `tests/percent_report_test.js` now covers eight pages. Added to the CI workflow and README.
- Not in this release: Mode 04 (batch analysis with CSV import and a chart), Mode 05 (engineering measurement analysis), measurement uncertainty and guard bands, the `/math/` and `/engineering/measurement-analysis/` URL structure in the brief. Known from v1.99.65 and not changed: the shared report card's report-number header is clipped at 390 px width.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 221 calculator pages, 635 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool; 20 standalone tools in `STANDALONE_TOOLS`). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.67. Test files that hold page counts as literals were updated (sitemap 338 + conversion pages, 263 cached calculator folders, 20 standalone tools).

## v1.99.68
- **Binary Calculator, Mode 01 (Binary Arithmetic).** New standalone page `calculators/binary-arithmetic-calculator/`, built by `tools/binarith_page.js` from `tools/binarith_engine.js` and `tools/binarith_client.js` (the engine and script are inlined in the page). From the "Binary Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note (Mode 01 of five). Arbitrary-precision (BigInt) add, subtract, multiply and divide of binary numbers: 2 to 20 numbers for add, subtract and multiply (left to right), exactly two for divide; up to 4,096 digits each; optional sign (+, -, minus sign), 0b prefix, leading zeros and grouping spaces; binary-only validation that names the first bad digit and the field (10201 is refused).
- Results: binary, decimal, hexadecimal and octal for every operand and the result, place-value expansion for short operands, bit length, ones and zeros, a bit-by-bit view up to 64 bits, and a "Show result as" control for leading zeros (shortest, match the longest number, or at least 4 to 64 bits; padding only widens and never truncates, and a note says when the result needs more bits). Division reports quotient and remainder (truncated toward zero; remainder takes the sign of the dividend) with the Q x B + R = A check. Copy buttons, TXT, CSV and JSON downloads, a keypad (0, 1, sign, delete, clear), Enter to save, and a 10-item history kept in the browser only.
- Working: carry row for addition, borrow row for subtraction, signed pairs reduced to magnitude add or subtract, partial-product table for multiplication (up to 64 multiplier bits and 256 product bits), long-division table (up to 256 dividend bits). Every written step is checked against exact BigInt arithmetic inside the engine, so a wrong working fails loudly instead of being shown.
- Scope decisions: negative results are shown in sign-and-magnitude with a note pointing to two's complement; fixed-width wrap-around, overflow flags, bitwise and shift operations stay on the existing Binary Calculator and Logic Gates page; entering decimal, hex or octal values is Mode 02 (not in this release).
- Tests: `tests/binarith_test.js` with 1,500 independent vectors from `tests/gen_binarith_vectors.py` (plain Python integers; carry and borrow rows from the identity carry = x ^ y ^ (x + y)), the brief's cases (1010 + 0101 = 1111, 1100 - 0101 = 0111, 101 x 11 = 1111, 1100 / 0011 = 0100 remainder 0, 10 + 11 + 100 = 1001, 10201 refused), signs, zero, padding, limits, exports.
- Not in this release: Mode 02 (number system conversion with fractions and a shared conversion engine), Mode 03 (bitwise display with highlighted bits), Mode 04 (signed binary representations) and Mode 05 (digital electronics tools), and the `/math/` URL structure in the brief.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 221 calculator pages, 636 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool; 21 standalone tools in `STANDALONE_TOOLS`). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.68. Test files that hold page counts as literals were updated (sitemap 339 + conversion pages, 264 cached calculator folders, 21 standalone tools).

## v1.99.69
- **Half-Life Calculator, Mode 01 (four-variable solver).** New standalone page `calculators/half-life-calculator/`, built by `tools/halflife_page.js` from `tools/halflife_engine.js`, `tools/halflife_client.js` and the mode strip `tools/halflife_modes.js` (engine and script are inlined in the page). From the "Half-Life Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note (Mode 01 of five) and the supplied TypeScript engine (`exponentialDecay.ts`, `units.ts`, `steps.ts`), ported to the site's dependency-free JavaScript. Enter any three of initial quantity N0, remaining quantity Nt, elapsed time t and half-life T; the fourth is found. Two ways to choose the unknown: leave one field empty (automatic, the default) or pick it in "Find".
- Results: initial, remaining and decayed quantity, remaining and decayed percentage, elapsed time, half-life, half-lives elapsed, decay constant lambda and mean lifetime tau, in one table, plus the elapsed time and half-life in seconds and Julian years. Working is written in Unicode text with the user's own numbers (decay law, the solved form, half-lives elapsed, percentages, lambda and tau, and a check that puts the four values back through the model by a different route). Inputs accept 11,460, 11 460, 1.5e-3 and a Unicode minus. One time unit (nanoseconds to billions of years) for t and T, so the units cannot be mixed; the quantity unit is a label only. Results to 4, 6, 8, 10, 12 significant figures or full precision. Eight example chips, copy of the result or of the whole working, TXT, CSV (full-precision numbers, no grouping), JSON, print, and a 10-item history kept in the browser only.
- Differences from the supplied TypeScript, all deliberate: (1) powers of two and `Math.log2` replace `exp` and `ln` for the result, so 100 g after three half-lives is exactly 12.5 g and 100 to 25 is exactly 2 half-lives (the original gave 12.500000000000004 and 2.000000000000001); (2) when Nt is typed it is reported exactly as typed instead of being recomputed from the other values; (3) when the initial quantity overflows, the remaining quantity is no longer recomputed as Infinity or NaN; (4) the remaining fraction that underflows below the smallest double is flagged with a warning and shown as about 0%, not as an exact 0%; (5) the field being solved is ignored, so junk typed into it cannot block the answer; (6) LaTeX output (`steps.ts`) is not used, because the site loads no external renderer; the working is plain text.
- Rules, documented on the page (CEO decisions 2 to 8): N0 and T must be greater than zero and t cannot be negative; for t and T the remaining quantity must be greater than zero and no greater than the initial quantity; a remaining quantity of exactly zero is refused with an explanation (it is reached only at infinite time); Nt equal to N0 over a non-zero time gives an infinite half-life with a warning; radioactive, biological, effective, RC and RL decay are listed separately in a table; measurement uncertainty is stated not to be carried through; the page says it is not a radiation-safety, dosimetry or exposure assessment.
- Tests: `tests/halflife_test.js` with 1,425 independent vectors from `tests/gen_halflife_vectors.py` (Python decimal at 60 digits from the exact decimal value of the double inputs; brief cases, tiny-decay and 1,000-half-life cases, an extreme 1e300 to 1e-300 ratio, 350 random cases for each of the four targets), each run once with the target chosen and once in automatic mode with the unknown left blank; the brief's independent cases and decay ladder; validation, parsing and formatting cases; 600 random round trips through all four solvers; export checks; static checks of the built page. 37,938 checks. Added to the CI workflow and README.
- Not in this release: Mode 02 (decay constant and mean lifetime converter as its own page), Mode 03 (decay percentage page), Mode 04 (decay table and interactive graph, CSV and image export, scenario comparison), Mode 05 (RC and RL engineering decay), effective half-life, isotope presets (the brief asks for presets "backed by verified data"; none are embedded until a source is recorded), the `/math/` URL structure in the brief, and separate time units for t and T (the brief requires one consistent unit). The mode strip marks Modes 02 to 05 as coming.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 221 calculator pages, 637 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool; 22 standalone tools in `STANDALONE_TOOLS`). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.69. Test files that hold page counts as literals were updated (sitemap 340 + conversion pages, 265 cached calculator folders, 22 standalone tools).

## v1.99.70
- **Half-Life Calculator, Mode 02 (decay constant and mean lifetime converter).** New standalone page `calculators/decay-constant-calculator/`, built by `tools/halflife_const_page.js` from `tools/halflife_const_engine.js` and `tools/halflife_const_client.js` (the Mode 01 engine `tools/halflife_engine.js` and the new engine and script are inlined in the page). From the "Half-Life Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note (Mode 02 of five) and the supplied TypeScript `decayConstants.ts`, ported to the site's dependency-free JavaScript. Enter a half-life T, a decay constant lambda or a mean lifetime tau and the other two are found, all three shown together: lambda = ln 2 / T, tau = 1 / lambda = T / ln 2, T = tau x ln 2 = ln 2 / lambda (the six conversions are exported by name from the engine).
- Inputs and units: "Start from" is Automatic (type one value; if several are typed, the half-life is used first, then the decay constant, and the others are compared with it) or one chosen value (the other two fields become read-only results and anything typed in them is ignored). One time unit (nanoseconds to billions of years) for T and tau, with lambda per that unit, so the units cannot be mixed. "Also show in" converts all three together to another unit (T and tau times the ratio of the units, lambda divided by it), which is the brief's "automatic time-unit consistency". Inputs accept 11,460, 11 460, 1.5e-3 and a Unicode minus; results to 4, 6, 8, 10, 12 significant figures or full precision.
- Results: the three values with copy buttons, a table (with a converted column when a second unit is chosen), what the numbers mean (50% left after one half-life, 1/e = 36.7879% after one mean lifetime, tau / T = 1 / ln 2 = 1.4427, the fraction left and decayed in one time unit), the half-life and mean lifetime in seconds and Julian years and lambda per second, and written working in the user's own numbers with a check (lambda x tau = 1 and lambda x T = ln 2). Eight example chips, copy of the result or of the whole working, TXT, CSV (full-precision numbers), JSON, print, and a 10-item history kept in the browser only. Page sections: worked examples, the six formulas with the derivation, what the three numbers mean, the fraction left after 0 to 5 mean lifetimes, rules and limits, 12 questions.
- Differences from the supplied TypeScript, all deliberate: (1) tau is taken from T / ln 2 and lambda from ln 2 / T directly, not by chaining 1 / lambda, so no value carries two roundings; (2) the consistency check is applied to every pair (the original compared only half-life with decay constant and half-life with mean lifetime), the message shows both numbers to 10 digits so a rounded input such as 0.0693147 is explained, and the value used is named; (3) a result that overflows (for example lambda from a half-life of 1e-323) is refused with a message to change the time unit, and one below the smallest normal double is flagged, where the original returned Infinity or a denormal silently; (4) the field being found is ignored in explicit mode, so junk typed into it cannot block the answer; (5) a change of time unit, the fraction left per time unit and the equivalents in seconds are added; (6) a zero half-life, decay constant or mean lifetime is refused with the field named.
- Mode strip: Mode 02 is now a link on both pages (`tools/halflife_modes.js`); Modes 03 to 05 are still shown as coming. The Mode 01 page was rebuilt to carry the new link.
- Tests: `tests/halflife_const_test.js` with 1,358 independent vectors from `tests/gen_halflife_const_vectors.py` (Python decimal at 60 digits; the decay per time unit at 450 digits so a decay of 1e-300 keeps its digits; the brief's cases, extreme values from 1e-300 to 1e300, all 132 ordered unit pairs, 1,200 random cases with and without a unit change), each run once with the start chosen and junk in the other fields and once in automatic mode; the six named conversions; validation, priority and consistency cases, overflow and subnormal limits; 600 random properties (lambda x tau = 1, lambda x T = ln 2, tau / T = 1 / ln 2, round trips through all three starts, monotonic, unit round trip); agreement with Mode 01; export checks; static checks of the built pages and the mode strip. Added to the CI workflow and README.
- Not in this release: Mode 03 (decay percentage page), Mode 04 (decay table and interactive graph, CSV and image export, scenario comparison), Mode 05 (RC and RL engineering decay), effective half-life, isotope presets (none are embedded until a source is recorded), the separate `/math/decay-constant-calculator/` and `/math/mean-lifetime-calculator/` addresses in the brief (one page serves both), and uncertainty propagation.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 221 calculator pages, 638 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool; 23 standalone tools in `STANDALONE_TOOLS`). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.70. Test files that hold page counts as literals were updated (sitemap 341 + conversion pages, 266 cached calculator folders, 23 standalone tools).

## v1.99.71
- **Half-Life Calculator, Mode 03 (remaining and decayed percentage).** New standalone page `calculators/decay-percentage-calculator/`, built by `tools/halflife_percent_page.js` from `tools/halflife_percent_engine.js` and `tools/halflife_percent_client.js` (the Mode 01 engine `tools/halflife_engine.js` and the new engine and script are inlined in the page). From the "Half-Life Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note (Mode 03 of five). Enter an elapsed time t and a half-life T (and, optionally, an initial quantity N0) and the page gives the number of half-lives n = t / T, the fraction remaining f = 2^(-n), the fraction decayed 1 - f, and with N0 the remaining quantity N0 f and the decayed quantity N0 (1 - f), plus lambda and tau.
- Inputs and units: the elapsed time and the half-life each have their own time unit (nanoseconds to billions of years); t is converted to the unit of T by the exact ratio of the units (Julian year = 365.25 days), and the ratio is exactly 1 for equal units so whole numbers of half-lives stay exact (100 g after three half-lives is exactly 12.5 g). The initial quantity is optional (percentages only without it) and has a free-text unit label; nothing is converted. Inputs accept 11,460, 11 460, 1.5e-3 and a Unicode minus; results to 4, 6, 8, 10, 12 significant figures or full precision.
- Results: three summary cards (remaining, decayed, half-lives elapsed), a remaining/decayed bar, a table of every value with copy buttons, the elapsed time and half-life in seconds and Julian years, the halving ladder (0 to max(5, ceil n) half-lives, at most 10, the current row marked), the time to fall to 90, 75, 50, 25, 10, 5, 1 and 0.1 percent for the half-life entered, and written working in the user's own numbers with a check (remaining + decayed = 1, and f against exp(-n ln 2)). Nine example chips, copy of the result or of the whole working, TXT, CSV (full-precision numbers), JSON, print, and a 10-item history kept in the browser only. Page sections: worked examples, the formulas, the halving ladder for 100 g, rules and limits, 12 questions.
- Rules: t cannot be negative (zero is allowed and leaves 100%); T must be greater than zero; N0, when entered, must be greater than zero (zero is refused with a note that a percentage of nothing is undefined and that the field can be left empty); a number of half-lives or a decayed quantity that cannot be represented is refused with the field named and a note to change the unit; a fraction below the smallest double shows as 0% with a note that it is not exactly zero, one below the smallest normal double is flagged, and an elapsed time so small that n rounds to zero is flagged.
- Numerics: 2^(-n) is Math.pow(2, -n); the decayed fraction is -expm1(-ln 2 n) for n < 0.5 (so one billionth of a half-life gives 6.93147e-10, not 0) and 1 - f otherwise; the fractions, quantities, lambda and tau agree exactly with Mode 01 for the same numbers (tested).
- Mode strip: Mode 03 is now a link on all three pages (`tools/halflife_modes.js`); Modes 04 and 05 are still shown as coming. The Mode 01 and Mode 02 pages were rebuilt to carry the new link, and `tests/halflife_const_test.js` was updated to expect it.
- Tests: `tests/halflife_percent_test.js` with 1,051 independent vectors from `tests/gen_halflife_percent_vectors.py` (Python decimal from the exact decimal value of the double inputs; the decayed fraction at 450 digits so a decay of 1e-300 keeps its digits; the brief's cases, whole numbers of half-lives 0 to 60, tiny decay from 1e-250, long times through the underflow edge, all 144 ordered unit pairs, large and small quantities, 800 random cases with and without a quantity and with mixed units), the brief's cases, validation, range and underflow limits, milestones and the ladder, 600 random properties (remaining + decayed = 1, agreement with exp, monotonic, f(2t) = f(t)^2, time-to-fraction round trip, unit invariance), exact agreement with Mode 01, export and working checks, and static checks of the built pages and the mode strip. Added to the CI workflow and README.
- Not in this release: Mode 04 (decay table and interactive graph, CSV and image export, scenario comparison), Mode 05 (RC and RL engineering decay), solving for a target percentage as an input (Mode 01 solves for the elapsed time; this page shows the time to common percentages), effective half-life, isotope presets (none are embedded until a source is recorded), and uncertainty propagation.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 221 calculator pages, 639 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool; 24 standalone tools in `STANDALONE_TOOLS`). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.71. Test files that hold page counts as literals were updated (sitemap 342 + conversion pages, 267 cached calculator folders, 24 standalone tools).

## v1.99.72
- **Half-Life Calculator, Mode 04 (decay table and graph).** New standalone page `calculators/decay-table-calculator/`, built by `tools/halflife_table_page.js` from `tools/halflife_table_engine.js` and `tools/halflife_table_client.js` (the Mode 01 engine `tools/halflife_engine.js` and the new engine and script are inlined in the page). From the "Half-Life Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note (Mode 04 of five). Enter a half-life T (scenario A) and, optionally, an initial quantity N0, and the page gives a table of the fraction remaining 2^(-n), the fraction decayed and, with N0, the quantity left, at evenly spaced rows from time zero, with an interactive decay curve.
- Table: rows are spaced either in half-lives of A (row i is at n = i x step, so whole numbers stay exact: 100 g after three half-lives is exactly 12.5 g; steps below one are allowed, 0.25 gives four rows per halving) or in time (row i is at t = i x step in a chosen table unit). 1 to 200 steps, so 2 to 201 rows, the first always at time zero.
- Scenario comparison: up to two more half-lives B and C, each with its own time unit, read at the same times as A; the table shows every scenario side by side and the graph draws one curve each (solid, dashed, dotted, with circle, square and triangle row markers, so the scenarios do not depend on colour). Unit conversion uses the exact ratio of the units (Julian year = 365.25 days), exactly 1 for equal units.
- Graph: a self-contained SVG made by the engine (fixed colours on a white background, title and description for screen readers), linear (0 to 100%) or logarithmic (computed as 2 - n log10 2, never from the fraction, so it does not underflow; clamped to 10^-300 at the lower edge). The curves are 240 points of the model itself, not joined table rows. Interactive readout: a slider, and hover or touch on the graph, move a marker along the curve and give the half-lives, percentage left, percentage decayed and quantity for every scenario at that exact time (`HalfLifeTable.at`, the same numbers as a table row).
- Exports: table as CSV (full-precision numbers, one block of columns per scenario), TXT and JSON; graph as SVG and as PNG (1520 x 840, drawn from the SVG in the browser, with a message if the browser cannot make a PNG); copy with working, print, a 10-item history kept in the browser only, nine example chips, written working in the user's own numbers with a check (every row: remaining + decayed = 1, and f against exp(-n ln 2)). Page sections: worked examples, the formulas, the halving ladder, how to read the graph, rules and limits, 12 questions.
- Rules: half-life greater than zero (A, B and C); step greater than zero; steps a whole number from 1 to 200; N0, when entered, greater than zero; a time or a number of half-lives that cannot be represented is refused with the field named; a fraction below the smallest double shows as 0% with a note that it is not exactly zero.
- Mode strip: Mode 04 is now a link on all four pages (`tools/halflife_modes.js`); Mode 05 is still shown as coming. The Mode 01, 02 and 03 pages were rebuilt to carry the new link, and the three assertions that expected Mode 04 to be marked "coming" in `tests/halflife_const_test.js` and `tests/halflife_percent_test.js` were changed to expect the link.
- Tests: `tests/halflife_table_test.js` with independent vectors from `tests/gen_halflife_table_vectors.py` (Python decimal at 450 digits from the exact decimal value of the double inputs: 73 tables, 1,639 rows, 2,579 scenario values, covering the brief's cases, quarter steps, 40 and 200 half-lives, the underflow edge, a tiny step, mixed units and 60 random tables with random units, spacing, scenarios and quantity), the brief's cases and exactness, validation and range limits, the probe against the table and against Mode 03, the graph (closed, self-contained SVG, one curve per scenario, log decay a straight line, no NaN), 600 random properties, export and working checks, and static checks of the built pages and the mode strip. Added to the CI workflow and README.
- Not in this release: Mode 05 (RC and RL engineering decay), effective half-life, isotope presets (none are embedded until a source is recorded), uncertainty propagation, and dragging to zoom the graph.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 221 calculator pages, 640 sitemap URLs (the new page is a standalone tool; 25 standalone tools in `STANDALONE_TOOLS`). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.72. Test files that hold page counts as literals were updated (sitemap 343 + conversion pages, 268 cached calculator folders, 25 standalone tools).

## v1.99.73
- **Merge release.** Two parallel branches are merged into the v1.99.72 line (half-life and decay tools): the Percent Error Calculator Mode 04 branch (built as v1.99.68, `Elecmetric-v1_99_68-batch-measurement`, based on v1.99.67) and the Binary Calculator Mode 02 branch (built as v1.99.69, `Elecmetric-v1_99_69-number-system-conversion`, based on v1.99.68). Their version numbers collided with the main line, so both ship as 1.99.73. Source changes were merged file by file against each branch's own base; generated pages (category hubs, All Tools catalogue) were rebuilt with `node tools/build_pages.js`, `docs/API.md` and the review pack regenerated, and every test in `.github/workflows/pages.yml` run.

### Percent Error Calculator, Mode 04 (from the batch-measurement branch)
- **Percent Error Calculator, Mode 04 (Batch Measurement Analysis).** New standalone page `calculators/batch-measurement-analysis/`, built by `tools/pe_batch_page.js` from `tools/pe_batch_engine.js`, `tools/pe_batch_client.js` and the shared `tools/pe_modes.js`, following the CEO product decision (Mode 04 of five). Inputs: one reference value, a list of observed readings (one per line, or separated by spaces, tabs or semicolons; up to 1000), a unit label (label only), decimal places for derived values, an optional symmetrical tolerance (percentage of the reference or an absolute value), and an optional .csv or .txt import with a column picker.
- Results: number of readings, sum, mean (average), median, minimum, maximum and range of the readings; mean signed error (bias); mean absolute error; maximum absolute error and the reading it belongs to; root mean square error; population and sample standard deviation; the same quantities as percentages of the reference; the signed error, absolute error and signed percentage deviation of every reading; with a tolerance, the limits, how many readings are within, on a limit and outside, which ones are outside, and how much of the tolerance the largest error uses; an inline SVG deviation chart (bars, reference, mean error, tolerance limits; outside bars are hatched and outlined so colour is not the only cue); formula, the user's numbers in it, numbered steps, copy, summary and readings .csv, .txt, print, history and the report card as in Modes 01 to 03. The brief's case: 229, 231, 230, 228, 232 V against 230 V gives mean signed error 0 V, mean absolute error 1.2 V, maximum absolute error 2 V, population standard deviation 1.414214 V and sample standard deviation 1.581139 V.
- Rules, documented on the page: the error is reading minus reference and percentages divide by the size of the reference, as in Modes 02 and 03. The standard deviation is the spread about the readings' own mean and does not depend on the reference. A sample standard deviation needs 2 or more readings and is shown as not defined for one. A reference of 0 is accepted (errors, means and spread are defined) and the percentages are not defined; a percentage tolerance needs a reference that is not 0. A comma between digits is refused (1,5 could be a decimal comma and 1,000 a thousand); a comma ending a reading is accepted. The tolerance is the one entered, no standard or datasheet is read, measurement uncertainty is not applied and a pass is never described as compliant, calibrated or approved. A section separates error, standard deviation, tolerance, uncertainty and accuracy.
- `tools/pe_batch_engine.js` uses the public API of `tools/percent_engine.js` only. Readings are scaled to BigInt integers, so sums and sums of squares are exact; variances are exact fractions; a standard deviation is rounded half up from an exact integer square root (no floating-point square root), and a root that is a finite decimal is shown exactly. Modes 02 and 03 are not called: the test compares every per-reading percentage with Mode 02 and the limits and status of the readings with Mode 03.
- Tests: `tests/pe_batch_test.js` with 428 independent cases from `tests/gen_pe_batch_vectors.py` (Python fractions and decimal with 3000-digit square roots: named cases, 150 cases with a reading on each limit and one smallest step beyond, 260 random batches with random places, tolerances, negative and zero references, and a 1000-reading batch), the brief's case, exact-root and single-reading cases, separator and refusal cases, and 400 random properties (order of the readings changes nothing, the spread does not depend on the reference, mean observed from exact integer sums, |mean error| <= mean absolute error <= maximum error, RMSE squared = bias squared + variance, sample = population x sqrt(n/(n-1)), a wider tolerance keeps passes). Static checks of the built page. `tests/percent_report_test.js` now covers nine pages. Added to the CI workflow and README.
- Mode strip on Modes 01 to 03 now links Mode 04; only Mode 05 stays plain text marked "coming". The three earlier pages differ from v1.99.67 only by that link.
- Not in this release: Mode 05 (engineering measurement analysis), measurement uncertainty and guard bands, an asymmetrical tolerance in batch mode (use Mode 03 for one reading), the deviation chart inside the report card (the report lists the same numbers in its tables), the `/math/` and `/engineering/measurement-analysis/` URL structure in the brief. Known from v1.99.65 and not changed: the shared report card's report-number header is clipped at 390 px width.

### Binary Calculator, Mode 02 (from the number-system-conversion branch)
- **Binary Calculator, Mode 02 (Number System Conversion).** New standalone page `calculators/number-system-converter/`, built by `tools/numconv_page.js` from `tools/numconv_engine.js` and `tools/numconv_client.js` (engine and script inlined in the page). From the "Binary Calculator: CEO Product Decision" note (Mode 02 of five). Exact (BigInt) conversion between binary, octal, decimal and hexadecimal: up to 4,096 digits, up to 512 after the radix point; optional sign, 0b/0o/0x prefix (only the one for the chosen system, so 0b stays a hex digit pair in hexadecimal), grouping spaces and underscores, validation that names the bad digit.
- Fractions: a value is held as the ratio M / S^k, so nothing is rounded on input. A fraction with a finite expansion in the target system is shown in full. One that has none (0.1 in binary) is cut off at 8, 16, 32, 64 or 128 digits (truncated, never rounded), marked with an ellipsis, and given as an exact repeating block (0.0(0011)) found by remainder cycle detection (up to 4,096 steps; beyond that the page says no repeat was found). Each result is checked inside the engine: exact results are read back, cut-off results are bounded below the true value by one unit of the last digit, repeating blocks are expanded back to the original ratio. A failed check throws, so a wrong result is never shown.
- Representation: a default "value with a minus sign" mode (any size, fractions allowed), and fixed-width 4, 8, 16, 32 or 64 bits as unsigned, two's complement, one's complement or sign-magnitude. Pattern input (binary, octal, hex) takes no sign; decimal input is encoded and refused, not wrapped, when out of range (the range is stated). The same bits are shown in all four representations with the selected one marked (11111111 is 255, -127, -0 and -1), with bit width, MSB, LSB, set and clear bits. Negative zero is reported for one's complement and sign-magnitude.
- Working written for every conversion: place values (digit, weight, value), repeated division with remainders read upward, repeated multiplication for fractions, bit grouping in 3s and 4s between binary, octal and hexadecimal, and the invert-and-add-1 steps for two's complement. Options: target system or all, fraction digit limit, leading-zero padding (shortest, match typed width, 4 to 64 bits; only widens), binary digit grouping for display, keypad, swap, copy (plain digits), TXT/CSV/JSON download, 10-item history kept in the browser.
- Tests: `tests/numconv_test.js` with 1,455 independent vectors from `tests/gen_numconv_vectors.py` (Python ints and Fractions; target digits are one big-integer division floor(frac x T^n), the pre-period by removing shared prime factors and the period by the multiplicative order, not the engine's repeated multiplication; fixed-width cases by masks), the brief's cases (170, 252, AA, 11111111, 111111, 10201 refused, 255 unsigned versus -1 two's complement, the register example), limits, padding, round trips and exports. The tests were checked by deliberately breaking the engine (digit count, two's complement offset) and both faults were caught. Page checked in Chromium at desktop and phone width: conversions, errors, keypad, swap, history after reload, three downloads, no console errors, no horizontal overflow with a 300-digit number.
- Mode 01 page now links to the converter and its FAQ points to it.
- Not in this release: Mode 03 (bitwise display with highlighted bits), Mode 04 (full signed analysis: overflow policies, saturation), Mode 05, fixed-width fractions, bases other than 2, 8, 10 and 16, and the `/math/` URL structure.

- Counts: 248 calculators, 221 calculator pages, 642 sitemap URLs (the two new pages are standalone tools; 26 standalone tools in `STANDALONE_TOOLS`). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.73. Test files that hold page counts as literals were updated (sitemap 345 + conversion pages, 270 cached calculator folders, 27 standalone tools).

## v1.99.75
- **Site-wide internal linking.** Follows the v1.99.68 internal-linking audit. Discovery was already sound (every sitemap URL reachable from the home page in two in-content clicks); the fixes are in catalogue completeness, category relevance and related-link control.
- **One register for standalone pages.** New `tools/site_pages.json` (loader and checks in `tools/site_pages.js`) records the 28 standalone tools and the Basic and Scientific Calculators once: primary and secondary category hub, master-register discipline, subcategory, tool type and task, keywords, approved related tools, `homepageFeatured`, `includeInAllTools` and `inboundFrom`. `STANDALONE_TOOLS`, the category hubs, the home page More tools list, All Tools and the standalone pages' Related sections now read it. An engine calculator is not listed there; its page still comes from the engine registry and `tools/master_register.json`.
- **All Tools is complete.** 30 published calculator pages (all 28 standalone tools, Basic and Scientific Calculator) were missing from the catalogue; they are now cards (623 in all). `tools/build_all_tools.js` also fails the build if any published `calculators/<slug>/` page is in neither the master register nor the site page register (unless `includeInAllTools` is false), so tests can no longer pass over an incomplete catalogue.
- **Category relevance.** The Engineering Mathematics and Electronics and Instrumentation hubs no longer both list every standalone tool. Fractions, percentages and the Random Number Generator are Mathematics only; digital-code tools are Electronics first with Mathematics as a secondary home; measurement-error tools sit in Mathematics with Electronics and Instrumentation secondary. Hubs show them in a "Standalone calculators and converters" section.
- **Related-link limit.** `tools/calc_related.js` applied its 6 to 9 limit before the reciprocal pass, so 69 pages carried up to 34 related links. The limit is now applied last. Reverse links are ranked by division, category and keyword closeness, are added only while a page has room, and a reachability floor (`MIN_INBOUND` = 2) keeps every calculator listed by at least two others. `EXCEPTIONS` (empty) is the only way to raise one page's limit. Transformer Sizing keeps its Transformer Regulation link as an explicit hand-chosen relationship. Test 23 no longer requires every link to be two-way.
- **Weak pages.** Standalone Related sections now draw on the registry (up to 9 links), engine pages can offer standalone tools through `inboundFrom` inside their own limit, and the home page features Basic Calculator, Scientific Calculator, Binary Arithmetic and Random Number Generator. No calculator page now has fewer than 5 distinct in-content inbound sources (previously 3 for Basic Calculator and 4 for Random Number Generator).
- **Tests.** `tests/internal_link_graph_test.js` checks the production build's contextual graph (reachability, orphans, All Tools and category coverage, inbound floor, related limits and duplicates, reverse converter links, broken links, anchor text); `tests/site_pages_test.js` checks the register; `tools/link_graph.js` prints a report for any built folder.
- Counts: 248 calculators, 221 calculator pages, 643 sitemap URLs (unchanged). Engine and service-worker cache 1.99.75.
